# 23. Sūrat al-Muʾminūn

**118 verses.** It is widely reported that it was revealed during the Meccan period. It takes its name from the word **ٱلْمُؤْمِنُون** in its first verse.

The sūra opens with **a verdict**: *qad aflaḥa'l-muʾminūn* — "the believers have attained deliverance". And immediately after the opening comes **a list of attributes** that fixes to whom that verdict is given (2-9). The sūra closes in the hundred and seventeenth verse with the same root: *innahū lā yufliḥu'l-kāfirūn* — "the disbelievers do not attain deliverance". **The sūra, that is, opens with the positive of a single root (ف-ل-ح) and ends with its negative.**

Everything in between is set down between these two sentences: the forming of the human being, rain and plants, a sequence of messengers beginning with Noah (Nūḥ), Moses (Mūsā) and Aaron (Hārūn), the son of Mary (Maryam) and his mother, the community that broke apart, people misled by prosperity, hearing and sight and heart, the demand made at the moment of death, the blast on the trumpet and the scales.

**The determining structural element of the sūra is its two lists of attributes.** The first is at 1-11, the second at 57-61. Both are built on the same grammatical pattern (*wa'lladhīna hum…*), and both close with a concluding sentence. **The first list enumerates acts that are seen from outside, the second states that are within.** Below, the two are treated separately and then compared.

And **the first list should be read together with the *ʿibādu'l-raḥmān* list at the end of Sūrat al-Furqān** (25/63-77). That list was treated in detail in `025-furkan.md`. **The two lists will be compared in a table in this file:** the shared items, those found in only one of them, and the position each list holds within its sūra.

---

## The structure of the sūra

The sūra consists of eight blocks. The block boundaries are for the most part drawn by the text's own repetitions.

| Block | Verses | Subject | What closes the block |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | 1-11 | **The first list of attributes** — six items opening with the prayer and closing with the prayer, and the inheritance | *hum fīhā khālidūn* (11) |
| **II** | 12-22 | The forming of the human being, the seven paths, water sent down by measure, plants, animals | *wa ʿalayhā wa ʿalā'l-fulki tuḥmalūn* (22) |
| **III** | 23-44 | **Noah and the sequence of peoples that follows him** | *fa-buʿdan li-qawmin lā yuʾminūn* (44) |
| **IV** | 45-50 | Moses and Aaron; the son of Mary and his mother | *rabwatin dhāti qarārin wa maʿīn* (50) |
| **V** | 51-56 | The address to the messengers, the one community, the breaking apart, the delusion of prosperity | *bal lā yashʿurūn* (56) |
| **VI** | 57-77 | **The second list of attributes**, the limit of the burden, the book that speaks, the end of those in prosperity | *idhā hum fīhi mublisūn* (77) |
| **VII** | 78-92 | Hearing, sight and heart; three questions and three answers; the proof of tawḥīd | *fa-taʿālā ʿammā yushrikūn* (92) |
| **VIII** | 93-118 | Prayer, the moment of death and the barzakh, the trumpet, the scales, the reckoning, the closing | *wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* (118) |

### Repetitions within the sūra — countable data

**What follows are facts that can be verified from the text.** The claim that they establish a design is interpretation, and I offer it as an observation.

| Repetition | First occurrence | Later occurrence(s) | The difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ف-ل-ح** | **1** — *qad aflaḥa'l-muʾminūn* | **102** — *humu'l-mufliḥūn* · **117** — *lā yufliḥu'l-kāfirūn* | The verdict, the weighing, its negative |
| **ن-ش-أ** | **14** — *anshaʾnāhu khalqan ākhar* | **19, 31, 42, 78** | **Five occurrences** — the backbone root of the sūra |
| *mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum* | **24** — Noah's people | **33** — the next people · **47** — *basharayni mithlinā* | **Three times, three separate peoples** |
| *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* | **23** — Noah | **32** — the next messenger | Two calls ending with the same sentence |
| *rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn* | **26** — Noah | **39** — the next messenger | **Word for word the same prayer** |
| *bihī jinna* | **25** — of Noah | **70** — of the Prophet addressed | The same charge, two ages |
| *ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn* | **24** — *mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn* | **68** — *am jāʾahum mā lam yaʾti ābāʾahumu'l-awwalīn* | A ground / a question |
| *ḥattā ḥīn* | **25** — *fa-tarabbaṣū bihī* (the other side says it) | **54** — *fa-dharhum fī ghamratihim* (the text says it) | **The same expression, in two mouths** |
| **غ-م-ر** | **54** — *fī ghamratihim* | **63** — *qulūbuhum fī ghamratin* | The place they are left in / the place of the hearts |
| **ت-ر-ف** | **33** — *atrafnāhum fi'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā* | **64** — *akhadhnā mutrafīhim bi'l-ʿadhāb* | Being given / being seized |
| *ḥattā idhā* | **64** — the seizing of those in prosperity | **77** — the opening of the gate · **99** — the coming of death | **Three turning points** |
| **س-ر-ع** | **56** — *nusāriʿu lahum fi'l-khayrāt* (what they suppose) | **61** — *yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt* (what is) | **The same verb, one a delusion, the other an attribute** |
| **ح-س-ب** | **55** — *a-yaḥsabūna* | **115** — *a-fa-ḥasibtum* · **117** — *ḥisābuhū ʿinda rabbih* | Two questions about supposing, one reckoning |
| *āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* | **66** — in this world | **105** — in the hereafter | **The same sentence, two places** |
| **ه-ج-ر** | **67** — *sāmiran tahjurūn* | (al-Furqān 25/30 — *qurʾāna mahjūrā*) | Single within the sūra, a pair across sūras |
| **ع-ل-و** | **46** — *qawman ʿālīn* | **91** — *la-ʿalā baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ* · **116** — *fa-taʿāla'llāh* | Rising, three times |
| *rabbu'l-ʿarsh* | **86** — *al-ʿaẓīm* | **116** — *al-karīm* | Two attributes, in two separate places |
| *rabbanā … wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | **109** — the prayer that is mocked | **118** — **the prayer that is commanded** | **The last sentence of the sūra** |
| *khayru…* | **14** — *aḥsanu'l-khāliqīn* | **29** — *khayru'l-munzilīn* · **72** — *khayru'l-rāziqīn* · **109, 118** — *khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | **Five superlative constructions** |

**The last row must be recorded separately:** in the hundred and ninth verse this prayer is in the mouth of **a group that is mocked for it**; **in the hundred and eighteenth verse the same sentence is the content of the command given to the one addressed.** The sūra, that is, makes the speech that was mocked its own last sentence. This is a weave that can be verified from the text.

---

## 23/1 — قَدْ أَفْلَحَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ

*qad aflaḥa'l-muʾminūn*

"**The believers have attained deliverance.**"

### أَفْلَحَ — root: ف-ل-ح

**The root was worked out in `002-bakara.md` and extended in the discussions of `091-sems.md` 91/9 (*qad aflaḥa man zakkāhā*) and `087-ala.md` 87/14 (*qad aflaḥa man tazakkā*).** **I do not repeat it here.** The picture established in al-Baqara is summed up as follows, and it has been used unchanged in the `058-mucadele.md`, `059-hasr.md`, `048-fetih.md` and `064-tegabun.md` sections:

> The root is **to split, to crack open**. *Fallāḥ* — the farmer; he takes the name because he splits the soil. *Falāḥ* — to break through an obstacle and come out, to crack the husk, to come into a state of bearing fruit.

**In `113-felak.md`, where the derivatives of the root were listed, this very verse was given as the example.** I rely on that treatment.

**What belongs here is this, and it is worth recording:** the same pattern (*qad aflaḥa*) opens a sentence in three places in the Qurʾān, and **in all three the agent is described differently:**

| Place | Sentence | How the agent is described |
|---|---|---|
| al-Shams 91/9 | *qad aflaḥa **man zakkāhā*** | **By a verb** — the one who purifies his soul |
| al-Aʿlā 87/14 | *qad aflaḥa **man tazakkā*** | **By a verb** — the one who is purified / who takes heed |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/1** | *qad aflaḥa **al-muʾminūn*** | **By a name** — and after it **a description nine verses long** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the length of the third row:** in the other two places the description of the agent is inside the sentence; **here a name is set down and the description is spread across separate verses.** The sūra, that is, sets about filling the word "believer" rather than leaving it empty.

### قَدْ + a perfect verb

**The word order must be recorded:** when *qad* comes at the head of a perfect verb it reports **confirmation** (*taḥqīq*). And the verb is **in the past** — whereas the attributes described (2-9) are given in **imperfect and active participle** patterns, that is, as ongoing states.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** **the verdict is framed in the past tense, the condition in the ongoing tense.** The lexicographers explain this kind of usage as "telling a thing as though it had happened, because its happening is certain"; I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.

### ٱلْمُؤْمِنُون

The root **أ-م-ن** — **safety, security.** *Muʾmin* is the active participle of Form IV: **the one who brings to safety / the one who trusts.** The root has occurred in many places across this series and was treated in `106-kureys.md` (*wa āmanahum min khawf*) and at `059-hasr.md` 59/23 (*al-Muʾmin*).

**And an entry must be placed here:** throughout the sūra the verdict will be attached **not to a group but to an attribute.** Accordingly the negative counterpart in the hundred and seventeenth verse is likewise built not with a name but **with the act of not believing.** **This is what the principle recorded in `STYLE.md` — that no blanket ruling is issued about a group — comes to in this sūra:** the verse attaches deliverance not to a faction but to **a whole set of states enumerated across nine verses.**

---

## 23/2 — ٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ فِى صَلَاتِهِمْ خَٰشِعُونَ

*alladhīna hum fī ṣalātihim khāshiʿūn*

"**They are those who are humble in their prayer.**"

### The grammatical pattern of the list — the word order shared by the six items

**The list consists of six items (2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9), and all six are built on the same word order.** This is a fact that can be counted from the text:

| Verse | Connective | Pronoun | **The fronted element** | Predicate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | *alladhīna* | *hum* | ***fī ṣalātihim*** | *khāshiʿūn* |
| 3 | *wa'lladhīna* | *hum* | ***ʿani'l-laghwi*** | *muʿriḍūn* |
| 4 | *wa'lladhīna* | *hum* | ***li'l-zakāti*** | *fāʿilūn* |
| 5 | *wa'lladhīna* | *hum* | ***li-furūjihim*** | *ḥāfiẓūn* |
| 8 | *wa'lladhīna* | *hum* | ***li-amānātihim wa ʿahdihim*** | *rāʿūn* |
| 9 | *wa'lladhīna* | *hum* | ***ʿalā ṣalawātihim*** | *yuḥāfiẓūn* |

**In all six of the six items the complement — the prepositional phrase — has been brought before its predicate.** In Arabic fronting (*taqdīm*) reports **specification and emphasis**; the lexicographers record this consistently.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is this sixfold regularity:** the sentences do not say "they are humble", "they turn away"; they say **"in their prayer — they are humble", "from idle speech — they turn away"**. **In each item, that is, the field is fixed first and the disposition within that field second.** The list thereby becomes readable as a list not of dispositions but of **fields**.

**And there is a second regularity:** **five of the six items are nouns (active participles), and only the last is a verb** — *yuḥāfiẓūn*. This difference will be treated separately at the ninth verse.

### خَاشِعُون — root: خ-ش-ع

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: to sink low, to subside, the lowering of the voice, the dropping of the eye.** From the same root:

| Word | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **خَشَعَ** | He sank low, he submitted, he lowered his voice |
| **خَاشِعَة** | **Dried, subsided ground** (Fuṣṣilat 41/39 — *tarā'l-arḍa khāshiʿatan*) |
| **خُشُوع** | The lowering of voice and gaze; the outward showing of an inward bowing |

**The use of the root for ground was treated at `041-fussilet.md` 41/39** (ground subsided before the rain, then swelling afterwards), and *khāshiʿa* occurred in `079-naziat.md` (79/9 — *abṣārun yawmaʾidhin khāshiʿa*).

**Two sides of the root must be recorded, and this is the lexicographers' common description:** *khushūʿ* is something that takes place **both inside and outside.** The lowering of the voice and the dropping of the gaze are the visible side; the bowing of the heart is the side that is not visible. **The word answers to both at once.**

### فِى صَلَاتِهِمْ — the particle, and the singular

**Two details must be recorded:**

**One. The particle is *fī* — "inside".** *Khushūʿ* is sought not **beside** the prayer but **inside** it. The adverbial phrase frames the prayer as a vessel.

**Two. *Ṣalāt* is singular.** In the ninth verse the same word will come in the **plural** (*ṣalawātihim*). This difference is too regular to be counted accidental, and it will be tabulated at the ninth verse.

**I give this as my own reading, and its ground is the contrast of singular and plural:** the second verse looks **inside one prayer** — the prayer being performed. The ninth verse will look **at the whole of the prayers.**

---

## 23/3 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ عَنِ ٱللَّغْوِ مُعْرِضُونَ

*wa'lladhīna hum ʿani'l-laghwi muʿriḍūn*

"**They are those who turn away from idle speech.**"

### لَغْو

**The root was worked out in detail at `056-vakia.md` 56/25 (*lā yasmaʿūna fīhā laghwan wa lā taʾthīmā*).** **I do not repeat it here.** The description given there is this:

> The description the lexicographers give: **speech or action that is not reckoned, that goes for nothing.** *Laghwa'l-shayʾu* — it became worthless, it did not count.

**And in `056-vakia.md` this very verse was cited to show the place of the word within the Qurʾān.** I rely on that treatment. The links of the word across this series are the following, and the fifth is here:

| Place | Use | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `056-vakia.md` 56/25 | *lā yasmaʿūna fīhā laghwan* | **Absent in the garden** — removed |
| `041-fussilet.md` 41/26 | *wa'lghaw fīhi* | **A tactic** — the other side makes noise |
| `028-kasas.md` 28/55 | *wa idhā samiʿū'l-laghwa aʿraḍū ʿanh* | **A reaction** — turning away on hearing it |
| `025-furkan.md` 25/72 | *idhā marrū bi'l-laghwi marrū kirāmā* | **An attribute** — passing it by with dignity |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/3** | *ʿani'l-laghwi muʿriḍūn* | **An attribute** — a standing state |

**The difference between al-Qaṣaṣ 28/55 and this verse is worth recording, since both use the same verb (أعرض):**

| | al-Qaṣaṣ 28/55 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/3** |
|---|---|---|
| Pattern | ***idhā** samiʿū … **aʿraḍū*** — perfect, tied to a condition | ***muʿriḍūn*** — active participle, unconditioned |
| What it reports | **A reaction given in the face of an event** | **A settled state** |
| Trigger | *samiʿū* — **when they hear** | **None** — no conditional clause is framed |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two patterns:** al-Qaṣaṣ describes an answer given to speech that is heard; **al-Muʾminūn reports an orientation.** The active participle records not the recurrence of an event but **the stance of the person.**

### أَعْرَضَ — root: ع-ر-ض

**The concrete sense of the root: to show the *ʿarḍ* of a thing, that is, its breadth, its side.** *Aʿraḍa ʿanhu* — he turned away from it; the picture in the word is **turning not the face but the side.**

**And this verb will occur once more in the sūra — but on the other side:** in the seventy-first verse, *fa-hum ʿan dhikrihim **muʿriḍūn*** — "turning away from their own reminder". **The same active participle, in the same pattern, over two different objects.**

| Verse | What is turned away from | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | ***al-laghw*** — idle speech | **An attribute** — included in deliverance |
| **71** | ***dhikruhum*** — the reminder that comes to them | **A fault** — the name of refusal |

**This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.** And I record this as my own reading: **the verb is neither good nor bad in itself; what settles the verdict is what is being turned away from.** Something like the same distinction was recorded for the verb *fariḥa* at `040-gafir.md` 40/75.

---

## 23/4 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ لِلزَّكَوٰةِ فَٰعِلُونَ

*wa'lladhīna hum li'l-zakāti fāʿilūn*

"**They are those who carry out the *zakāt*.**"

### ز-ك-و

**The root was treated in detail at `091-sems.md` 91/9 (*qad aflaḥa man zakkāhā*)** and taken up again in `080-abese.md` (*laʿallahū yazzakkā*). **I do not repeat it here.** The summary recorded there is this:

> Beneath the root **two senses stand together** — **purification and growth.** *Tazkiya* is growth brought about by reduction.

### فَاعِلُون — the choice of verb

**And a detail of word order must be recorded here:** the verse does not say *yuʾtūna'l-zakāta* (they give the *zakāt*) — **which is the common counterpart of the word in other verses.** The verb chosen here is **فعل**: "to do, to perform".

**Two readings of the meaning of the word are transmitted:**

| Reading | What *zakāt* is | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Giving a fixed portion of one's property** — *zakāt* in the institutional sense | The common use of the word in the Qurʾān |
| 2 | **The work of purification** — the act that cleanses the self and the property | The original sense of the root; and the sūra being Meccan |

**The ground of the second reading must also be recorded:** the institutional form of *zakāt*, fixed by amount and threshold, took shape, as it is transmitted, in the Medinan period; **and this sūra is counted Meccan.** This does not make the second reading necessary — the word *zakāt* occurs in Meccan verses too — but it shows the source of the dispute.

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **And I issue no legal ruling:** the threshold, the rate and the categories of *zakāt* have been discussed in detail among the schools of law, and that is not the subject of this commentary.

**On the choice of verb I give the following as my own reading:** the word *fāʿilūn* is **wider than giving.** *Fiʿl* reports a work, an action. **The item therefore stays wide enough to take in not only the moment of transfer but the work done towards purification.**

---

## 23/5-7 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ لِفُرُوجِهِمْ حَٰفِظُونَ · إِلَّا عَلَىٰٓ أَزْوَٰجِهِمْ أَوْ مَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَٰنُهُمْ فَإِنَّهُمْ غَيْرُ مَلُومِينَ · فَمَنِ ٱبْتَغَىٰ وَرَآءَ ذَٰلِكَ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْعَادُونَ

*wa'lladhīna hum li-furūjihim ḥāfiẓūn · illā ʿalā azwājihim aw mā malakat aymānuhum fa-innahum ghayru malūmīn · fa-mani'btaghā warāʾa dhālika fa-ulāʾika humu'l-ʿādūn*

"**They are those who guard their chastity** — except with their spouses…; in that they are not blamed. **Whoever seeks beyond that — those are the ones who transgress.**"

### فُرُوج — root: ف-ر-ج

**The concrete sense of the root: the opening, the cleft, the gap between two things.** *Faraj* — an interval; *infirāj* — an opening out; and from here comes the sense of **the opening up of distress** (*faraj* — relief).

**In this context the word is a figure used for the private parts**, and the lexicographers record it as such. **And there is a point worth recording:** the same root is used in the Qurʾān for the sky as well — *mā lahā min **furūj*** (Qāf 50/6, "it has no cracks in it"). **That verse was treated in `050-kaf.md`.** **The same root: in one place the proof of an unbroken surface, in the other the area that is to be guarded.** I record this as an observation about the language, and I build no connection of ruling between the two uses.

### حَافِظُون — and the breadth of the item

***Ḥāfiẓ*: guarding, watching over. The root ح-ف-ظ was treated at `085-buruc.md` 85/22 (*fī lawḥin maḥfūẓ*) and at `086-tarik.md` 86/4 (*laysa ʿalayhā ḥāfiẓ*).**

**A detail of word order must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it:** in the fifth verse the particle is **لِ** (*li-furūjihim*), and in the exception in the sixth verse the particle turns to **عَلَىٰ** (*illā ʿalā azwājihim*).

**Two approaches to explaining this change are transmitted:**

| Reading | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | The exception attaches not to the verb *ḥāfiẓūn* but to the sentence as a whole; *ʿalā* carries the sense "with regard to…" |
| 2 | *Ḥāfiẓūn* here means "those who hold themselves back", and *ʿalā* marks the place where that holding back is lifted |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### مَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَٰنُهُمْ

**The construction names an institution that in fact existed in the period the Qurʾān was revealed — slavery.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`), I issue no legal ruling here and I do not go into the details of the historical institution.** Two matters must be recorded, and both can be verified from the text:

**One.** The Qurʾān does not **establish** this institution; it names an arrangement it found already in place in the society into which it was revealed.

**Two.** Other verses of the Qurʾān in the same field set the release from slavery **again and again as a good, an expiation and a goal**: *fakku raqaba* (al-Balad 90/13 — treated in `090-beled.md`), *fa-taḥrīru raqaba* (al-Nisāʾ 4/92, al-Māʾida 5/89, al-Mujādala 58/3 — treated in `058-mucadele.md`), *fa-kātibūhum* (al-Nūr 24/33).

**Beyond these two entries I draw no conclusion.** The historical and legal frame of the matter cannot be settled under this verse, and no attempt is made to settle it.

### ٱلْعَادُون — root: ع-د-و

**The concrete sense of the root: to cross a boundary, to step over it.** From the same root come *ʿaduww* (enemy — the one who tramples the boundary), *ʿudwān* (transgression), *ʿadā* (he ran, he crossed).

**The choice of word is worth recording:** the seventh verse does not say "sinners" or "*fāsiqūn*". **The picture in the word is the crossing of a line** — and the sixth verse had just drawn that line. **The verdict, that is, is attached to the boundary that has been drawn.**

---

## 23/8 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ لِأَمَٰنَٰتِهِمْ وَعَهْدِهِمْ رَٰعُونَ

*wa'lladhīna hum li-amānātihim wa ʿahdihim rāʿūn*

"**They are those who watch over their trusts and their covenant.**"

### أَمَانَات and عَهْد — plural and singular

**A detail of word order must be recorded:** *amānāt* has come in the **plural**, *ʿahd* in the **singular**.

**The explanations the lexicographers give for this difference are transmitted:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | Trusts are **many and various** (property, a word, an office, a secret); a covenant is **a single kind of binding** |
| 2 | *ʿAhd* here is **a generic noun**; its coming in the singular does not exclude plurality |
| 3 | Among the variant readings a reading *li-amānati* (singular) is also transmitted; on that reading both words are singular |

**The variant reading must be recorded here: the readings *li-amānātihim* (plural) and *li-amānatihim* (singular) are transmitted.** **It produces no radical change in meaning** — one reports the trusts one by one, the other the kind "trust".

**The root أ-م-ن was treated at the first verse.** **What belongs here is this:** *amāna* and *īmān* are **from the same root.** The name of the sūra and the name of this item, that is, come from one root. I record this as a datum of the language; **I draw no ruling from it**, but I give this much as an observation: **the root derives being trustworthy and trusting from the same place.**

### رَاعُون — root: ر-ع-ي

**The concrete sense of the root: to pasture an animal, to watch over it.** *Rāʿī* — shepherd; *raʿy* — pasturing; *riʿāya* — watching over, tending.

**The choice of word is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being the pastoral sense of the root:** *ḥifẓ* (the verb in the fifth verse) is **to hold, to keep** — it is static. *Riʿāya* is **to keep the eye on a thing continually**; the shepherd stands at the head of the flock, walks, counts. **The two items use two different verbs: one for guarding, the other for watching over.**

---

## 23/9 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ عَلَىٰ صَلَوَٰتِهِمْ يُحَافِظُونَ

*wa'lladhīna hum ʿalā ṣalawātihim yuḥāfiẓūn*

"**They keep watch over their prayers.**"

### The frame of the list — opening with the prayer and closing with the prayer

**This is the clearest structural element of the sūra, and it can be counted directly from the text:** the list consists of six items, and **the first item and the sixth are on the same subject — the prayer.**

| Order | Verse | Field |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **2** | **The prayer** |
| 2 | 3 | Speech — *laghw* |
| 3 | 4 | Property — *zakāt* |
| 4 | 5-7 | The body — *furūj* |
| 5 | 8 | Relationship — *amāna* and *ʿahd* |
| **6** | **9** | **The prayer** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the list's own arrangement:** the four items in between are **speech, property, the body and relationship** — that is, the four fields in which a person comes into contact with others. **The list takes these four fields between its two items on prayer.** The frame is outside, life is inside.

**And the lexicographers record this frame as well;** but the meaning loaded onto the frame is interpretation, and I do not present the reading above as binding.

### The difference in wording between the two items on prayer

**The differences between the two verses can be counted one by one, and all four are regular:**

| | **23/2** | **23/9** |
|---|---|---|
| The word | *Ṣalāt* — **singular** | *Ṣalawāt* — **plural** |
| The particle | *Fī* — **inside** | *ʿAlā* — **over / above** |
| Kind of predicate | *Khāshiʿūn* — **active participle (a noun)** | *Yuḥāfiẓūn* — **imperfect (a verb)** |
| The form of the verb | Form I (*khashaʿa*) | **Form III (*ḥāfaẓa*)** — continuity and reciprocity |
| What it reports | **An inward state** | **An ongoing guarding** |

**All four differences can be verified from the text.** And the reading I build on them is the following, which I give as my own reading:

**One. Singular and plural.** The second verse looks **inside a prayer being performed**; the ninth looks **at the whole of the prayers**, that is, at the appointed times and the order.

**Two. *Fī* – *ʿalā*.** *Fī* reports the inside, *ʿalā* a watching from above. **One tells of what is inside the prayer, the other of standing over the prayer.**

**Three. Noun and verb.** In Arabic a nominal sentence reports **fixity** (*thubūt*), a verbal sentence **renewal** (*tajaddud*); this is a distinction the lexicographers record consistently. ***Khushūʿ* is given as a settled state, keeping watch as a repeated work.**

**Four. Form III.** The pattern *ḥāfaẓa*, unlike *ḥafiẓa* of Form I, reports **continuity and reciprocal effort**. The same root appeared in the fifth verse as *ḥāfiẓūn* (active participle, from Form I).

**To draw it together — and this drawing together is my own reading:** the list opens with **the state inside the prayer** and closes with **the keeping up of the prayers.** **The difference between the two is the difference between one prayer and a lifetime of prayer.** And the list places between the two all the fields in which a person makes contact.

---

## 23/10-11 — أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْوَٰرِثُونَ · ٱلَّذِينَ يَرِثُونَ ٱلْفِرْدَوْسَ هُمْ فِيهَا خَٰلِدُونَ

*ulāʾika humu'l-wārithūn · alladhīna yarithūna'l-firdawsa hum fīhā khālidūn*

"**Those are the heirs** — who will inherit al-Firdaws; there they remain."

### أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ — the return of the verdict

**The word order must be recorded:** the conditional clause that has run for nine verses is gathered up in the tenth by **a demonstrative pronoun**. *Ulāʾika* — "those are the ones". And it is reinforced by **the pronoun of separation (*hum*)**: in Arabic this pronoun, coming between subject and predicate, reports **specification**.

**The sentence, that is, is closer to "the ones who are heirs are precisely these" than to "they are heirs".**

### و-ر-ث — inheritance

**The sense of the root: the passing of a property or a standing from its owner to another.** *Wārith* — the one who takes what passes; *mīrāth* — the thing that passes.

**The choice of word is worth recording, and it has two sides:**

**One. An inheritance is not something earned by working for it** — it passes from another. **Two. But being an heir depends on a qualification** — kinship, entitlement, standing.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this double character of the word:** after nine verses of enumerated acts, the outcome is given not with a word for **earning** but with a word for **transfer**. **The outcome, that is, is named not as the price of the acts enumerated but as something that passes to those who carry them.**

**The same root was treated at `044-duhan.md` 44/28 (*wa awrathnāhā qawman ākharīn*) and in `026-suara.md`** — there the inheritance was what a destroyed people left behind. **The same word, in two different directions.**

### ٱلْفِرْدَوْس

**The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: here, and al-Kahf 18/107** (*jannāti'l-firdaws*).

**The lexicographers differ over the origin of the word:**

| View | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | **It is Arabic** — *firdaws*, a garden enclosed about, with trees and vines |
| 2 | **It is a foreign word taken into Arabic** — of Persian/Syriac origin, "a garden surrounded by a wall" |

**Both views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The point at which the two views meet is worth recording:** at the core of the word lies the idea of **an enclosed, protected garden.**

**And the pronoun must be recorded: *hum fīhā khālidūn* — a feminine singular pronoun.** *Firdaws* is counted masculine; the lexicographers record that the pronoun refers back to the word **janna** (at al-Kahf 18/107 the construction is *jannāti'l-firdaws*).

### The end of the block — where the list is attached

**And a structural entry:** the list closes with **a place.** **This overlaps with the closing of the *ʿibādu'l-raḥmān* list treated in `025-furkan.md`** — there too the list closed with a place: *ulāʾika yujzawna'l-**ghurfata** bimā ṣabarū* (25/75). **Two lists, in two separate sūras, both ending with the name of a dwelling.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

---

## The two lists of attributes — al-Muʾminūn 23/1-11 and al-Furqān 25/63-77

**This comparison is required by this commentary, because the two lists do the same work in two separate sūras of the Qurʾān: they describe the one who will attain deliverance not by a name but by a series of attributes.** In `025-furkan.md` that list was treated item by item; here a **comparison** is made, the analysis is not repeated.

### The difference in position

| | **al-Muʾminūn 23/1-11** | **al-Furqān 25/63-77** |
|---|---|---|
| Its place in the sūra | **The opening** — the first eleven verses | **The closing** — the last fifteen verses |
| What precedes it | **Nothing** — the sūra begins with the list | **A chain of objections** (25/4-8) and the question *wa mā'l-raḥmān* (25/60) |
| The name of the list | ***al-Muʾminūn*** — by way of faith | ***ʿIbādu'l-Raḥmān*** — by way of service and of a name |
| Where the verdict falls | **At the start** (23/1) and at the end (23/10-11) | **At the end** (25/75-76) |
| The closing | *al-Firdaws* — an inheritance | *al-Ghurfa* — a recompense |
| The grammar | *alladhīna **hum** + fronted complement + noun* | *alladhīna* + verb (for the most part) |

**The difference in position is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** the al-Muʾminūn list **is not an answer to anything** — the sūra begins with it, there is no objection in the middle. The al-Furqān list, as was shown in `025-furkan.md`, **is an indirect answer to the objection in the seventh verse of that sūra**, and its first item (walking) is precisely the subject of that objection. **One is framed as a description, then, the other as an answer.**

### An item-by-item comparison

| Field | **al-Muʾminūn 23** | **al-Furqān 25** | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Prayer / night worship** | 2 — *fī ṣalātihim khāshiʿūn* · 9 — *ʿalā ṣalawātihim yuḥāfiẓūn* | 64 — *yabītūna li-rabbihim sujjadan wa qiyāmā* | **Shared** — the forms differ |
| **Idle speech (*laghw*)** | 3 — *ʿani'l-laghwi muʿriḍūn* | 72 — *idhā marrū bi'l-laghwi marrū kirāmā* | **Shared — the same word** |
| **Property** | 4 — *li'l-zakāti fāʿilūn* | 67 — *lam yusrifū wa lam yaqturū … qawāmā* | **Shared** — one an act, the other a measure |
| **Chastity** | 5-7 — *li-furūjihim ḥāfiẓūn* + the exception + *al-ʿādūn* | 68 — *wa lā yaznūn* | **Shared** — one a guarding, the other a prohibition |
| **Trust and covenant** | 8 — *li-amānātihim wa ʿahdihim rāʿūn* | 72 — *lā yashhadūna'l-zūr* (a neighbouring field) | **Explicit only in al-Muʾminūn** |
| **Walking / bearing** | **Absent** | 63 — *yamshūna ʿalā'l-arḍi hawnā* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **The answer to the ignorant** | **Absent** | 63 — *qālū salāmā* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **Associating partners, and killing** | **Absent** (separately at the end of the sūra, at 117) | 68 — *lā yadʿūna … wa lā yaqtulūna'l-nafs* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **The door of repentance** | **Absent** | 70-71 — *illā man tāba … yubaddilu'llāhu sayyiʾātihim ḥasanāt* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **The prayer for protection from the Fire** | **Absent** | 65-66 — *iṣrif ʿannā ʿadhāba jahannam* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **The bearing towards the verses** | **Absent** (treated separately in the sūra at 66 and 105) | 73 — *lam yakhirrū ʿalayhā ṣumman wa ʿumyānā* | **Only in al-Furqān** |
| **The prayer for one's family** | **Absent** | 74 — *hab lanā min azwājinā … qurrata aʿyun* | **Only in al-Furqān** |

### Observations that follow from the comparison

**What follows is my own reading and is not binding; its grounds are the table above.**

**One. The shared items are four: prayer, idle speech, property, chastity.** And these four are **fields present in the daily life of every human being.** The two lists meet on common ground in these four fields.

**Two. The item on *laghw* is in both lists, and in both the verb is "to pass by" or "to turn away"** — in neither is it "to silence", "to prevent", "to answer". **The two sūras propose the same bearing on this item:** not removal but **not making contact.**

**Three. The al-Furqān list is longer than the al-Muʾminūn list and contains more items about "relationship"** — walking, the answer to the ignorant, the prayer for one's family, the request to lead. **The al-Muʾminūn list contains more items about "obligation"** — prayer, *zakāt*, chastity, trust.

**Four. The item on trust and covenant is explicitly present only in al-Muʾminūn.** And it stands immediately before the sixth item of the list, that is, **right inside the frame.**

**Five. Both lists end with a dwelling** — *al-Firdaws* and *al-Ghurfa*. **And neither gives a name on its own:** al-Muʾminūn says "they will inherit", al-Furqān says "they will be recompensed for their patience". **In both places the outcome comes after a verb.**

**Six — and I record this separately:** the two lists are neither copies of one another nor complements of one another. **Each is bound to the work of its own sūra:** al-Furqān is answering an objection, al-Muʾminūn is building a description. **Two different structures come out of the same material** — and this shows that reading the lists as a "sum of items" falls short.

---

## 23/12 — وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَٰنَ مِن سُلَٰلَةٍ مِّن طِينٍ

*wa laqad khalaqnā'l-insāna min sulālatin min ṭīn*

"**We created the human being, assuredly, from an extract of clay.**"

### The limit of this block — the methodological note, set at the very start

**These verses belong to a field treated several times across this series, and the same limit was drawn each time. I set that limit here too, at the outset:**

> **At `040-gafir.md` 40/67:** *"the dictionary senses of the words *nuṭfa* and *ʿalaqa* are given by the lexicographers. **I do not enter into equating these one-to-one with the terms of modern embryology;** the work of the verse is to give a description of stages, and the proof lies in these stages **not being in human hands**."*

> **At `039-zumer.md` 39/6:** *"at `040-gafir.md` 40/67 the same staged formation was told in six steps, and the methodological limit was drawn there: one does not enter into equating these expressions one-to-one with the terms of modern embryology. I keep the same limit here."*

> **At `032-secde.md` 32/7-9** the same field was treated; **in `096-alak.md`** a section with five grounds was written on the word *ʿalaq* and the embryological claim was rejected; and in the **`075-kiyame.md`, `086-tarik.md`, `077-murselat.md`, `053-necm.md`, `036-yasin.md` and `071-nuh.md`** sections the same position was kept.

**I keep the same limit here and do not repeat its grounds.** In brief: **the dictionary sense of the words is given and not cut short; but this is not presented as the claim of the verse and is not turned into a proof of miracle.** What will be treated in these verses is the meanings of the words and **the ordering of the verbs** — not a table of development.

**And one further entry, as it was written in `077-murselat.md`:** the argument of the verse sets out **from something the one addressed knows.** Birth, miscarriage, bone and flesh were things everyone saw. **A proof cannot be built with something the one addressed does not know;** for this reason dressing the verse in modern knowledge does not strengthen the argument but weakens it.

### سُلَالَة — root: س-ل-ل

**The root was worked out at `032-secde.md` 32/8** (*thumma jaʿala naslahū min sulālatin min māʾin mahīn*). **I do not repeat it here.** The summary given there:

> **The concrete sense of the root:** to draw a thing slowly out of its sheath, stripping it clear. *Salla'l-sayfa* — he drew the sword from its scabbard. *Sulāla* — **the extract drawn out** of a thing. *Salīl* — lineage, offspring.

**What belongs here is the difference between the two verses, and it must be recorded:**

| | **al-Sajda 32/8** | **al-Muʾminūn 23/12** |
|---|---|---|
| The extract of what | *min sulālatin min **māʾin mahīn*** — the extract of **a despised fluid** | *min sulālatin min **ṭīn*** — the extract of **clay** |
| Of whom | *naslahū* — the human being's **offspring** | *al-insān* — **the human being** |
| Where it stands | The **second** step of the creation | The **beginning** of the creation |

**The difference is worth recording:** in al-Sajda *sulāla* reports the source of **the offspring**, here the source of **the human being**. **And read together the two verses complete each other:** al-Sajda had said first that the beginning was from clay (*badaʾa khalqa'l-insāni min ṭīn*) and then that the offspring came from a fluid; **al-Muʾminūn joins the two in a single construction** — *sulālatin min ṭīn*.

### مِن طِين

**طِين** — **clay: the mixture of earth and water.** The word was treated at `032-secde.md` 32/7 and at `037-saffat.md` 37/11 (*min ṭīnin lāzib* — sticky clay).

**And the twofold repetition of the particle *min* must be recorded:** *min sulālatin **min** ṭīn.* **There are two steps: clay → the extract drawn from clay → the human being.** The verse, that is, does not say "from clay" directly; **it places an operation in between.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is the second *min*:** an **operation** is set between the material and the product.

---

## 23/13 — ثُمَّ جَعَلْنَٰهُ نُطْفَةً فِى قَرَارٍ مَّكِينٍ

*thumma jaʿalnāhu nuṭfatan fī qarārin makīn*

"**Then We made him a drop in a secure lodging.**"

### نُطْفَة

**Root ن-ط-ف: the core sense the lexicographers give is to drip, to trickle.** *Naṭafa'l-māʾ* — the water dripped; *nuṭfa* — a small quantity of water, a drop. **The word was treated at `075-kiyame.md` 75/37 (*a-lam yaku nuṭfatan min maniyyin yumnā*), at `076-insan.md` 76/2 (*min nuṭfatin amshāj*) and at `040-gafir.md` 40/67.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**The methodological limit was set above; the meaning of the word consists of this, and no technical description is built upon it.**

### قَرَارٍ مَّكِين — two words

**قَرَار — root ق-ر-ر:** **to stand in a place, to be fixed, to settle.** From the same root come *qarār* (the place one stops), *mustaqarr* (a place of settlement), *qurra* (the cooling of the eye — the eye coming to rest in one place; treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/74).

**مَكِين — root م-ك-ن:** **to have a place, to become firm.** *Makān* is from the same root. *Makīn* — **firm in place, strong in standing.** The word is used in the Qurʾān of a human being as well: *innaka'l-yawma ladaynā **makīnun** amīn* (Yūsuf 12/54).

**The build of the construction must be recorded:** *qarārin makīn* — "a firm resting place". **Both words report fixity**, and one comes as the adjective of the other. **There is emphasis, that is.**

**And this construction will come back once more in the sūra:** in the fiftieth verse the place in which the son of Mary and his mother are sheltered will be named *rabwatin **dhāti qarārin** wa maʿīn*. **The same word: one the place at the beginning of the forming, the other a refuge.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation: **both places are sheltered, and both are named with the word *qarār*.**

### The change of verb — from خَلَقْنَا to جَعَلْنَا

**And here the real question of the block begins.** In the twelfth verse the verb was *khalaqnā*; in the thirteenth it turns to *jaʿalnā*.

**The distinction was recorded at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/3 and holds here:**

> ***Jaʿala* in Arabic reports the putting of a thing from one state into another — this is what separates it from *khalaqa* (to bring into being from nothing).**

**At the second step, that is, nothing new is brought into being; what exists is put from one state into another.** This is the first link of the chain of verbs that will be tabulated at the fourteenth verse.

---

## 23/14 — ثُمَّ خَلَقْنَا ٱلنُّطْفَةَ عَلَقَةً فَخَلَقْنَا ٱلْعَلَقَةَ مُضْغَةً فَخَلَقْنَا ٱلْمُضْغَةَ عِظَٰمًا فَكَسَوْنَا ٱلْعِظَٰمَ لَحْمًا ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَٰهُ خَلْقًا ءَاخَرَ فَتَبَارَكَ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنُ ٱلْخَٰلِقِينَ

*thumma khalaqnā'l-nuṭfata ʿalaqatan fa-khalaqnā'l-ʿalaqata muḍghatan fa-khalaqnā'l-muḍghata ʿiẓāman fa-kasawnā'l-ʿiẓāma laḥman thumma anshaʾnāhu khalqan ākhar, fa-tabāraka'llāhu aḥsanu'l-khāliqīn*

"**Then We made that drop an *ʿalaqa*, and made the *ʿalaqa* a morsel, and made the morsel bones, and clothed the bones with flesh. Then We built him up as another creation. How exalted is God, the best of creators!**"

### The words — kept to their dictionary sense

**The limit set above holds for all three of these words.**

| Word | Root | The sense the lexicographers give | Where in this series |
|---|---|---|---|
| **عَلَقَة** | ع-ل-ق | **Something that hangs, holds on, clings**; and a clot; and a leech (*ʿalaq*) | `096-alak.md` 96/2 — with the limit section on five grounds |
| **مُضْغَة** | م-ض-غ | **A piece the size of one chew** — *maḍgh*, to chew | Here for the first time in this series |
| **عِظَام** | ع-ظ-م | **Bones** — *ʿaẓm*; from the root, *ʿaẓīm* (great, hard) | `036-yasin.md` 36/78, `075-kiyame.md` 75/3 |

**The section written on the word *ʿalaq* in `096-alak.md` holds here too, and I do not repeat it here.** The conclusion there was this: **the sense of the word — "hanging, holding on, clinging" — is given and not cut short; but it cannot be presented as the claim of the verse and cannot be turned into a proof of miracle.**

**An addition on مُضْغَة:** the word reports a **measure** — the amount taken into the mouth at one time. **The picture in the word, that is, is of size**, not of shape. I give this as an entry about the language.

### The ordering of the verbs — the real subject of this block

**The verse tells the forming with seven verbs, and the verbs are not the same.** This is a fact that can be counted directly from the text:

| Order | Connective | Verb | Root / form | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | — | *khalaqnā* (12) | خ-ل-ق | **Created** — the human being, from the extract of clay |
| 2 | **ثُمَّ** | *jaʿalnā* (13) | ج-ع-ل | **Put from one state into another** — a drop |
| 3 | **ثُمَّ** | *khalaqnā* | خ-ل-ق | Drop → *ʿalaqa* |
| 4 | **فَ** | *fa-khalaqnā* | خ-ل-ق | *ʿAlaqa* → *muḍgha* |
| 5 | **فَ** | *fa-khalaqnā* | خ-ل-ق | *Muḍgha* → bones |
| 6 | **فَ** | *fa-kasawnā* | ك-س-و | **Clothed** — the bones with flesh |
| 7 | **ثُمَّ** | *thumma anshaʾnāhu* | ن-ش-أ | **Built up** — as another creation |

**Three regularities can be counted:**

**One. *Thumma* occurs three times, *fa* three times.** In Arabic *fa* reports **succession without a gap**, *thumma* **succession with a gap**; this is the lexicographers' common entry.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this distinction between the two particles:** the three occurrences of *thumma* stand at three points — human being→drop, drop→*ʿalaqa*, and after the flesh has been put on→**another creation.** **The text, that is, sets a gap at three places; the steps in between are given joined to one another with *fa*.** Three thresholds, four adjoining steps.

**Two. The verb is *khalaqnā* four times, then once *kasawnā*, then once *anshaʾnā*.** That is, **the verb changes in the last two steps.**

**Three. Each time, the object is the object of the preceding sentence, and it comes definite with the article *alif-lām*:** *al-nuṭfata → al-ʿalaqata → al-muḍghata → al-ʿiẓāma.* **The chain is built at the level of the words as well:** each sentence makes the product of the one before it its own material. This is a form of *takrīr* that the lexicographers record.

### فَكَسَوْنَا ٱلْعِظَٰمَ لَحْمًا — "We clothed"

**The root ك-س-و is worked out here for the first time in this series.** **The concrete sense of the root: to clothe with a garment.** *Kiswa* — clothing; *kisāʾ* — a wrap taken over oneself; *iksāʾ* — the act of clothing.

**And the choice of verb must be recorded:** the verse does not say *khalaqnā* here. **While "We created" is said at five steps, at the sixth "We clothed" is said.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this single change of verb:** at the other steps one thing **turned into another** — the drop became an *ʿalaqa*, the *ʿalaqa* became a morsel. **Here there is no turning: the bone remains bone, and something else is put over it.** The verb carries this difference. **To clothe does not require the disappearance of what is clothed.**

**And a link between words is worth recording:** the same idea is used in the Qurʾān for a garment as well — *wa jaʿalnā'l-layla **libāsā*** (al-Nabaʾ 78/10; treated in `078-nebe.md` and at `025-furkan.md` 25/47). **The root is different (ل-ب-س / ك-س-و) and I build no derivational link between them;** what is shared is the idea that **a covering does not do away with what lies beneath it.**

### ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَٰهُ خَلْقًا ءَاخَرَ — the centre of gravity of the block

**This sentence deserves separate and detailed treatment.**

#### أَنشَأَ — root: ن-ش-أ

**The root was worked out at `056-vakia.md` 56/35 (*innā anshaʾnāhunna inshāʾā*) and at 56/62 (*wa laqad ʿalimtumu'l-nashʾata'l-ūlā*).** **I do not repeat it here.** In summary, what was recorded there is this:

> **The verb is *anshaʾnā* — not "We created" but "We built up".** The verse reports **a rebuilding**. And the same root is used at 56/62 for birth in this world: *al-nashʾata'l-ūlā* — "the first building up".

**And the following point recorded in `056-vakia.md` attaches directly here:** al-Wāqiʿa used the verb it applies to the garden, twenty-seven verses later, for the forming in this world. **al-Muʾminūn, for its part, uses the same verb for the *last step* of the forming in this world.**

**The other links of the root across this series:** `067-mulk.md` 67/23 (*alladhī anshaʾakum*), `056-vakia.md` 56/72 (*am naḥnu'l-munshiʾūn*).

#### The word order of the sentence

**Three details must be recorded:**

**One. The particle is *thumma*** — that is, a gap is set between this and what precedes. **The largest gap among the seven steps is here**, because after this *thumma* the verb changes as well.

**Two. The object pronoun is هُ — "him".** That is, **the same being** continues. The sentence does not say "We created something else"; it says **"We built him up as another creation"**. **The continuity of the subject is preserved by the pronoun.**

**Three. *Khalqan ākhar* — the lexicographers differ over its parsing:**

| View | Parsing | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Absolute object** (reporting the kind) | "We built him up **with a building of another kind**" |
| 2 | **Circumstantial accusative** | "We built him up **in the state of another creation**" |
| 3 | **A second object** — *anshaʾa* here in the sense of *jaʿala* | "We made him **another creature**" |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **What is common to all three is this:** what is new is not the material but **the kind.**

#### What "another creation" is — and why one stops here

**The verse does not say what it is.** This is a silence of the text, and it must be recorded.

**The explanations widely transmitted in the classical exegetical tradition are these:**

| # | Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The breathing of the spirit** | Joining it with *wa nafakha fīhi min rūḥihī* at al-Sajda 32/9 |
| 2 | **Coming alive and moving** — the passage from a lifeless mass to a living being | The sense of the verb (*anshaʾa*) "to set up and stand upright" |
| 3 | **The giving of the human faculties** — reason, speech, discrimination | The verse ending with *aḥsanu'l-khāliqīn* |
| 4 | **Birth** — the coming out | *Thumma* reporting a large gap |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **And an entry, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** since the verse gives no modality, no modality is produced here. The entry set for the construction *min rūḥihī* at `032-secde.md` 32/9 holds here as well.

**The only thing I record as my own reading is this, and its ground is the change of verb:** after steps that can be counted, the verse sets **a step that cannot be counted.** **The preceding six steps change the object; the seventh changes the kind.** And the text does not give the name of that kind. **This silence can be read as a part of the structure of the verse:** after the part that can be told has been told, a threshold is set.

### فَتَبَارَكَ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنُ ٱلْخَٰلِقِينَ

**تَبَارَكَ** — **the root ب-ر-ك was worked out at `067-mulk.md` 67/1** and treated again at `025-furkan.md` 25/1. **I do not repeat it here.** In summary: the core of the root is **baraka** — the kneeling of the camel, settling in a place and staying fixed; and from this, **a good that lasts and increases.**

**And the word order must be recorded: the sentence begins with *fa*.** The praise, that is, comes as **the outcome** of the process described — it is not an independent sentence.

**أَحْسَنُ ٱلْخَٰلِقِين** — "the best of creators". **The lexicographers dwell on the construction, because the elative is built with a plural.**

| Reading | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | In Arabic the verb **خلق** is also used in the sense of "to give shape to an existing material, to fashion" (thus at al-Māʾida 5/110 *takhluqu mina'l-ṭīni* occurs of Jesus (ʿĪsā)). The construction is built according to this usage of the language |
| 2 | **The speech is framed in the language the one addressed accepts:** they called others "creator" too; the verse points, from inside that language, to the highest of them |
| 3 | The elative here reports **not comparison but absolute pre-eminence** (this usage is recorded in Arabic) |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **And I do not enter a theological dispute:** no ruling about the existence of other creators is drawn from the construction — indeed the ninety-first verse of the sūra will establish exactly the opposite.

**A link within the sūra must be recorded:** this is **the first of the five superlative constructions** in the sūra. The others: *khayru'l-munzilīn* (29), *khayru'l-rāziqīn* (72), *khayru'l-rāḥimīn* (109 and 118). **All five of the five constructions are built on the agent of a verb** — creating, sending down, providing, showing mercy. This is a pattern that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 23/15-16 — ثُمَّ إِنَّكُم بَعْدَ ذَٰلِكَ لَمَيِّتُونَ · ثُمَّ إِنَّكُمْ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ تُبْعَثُونَ

*thumma innakum baʿda dhālika la-mayyitūn · thumma innakum yawma'l-qiyāmati tubʿathūn*

"**Then, after that, you will surely die. Then, on the Day of Resurrection, you will be raised.**"

### The change of pronoun

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the block:** what is told from the twelfth verse to the fourteenth is in **the third person** (*al-insān*, *jaʿalnāhu*, *anshaʾnāhu*). **In the fifteenth verse the address turns to the second person:** *innakum* — "you".

**This is an operation called *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address) in Arabic rhetoric.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being that the change of pronoun falls exactly at this point:** while the forming is being told, the one addressed is outside — what is told is a third party. **When death and resurrection are named, the one addressed is brought inside.** The process described thereby stops being a piece of information and becomes a piece of news.

### Two occurrences of *thumma*, and two patterns

| Verse | Pattern | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| **15** | *innakum … **la**-mayyitūn* — **active participle**, reinforced with *lām* | **A settled, unavoidable state** |
| **16** | *innakum … **tubʿathūn*** — **imperfect verb**, passive | **A work to be done** — whose agent is not named |

**The difference is worth recording:** death is given as a **state** (with a noun), resurrection as a **work** (with a verb). **And the verb of the resurrection is passive** — the doer is not named, because it is being named throughout the sūra anyway.

**مَيِّتُون** — the lexicographers dwell on the pattern of the word: a distinction between *mayyit* and *māʾit* is transmitted; **the first is one who has died, the second one who is going to die.** Here the meaning is **"those who are going to die"**, and the lexicographers record that the pattern *mayyit* is used in this sense as well.

### Context — how the chain of proofs is built

**And the logic of the block must be recorded:** twelve to fourteen tell that the human being passes through a process that **can be seen**. Fifteen and sixteen add **two further steps** to the same sentence: death and resurrection.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being that the three occurrences of *thumma* stand in one and the same series:** the text does not build a separate proof for the resurrection — **it presents it as a link in the series it is already working through.** The same method was treated at `036-yasin.md` 36/77-79 and at `075-kiyame.md` 75/36-40. **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 23/17 — وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا فَوْقَكُمْ سَبْعَ طَرَآئِقَ وَمَا كُنَّا عَنِ ٱلْخَلْقِ غَٰفِلِينَ

*wa laqad khalaqnā fawqakum sabʿa ṭarāʾiqa wa mā kunnā ʿani'l-khalqi ghāfilīn*

"**We created above you seven paths, assuredly. And We have not been heedless of the creation.**"

### طَرَآئِق — root: ط-ر-ق

**The root was worked out in `086-tarik.md`.** The description given there: **the original sense of the root is to strike, to beat, to hit with something hard.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the second branch of the root, and the lexicographers record the two together:**

| Word | Meaning | The link |
|---|---|---|
| **طَرَقَ** | To strike, to hit | The core of the root |
| **طَرِيق** | **Road** | The track opened by feet **striking it again and again** — the explanation the lexicographers give |
| **مِطْرَقَة** | Hammer | The instrument of striking |
| **طَرِيقَة** | **A road; and a layer of a thing laid one over another** | The two branches |
| **طَرَائِق** | The plural of *ṭarīqa* | The word here |

**The lexicographers differ over what the word reports in this verse:**

| Reading | What *ṭarāʾiq* is | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The seven heavens** — a naming that stands in place of *samāwāt* | *fawqakum* (above you) and the number "seven"; at the sūra's 86th verse *al-samāwāti'l-sabʿ* occurs explicitly |
| 2 | **Layers laid one over another** | The sense of the word *ṭarīqa* as "a layer over a thing" |
| 3 | **Paths** — the paths down which the angels or the affairs descend | The original sense of the root |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **The first reading has a ground within the sūra, and I record it:** the eighty-sixth verse will say *rabbu'l-samāwāti'l-sabʿ*. **The same sūra names the same number with two different words.**

**And a limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** on the basis of this verse **I do not enter into any matching with layers of the atmosphere, belts of celestial bodies or similar modern classifications.** The grounds written in `086-tarik.md` for astronomical claims and in `096-alak.md` for embryological claims hold here as well. **The meaning of the word is given; nothing is built beyond it.**

### وَمَا كُنَّا عَنِ ٱلْخَلْقِ غَٰفِلِينَ

**The sentence is worth recording, and it is the key to this block.**

The root **غ-ف-ل**: **to remain unaware of a thing, to let it slip from mind, to give it no attention.** *Ghafla* — inattention; *ghufl* — bearing no mark upon it, unrecognised.

**The word order must be recorded: *mā kunnā* — a negated perfect, "We have not been".** That is, at no time. **And *ʿani'l-khalqi* has been brought forward** (*taqdīm*): "of the creation — We are not heedless."

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the place of the sentence in the verse:** after the seven layers have been named, the sentence one expects is a statement of power. **The sentence that comes is a statement of *attention*.** The verse, that is, speaks not of greatness but of **not having been left.**

**The same idea was recorded in `001-fatiha.md` in the analysis of the name *Rabb*:** *"Rabb is the name of an order that is working; not of a clock wound up and abandoned."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the scope of the word *al-khalq* must be recorded:** the word means both "the act of creating" and "the created things"; both parsings are transmitted. **In either case the verdict comes out at the same place.**

---

## 23/18 — وَأَنزَلْنَا مِنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مَآءًۢ بِقَدَرٍ فَأَسْكَنَّٰهُ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَإِنَّا عَلَىٰ ذَهَابٍۭ بِهِۦ لَقَٰدِرُونَ

*wa anzalnā mina'l-samāʾi māʾan bi-qadarin fa-askannāhu fi'l-arḍ, wa innā ʿalā dhahābin bihī la-qādirūn*

"**We sent down water from the sky by a measure and settled it in the earth. And We are certainly able to take it away.**"

### بِقَدَرٍ — measure

**The root ق-د-ر was worked out in detail in `097-kadr.md`, and separately at `054-kamer.md` 54/49 (*innā kulla shayʾin khalaqnāhu bi-qadar*)**, and treated again at `039-zumer.md` 39/67 and `025-furkan.md` 25/2. **I do not repeat it here.** In summary: **the most basic sense of the root is to measure, to cut to size, to fix the amount of a thing.**

**Exactly this connection was made in `097-kadr.md`:** the verse *"We created everything according to a measure (bi-qadar) (al-Qamar 54/49)"* was adduced as the proof of the sense "measure" for the word *qadr*. **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is this, and it is worth recording:** al-Qamar 54/49 sets the measure as a general ruling for **everything**; **this verse applies the same measure to a single phenomenon, rain.**

| Place | Scope |
|---|---|
| `054-kamer.md` 54/49 | *kulla shayʾin* — **everything** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/18** | *māʾan* — **water** |

**And the particle must be recorded: *bi-qadar* — "with a measure".** The *bāʾ* here reports **accompaniment or instrument**; the water comes down together with its measure.

### فَأَسْكَنَّٰهُ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ — and a comparison with al-Zumar 39/21

The root **س-ك-ن**: **the ceasing of motion, settling in a place.** *Sukūn* — stillness; *sakīna* — inward stillness; *maskan* — the place settled in; *sākin* — motionless.

**The root was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/45** (*wa law shāʾa la-jaʿalahū sākinā* — of the shadow) and at `030-rum.md` 30/21 (*li-taskunū ilayhā*).

**And the same phenomenon — the water that comes down passing into the ground — was told with a different verb at `039-zumer.md` 39/21. The two verses must be set side by side:**

| | **al-Zumar 39/21** | **al-Muʾminūn 23/18** |
|---|---|---|
| The sentence | *anzala mina'l-samāʾi māʾan **fa-salakahū yanābīʿa** fi'l-arḍ* | *anzalnā mina'l-samāʾi māʾan … **fa-askannāhu** fi'l-arḍ* |
| The verb | ***salaka*** — س-ل-ك: **to send into a path, to make pass, to make travel** | ***askana*** — س-ك-ن: **to bring to a stop, to settle** |
| What happens to the water | **It travels** — in the form of springs | **It stands** — it is held in the ground |
| What follows | *thumma yukhriju bihī zarʿan* — **a crop comes out** | *innā ʿalā dhahābin bihī la-qādirūn* — **it can be taken away** |
| The emphasis | **The process** — the working of the cycle | **The trust** — the water's being held there |

**The two verbs are the opposite of one another: one to make travel, the other to bring to a stop.** This is a contrast that can be verified from the two texts.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the continuing sentences of the two verses:** in al-Zumar the movement of the water is told, because the proof there is **the crop coming up and then drying** — a cycle. **In al-Muʾminūn the standing of the water is told, because the proof here is that the water is something *held in hand*.** **The same phenomenon, put to two different tasks in two sūras.**

**And the root *salaka* will occur in this sūra too:** in the twenty-seventh verse, *fa'sluk fīhā min kulli zawjayni'thnayn* — "put into it two of every kind, a pair". **The same root: one sends water into the ground, the other living things into the ship.** I give this as an entry about the word and build no connection of meaning between them.

### وَإِنَّا عَلَىٰ ذَهَابٍۭ بِهِۦ لَقَٰدِرُونَ

**The build of the sentence must be recorded, and I give it as my own reading:** the threat one expects would be "We cut off the rain". **The verse does not say this:** *dhahābin bihī* — **"to take it away".** It reports, that is, that **the water that has come down and settled in the ground** can be taken back.

**My ground is the object of the verb:** the pronoun *bihī* refers back to the water settled in the ground in the preceding sentence. **The threat is aimed not at what will not come but at what is already in hand.**

**The same line is drawn at al-Mulk 67/30 as well** (*in aṣbaḥa māʾukum ghawran fa-man yaʾtīkum bi-māʾin maʿīn*) — **treated in `067-mulk.md`.** I rely on that treatment.

**And a limit, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** on the basis of this verse **I build no claim of "foretelling" about underground water tables, aquifer structures or the hydrological cycle.** **What the verse describes is a phenomenon known by observation:** the water that falls does not stay on the surface, it goes down into the ground; wells and springs come out of it; and in drought these recede. **The one addressed needed no special knowledge to know this** — and the force of the argument comes from exactly that. The same entry was made at `077-murselat.md` 77/20-23.

---

## 23/19 — فَأَنشَأْنَا لَكُم بِهِۦ جَنَّٰتٍ مِّن نَّخِيلٍ وَأَعْنَٰبٍ لَّكُمْ فِيهَا فَوَٰكِهُ كَثِيرَةٌ وَمِنْهَا تَأْكُلُونَ

*fa-anshaʾnā lakum bihī jannātin min nakhīlin wa aʿnāb, lakum fīhā fawākihu kathīratun wa minhā taʾkulūn*

"**With it We brought into being for you gardens of date palms and vines. In them you have many fruits, and from them you eat.**"

### أَنشَأْنَا — the second occurrence of the root

**The root was treated at the fourteenth verse.** **What belongs here is its distribution across the sūra, and this is a fact that can be counted from the text:**

| Verse | Sentence | What is built |
|---|---|---|
| **14** | *thumma anshaʾnāhu khalqan ākhar* | **The last step of the human being** |
| **19** | *fa-anshaʾnā lakum bihī jannāt* | **Gardens** |
| **31** | *thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim qarnan ākharīn* | **A generation** |
| **42** | *thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim qurūnan ākharīn* | **Generations** |
| **78** | *wa huwa'lladhī anshaʾa lakumu'l-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾida* | **Hearing, sight, hearts** |

**All five of the five occurrences report not "bringing into being from nothing" but *setting up, standing upright*** — this is the sense of the root worked out in `056-vakia.md`. **And all five stand in separate blocks of the sūra.** I record this as an observation about the structure: **the sūra names the human being, the plant, the generations and the organs with the same verb.**

### نَخِيل وَأَعْنَاب — two plants

**Date palms and vines.** **The two plants are regularly named together in the Qurʾān** — al-Baqara 2/266, al-Anʿām 6/99, al-Raʿd 13/4, al-Naḥl 16/11 and 16/67, al-Isrāʾ 17/91, al-Kahf 18/32, Yā-Sīn 36/34.

**This pair was treated at `036-yasin.md` 36/34.** I rely on that treatment.

**And a detail worth recording:** both are crops that **depend on water in a dry climate**, and both **can be stored** (dried dates, raisins). **Two staple products of the daily life of the one addressed are chosen.**

### فَوَٰكِه — and its distinction from *akl*

The root **ف-ك-ه**: **to take pleasure, to enjoy.** *Fākiha* — fruit; *fukāha* — pleasant talk, jesting. **The root was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/20 and 56/32, and at `080-abese.md` 80/31.**

**And the build of the sentence must be recorded:** *lakum fīhā **fawākihu** kathīratun **wa minhā taʾkulūn*** — two separate things are being said.

**The lexicographers record this distinction:** *fākiha* is what is **eaten for pleasure, not out of necessity**; *akl* is **nourishment.** **The sentence, that is, names first the surplus and then the food itself.**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse counts two needs separately — **what feeds and what pleases.** The same distinction was recorded in `056-vakia.md` for the table of the garden.

---

## 23/20 — وَشَجَرَةً تَخْرُجُ مِن طُورِ سَيْنَآءَ تَنۢبُتُ بِٱلدُّهْنِ وَصِبْغٍ لِّلْءَاكِلِينَ

*wa shajaratan takhruju min ṭūri saynāʾa tanbutu bi'l-duhni wa ṣibghin li'l-ākilīn*

"**And a tree that comes out of Mount Sinai: it brings forth oil, and a relish for those who eat.**"

### The tree is not named

**The verse does not give the name of the tree.** **In the classical exegetical tradition it is widely said to be the olive tree**, and two grounds are given for this:

| Ground | Verse |
|---|---|
| 1 | **al-Nūr 24/35** — *shajaratin mubārakatin **zaytūnatin*** — the blessed tree named for its oil |
| 2 | **al-Tīn 95/1-2** — *wa'l-tīni wa'l-zaytūni · wa **ṭūri sīnīn*** — the olive and Sinai in the same oath |

**This oath was treated in `095-tin.md`**, where it was recorded that Sinai is the mountain on which Moses received revelation, and that the fig and the olive may stand for a region. **I rely on that treatment.**

**I pass on the explanation that the tree is the olive as it is transmitted; since the verse gives no name, I do not present it as a settled ruling.**

### طُورِ سَيْنَآء

**طُور** — **mountain.** The word was treated in `052-tur.md` on the occasion of that sūra's name. **I do not repeat it here.**

**سَيْنَآء** — there is a variant reading in the pronunciation of the word: ***saynāʾ*** and ***sīnāʾ***. **It does not change the meaning;** both readings point to the same place.

**The lexicographers differ over the origin of the word** — whether it is an Arabic adjective (rocky/wooded) or a place name. **I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And an entry on context:** the sūra will name Moses twenty-five verses after this one (45). **The name Sinai occurs, before the narrative, in a passage about a plant.** I record this as an observation and build no claim upon it.

### تَنۢبُتُ بِٱلدُّهْنِ — the variant reading, and the work of the *bāʾ*

**This construction is the most disputed place in the verse.**

**The variant reading:**

| Reading | Pattern | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***tanbutu*** | Form I, intransitive | "**It grows with oil**" — the tree grows together with its oil |
| ***tunbitu*** | Form IV, transitive | "**It brings forth oil**" — the tree produces oil |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

The lexicographers also differ over the work of the ***bāʾ***:

| Reading | The work of the *bāʾ* | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Attachment / accompaniment** (*mulābasa* / *muṣāḥaba*) | "It grows together with its oil" |
| 2 | **Redundant** (required by the parsing, adding no meaning) | "It brings forth its oil" |
| 3 | **Transitivising** (*taʿdiya*) | "It yields oil" |

**All three explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **In all three the outcome is close:** the tree is a tree whose produce is oil.

### صِبْغ — a relish

The root **ص-ب-غ**: **to dye, to dip a thing into a liquid.** *Ṣibgha* — dye, colour (al-Baqara 2/138 — *ṣibghata'llāh*).

**The lexicographers give the meaning of the word here as follows: the thing bread is dipped into, a relish.** **The "dipping" sense of the root, that is, is applied to an act at the table.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the word names the **way olive oil is used** at the table — not the produce but the work done with the produce. **And the qualification *li'l-ākilīn* reinforces this: "for those who eat".**

---

## 23/21-22 — وَإِنَّ لَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَنْعَٰمِ لَعِبْرَةً نُّسْقِيكُم مِّمَّا فِى بُطُونِهَا وَلَكُمْ فِيهَا مَنَٰفِعُ كَثِيرَةٌ وَمِنْهَا تَأْكُلُونَ · وَعَلَيْهَا وَعَلَى ٱلْفُلْكِ تُحْمَلُونَ

*wa inna lakum fi'l-anʿāmi la-ʿibrah, nusqīkum mimmā fī buṭūnihā wa lakum fīhā manāfiʿu kathīratun wa minhā taʾkulūn · wa ʿalayhā wa ʿalā'l-fulki tuḥmalūn*

"**In the grazing animals too there is a lesson for you: We give you to drink of what is in their bellies; in them you have many benefits, and from them you eat. On them and on the ship you are carried.**"

### عِبْرَة — root: ع-ب-ر

**The root was worked out at `079-naziat.md` 79/26 and at `059-hasr.md` 59/2 (*fa'tabirū yā ulī'l-abṣār*).** **I do not repeat it here.** The summary given there:

> **The concrete sense of the root: to pass from one side of a thing to the other.** *ʿUbūr* — crossing over; *maʿbar* — a crossing place; *ʿibāra* — the speech that carries a meaning from one mind to another; *taʿbīr* — the carrying of a dream from image to meaning; *ʿabra* — the tear that spills over from the eye.

**What belongs here is this, and I give it as my own reading:** the picture in the word is **passing from one place to another**. **A "lesson" (*ʿibra*), that is, is not the watching of what is seen — it is the crossing from what is seen to what is not seen.** And the verse shows at once where this crossing runs from and to: **from the drink that comes out of the animal's belly, to the order that brings it out.**

### نُّسْقِيكُم مِّمَّا فِى بُطُونِهَا

**The sentence is worth recording:** the verse does not say "milk". ***Mimmā fī buṭūnihā*** — "of what is in their bellies".

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** instead of a name, **a place** is reported. **And this fits the logic of the word "lesson" itself:** the attention is drawn not to the product but to the place the product comes out of.

**The same phenomenon is told in more detail at al-Naḥl 16/66** (*min bayni farthin wa damin labanan khāliṣan*). **This verse keeps it short.**

The root **س-ق-ي**: **to give to drink.** The pattern is Form IV (*asqā*), and the lexicographers record a nuance between *saqā* and *asqā*: **the first is giving a drink once, the second providing a standing source of water.** I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.

### مَنَٰفِعُ كَثِيرَة

The root **ن-ف-ع**: **benefit.** The word comes in the plural and with an adjective: *manāfiʿu kathīra* — "many benefits". **The verse does not enumerate them.**

**The same expression occurs at al-Naḥl 16/5 (*lakum fīhā difʾun wa manāfiʿu wa minhā taʾkulūn*) and at al-Ḥajj 22/33.**

### وَعَلَيْهَا وَعَلَى ٱلْفُلْكِ تُحْمَلُونَ

**فُلْك** — **the root ف-ل-ك was worked out at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/12.** **I do not repeat it here.** The summary given there: **the core the lexicographers give is roundness and turning;** *falak* — an orbit; *falka* — the round weight at the head of a spindle. **Its use for a ship is explained by way of the ship's hull, or of its going by rocking on the water.**

**And the word order must be recorded:** *ʿalayhā* (on them) and *ʿalā'l-fulk* (on the ship) are joined **by the same particle and by a single verb**: *tuḥmalūn* — "you are carried", passive.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the passive of the verb:** the sentence does not say "you ride" — **"you are carried."** The agent of the verb, that is, is not you. **Land and sea are joined in a single verb of carrying.**

**The same pair was treated at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/12 (*wa jaʿala lakum mina'l-fulki wa'l-anʿāmi mā tarkabūn*).** **The difference here must be recorded:** in al-Zukhruf the verb is ***tarkabūn*** (you ride) — active; **here it is *tuḥmalūn* (you are carried) — passive.**

| Place | Verb | Voice |
|---|---|---|
| `043-zuhruf.md` 43/12 | *tarkabūn* — **you ride** | **Active** — the human being does it |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/22** | *tuḥmalūn* — **you are carried** | **Passive** — it is done to the human being |

**The same phenomenon, in two sūras, in two different voices.** **And this fits the block as a whole:** in everything told since the twelfth verse, the agent of the verb is not the human being.

### The close of the block — block II as a whole

**Twelve to twenty-two is a single series, and its order is worth recording:**

| Verses | Subject | The shared aspect |
|---|---|---|
| 12-16 | **The human being himself** | He had no part in his own forming |
| 17 | **The seven paths above him** | And he has not passed out of being watched over |
| 18 | **Water sent down by measure** | It is not held in his hand |
| 19-20 | **Plants** | Dependent on water |
| 21-22 | **Animals and the ship** | Food and transport |

**The direction of the series must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** it goes from the innermost (the human being's own body) out to the outermost (the heavens) and back in again (what he eats, drinks and rides). **And throughout the series the agent of the verbs is one.** The same method was treated at `056-vakia.md` 56/58-72 — there too the seed, the water and the fire were enumerated in turn and at each the question "did you make it?" was put. **I rely on that treatment.** **I record the difference: al-Wāqiʿa asks a question, al-Muʾminūn gives news.**

---

## 23/23 — وَلَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَا نُوحًا إِلَىٰ قَوْمِهِۦ فَقَالَ يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥٓ أَفَلَا تَتَّقُونَ

*wa laqad arsalnā nūḥan ilā qawmihī fa-qāla yā qawmi'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, a-fa-lā tattaqūn*

"**We sent Noah to his people, assuredly. He said: 'My people, serve God; you have no god other than Him. Will you not guard yourselves?'**"

### The pattern of the sequence — how this section is built

**How a sequence of prophets is built was treated in detail in `037-saffat.md`:** there each narrative ended with the same **seal** of three or four sentences (*wa taraknā ʿalayhi fi'l-ākhirīn*, *salāmun ʿalā…*, *innā kadhālika najzi'l-muḥsinīn*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The sequence of al-Muʾminūn uses a different pattern, and its difference must be recorded:**

| | `037-saffat.md` 37/75-148 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/23-44** |
|---|---|---|
| How many prophets | **Seven** — named | **Noah** by name; the others **unnamed** |
| The repeated element | **The closing seal** — peace and praise | **The opening call** and **the objection of the other side** |
| The emphasis | **Those delivered** | **Those who deny** |
| The end of the sequence | *fa-mattaʿnāhum ilā ḥīn* (148) | *fa-buʿdan li-qawmin lā yuʾminūn* (44) |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the two texts, and I interpret it as my own reading:** the al-Ṣāffāt sequence showed the repetition of **deliverance**; **the al-Muʾminūn sequence shows the repetition of *the objection*.** Indeed the same sentence of objection (*mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum*) will be repeated for three separate peoples.

### يَٰقَوْمِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥ

**The sentence will be repeated word for word in the thirty-second verse** — there in the mouth of a messenger who is not named. **There is not one word of difference between the two calls.**

**And the word order must be recorded:** *mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh* — the negation is reinforced with **the redundant *min***. In Arabic, when *min* enters upon an indefinite noun in a negative sentence it reports **istighrāq** (none at all, never); the lexicographers record this consistently. **Not "you have no other god", that is, but "you have not any other god at all."**

**أَفَلَا تَتَّقُونَ** — "will you not guard yourselves?" **The call ends with a question, not with a threat.**

**And this question will return five times in the sūra, in three different patterns:** *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* (23, 32), *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* (80), *a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn* (85), *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* (87). **All the questions of the sūra are in this pattern.**

---

## 23/24-25 — فَقَالَ ٱلْمَلَؤُا۟ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ مِن قَوْمِهِۦ مَا هَٰذَآ إِلَّا بَشَرٌ مِّثْلُكُمْ يُرِيدُ أَن يَتَفَضَّلَ عَلَيْكُمْ وَلَوْ شَآءَ ٱللَّهُ لَأَنزَلَ مَلَٰٓئِكَةً مَّا سَمِعْنَا بِهَٰذَا فِىٓ ءَابَآئِنَا ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · إِنْ هُوَ إِلَّا رَجُلٌۢ بِهِۦ جِنَّةٌ فَتَرَبَّصُوا۟ بِهِۦ حَتَّىٰ حِينٍ

*fa-qāla'l-malaʾu'lladhīna kafarū min qawmihī mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum yurīdu an yatafaḍḍala ʿalaykum, wa law shāʾa'llāhu la-anzala malāʾikah, mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn · in huwa illā rajulun bihī jinnatun fa-tarabbaṣū bihī ḥattā ḥīn*

"The **notables** of his people who disbelieved said: '**This is nothing but a human being like yourselves; he wants to set himself above you. Had God willed, He would have sent down angels. We never heard of this among our forefathers.** He is nothing but a man possessed; wait and watch him for a time.'"

### ٱلْمَلَأ — root: م-ل-أ

**The concrete sense of the root: to fill.** *Malaʾa'l-INāʾa* — he filled the vessel; *milʾ* — a fill of something.

**And *al-malaʾ* is described by the lexicographers as follows: the leading men of a community, its notables.** **Two explanations are transmitted for the naming:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **They fill the assembly** — those present at the gathering |
| 2 | **They fill the eye** — by their bearing |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the distribution of the word across the sūra must be recorded:** *al-malaʾ* occurs at 24 among Noah's people, at 33 among the next people, at 46 alongside Pharaoh. **Three peoples, the same class.**

**I record this as an observation:** each time it is **not the common people but the leading men** who voice the objection. This can be verified from the text in all three verses.

### مَا هَٰذَآ إِلَّا بَشَرٌ مِّثْلُكُمْ — and its link with al-Furqān 25/7
