**This objection was treated in detail at `025-furkan.md` 25/7.** The sentence there was: *mā li-hādhā'l-rasūli yaʾkulu'l-ṭaʿāma wa yamshī fi'l-aswāq.* **And this was recorded there:**

> **The logic of the objection must be recorded:** the one who is a messenger ought to be free of human needs. **What is expected is that the messenger should possess a visible privilege.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **What belongs here is the comparison of the two sentences:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/7 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/24** |
|---|---|---|
| The pointing | *hādhā*'l-rasūl | *hādhā* — **no name**, only the pointing |
| What is counted a defect | *yaʾkulu'l-ṭaʿāma wa yamshī fi'l-aswāq* — **eating and walking** | *basharun mithlukum* — **sharing the kind** |
| The alternative demanded | *law lā unzila ilayhi malakun* | *law shāʾa'llāhu la-anzala malāʾikah* |
| Additional ground | — | *mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn* — **the forefathers** |
| The second move | 25/8 — **the demand for wealth** | 23/25 — **the charge of madness** |

**The two objections come out of the same place, and this can be verified from the two texts:** in both the sending down of an angel is demanded, and in both the messenger's **humanity** is counted a defect.

**And at `025-furkan.md` 25/20 the answer given to this objection was treated:** *wa mā arsalnā qablaka mina'l-mursalīna illā innahum la-yaʾkulūna'l-ṭaʿāma wa yamshūna fi'l-aswāq* — **the objection is not rejected, it is generalised.**

**The answer of al-Muʾminūn lies elsewhere, and it is one of the most striking structural elements of this sūra:** **the fifty-first verse.** There, addressing all the messengers, it will be said *kulū mina'l-ṭayyibāti wa'ʿmalū ṣāliḥā* — **"eat of the good things and do righteous deeds"**. **The very act that was the subject of the objection (eating), that is, becomes the content of a command.** This will be treated separately at the fifty-first verse.

### يُرِيدُ أَن يَتَفَضَّلَ عَلَيْكُمْ

The root **ف-ض-ل**: **to increase, to be in excess, to be superior.** *Faḍl* — surplus, bounty; *tafaḍḍul* — **to claim superiority, to strive to stand above** (Form V, reflexive).

**And the form of the accusation is worth recording:** the claim is aimed not at **what the messenger says** but at **his intention.** *Yurīdu* — "he wants".

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being that this is the verb used:** the other side is not disputing the content of the call; **it is describing the purpose behind the call.** The same point was recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/4-8: *"not one of the objections looks at what the text says."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the meaning of Form V must be recorded:** the pattern *tafaḍḍul* reports that superiority **is being sought by one's own effort.** **The charge, that is, is not "superior" but "trying to make himself superior".**

### مَّا سَمِعْنَا بِهَٰذَا فِىٓ ءَابَآئِنَا ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ

**The argument from the forefathers.** **And this expression will be turned about in the sixty-eighth verse of the sūra:**

| Verse | Who says it | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **24** | **Noah's people** | *mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī **ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn*** — "we did not hear of it among our forefathers" |
| **68** | **The text** | *am jāʾahum mā lam yaʾti **ābāʾahumu'l-awwalīn*** — "or has something come to them that did not come to their forefathers?" |

**The same construction (*ābāʾ* + *al-awwalīn*), once as a ground and once as a question.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading:** the other side counted **novelty** a defect; the sixty-eighth verse takes the same point and asks **"has anything new come, then?"** **The assumption the objection rests on — that this had not been heard before — is thereby put in question.**

### بِهِۦ جِنَّةٌ

The root **ج-ن-ن**: **to cover, to conceal.** *Janna* — a garden (the trees cover the ground); *jinn* — the unseen; *janīn* — the one covered in the womb; *junna* — a shield; *junūn* — the covering over of the mind. **The root was treated in `054-kamer.md` and in `072-cin.md`.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And this charge will occur once more in the sūra, in the seventieth verse:**

| Verse | Of whom | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **25** | **Noah** | *in huwa illā rajulun **bihī jinnatun*** |
| **70** | **The Prophet addressed** | *am yaqūlūna **bihī jinnatun*** |

**The very same construction (*bihī jinna*), in two different ages.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation: **the sūra answers the charge levelled at those it addresses by showing that it was levelled at Noah as well.** The same method was recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/31 and at `035-fatir.md` 35/4: **the situation is not removed, it is set in a series.**

### فَتَرَبَّصُوا۟ بِهِۦ حَتَّىٰ حِينٍ

The root **ر-ب-ص**: **to wait, to watch, to keep a lookout for something to happen.** *Tarabbuṣ* — Form V: **to wait and watch.**

**And the expression will occur once more in the sūra — but on the other side:**

| Verse | Who says it | Sentence | Of whom |
|---|---|---|---|
| **25** | **Noah's people** | *fa-tarabbaṣū bihī **ḥattā ḥīn*** | **Waiting for Noah** |
| **54** | **The text** | *fa-dharhum fī ghamratihim **ḥattā ḥīn*** | **Leaving them be** |

**The same two words (*ḥattā ḥīn*), in two different mouths.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the exact sameness of the expression:** the other side says "wait a while, it will pass"; **the fifty-fourth verse sets the same span for them.** In neither sentence **is the length of the span stated** — *ḥīn* is an indefinite stretch of time.

---

## 23/26 — قَالَ رَبِّ ٱنصُرْنِى بِمَا كَذَّبُونِ

*qāla rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn*

"**He said: 'My Lord, help me, for they have denied me.'**"

### The brevity of the prayer

**The sentence is three words, and it will be repeated word for word in the thirty-ninth verse of the sūra** — there in the mouth of a messenger who is not named.

**The word order must be recorded: *bimā kadhdhabūn* — the *bāʾ* here reports cause** ("because they denied me"). Another parsing is also transmitted: *mā* is counted *maṣdariyya* and the sense given is "in return for their denial". **Both parsings come out at the same place.**

**And the addressee of the complaint must be recorded: the prayer is directed to the Lord, not to the people.** **The same point was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/30** (*inna qawmi'ttakhadhū hādhā'l-qurʾāna mahjūrā*), and it was recorded there: **while the objections are voiced openly, the answer is directed upward as a complaint.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**One further entry:** the prayer **does not say "destroy them."** *Unṣurnī* — "help me". **What is being asked for is not specified.** I give this as an observation about the word order.

---

## 23/27 — فَأَوْحَيْنَآ إِلَيْهِ أَنِ ٱصْنَعِ ٱلْفُلْكَ بِأَعْيُنِنَا وَوَحْيِنَا فَإِذَا جَآءَ أَمْرُنَا وَفَارَ ٱلتَّنُّورُ فَٱسْلُكْ فِيهَا مِن كُلٍّ زَوْجَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ وَأَهْلَكَ إِلَّا مَن سَبَقَ عَلَيْهِ ٱلْقَوْلُ مِنْهُمْ وَلَا تُخَٰطِبْنِى فِى ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوٓا۟ إِنَّهُم مُّغْرَقُونَ

*fa-awḥaynā ilayhi ani'ṣnaʿi'l-fulka bi-aʿyuninā wa waḥyinā, fa-idhā jāʾa amrunā wa fāra'l-tannūru fa'sluk fīhā min kullin zawjayni'thnayni wa ahlaka illā man sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawlu minhum, wa lā tukhāṭibnī fi'lladhīna ẓalamū, innahum mughraqūn*

"**We revealed to him: 'Build the ship under Our eyes and by Our revelation. When Our command comes and the oven boils over, put into it two of every kind, and your family — except those of them against whom the word has already gone forth. And do not address Me concerning those who have done wrong; they are to be drowned.'**"

### بِأَعْيُنِنَا — "under Our eyes"

**The construction was treated at `054-kamer.md` 54/14 (*tajrī bi-aʿyuninā*)** and occurred at `052-tur.md` 52/48 (*fa-innaka bi-aʿyuninā*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is the difference between the two verses, and it must be recorded:**

| Place | Sentence | What *bi-aʿyuninā* attaches to |
|---|---|---|
| `054-kamer.md` 54/14 | *tajrī bi-aʿyuninā* | **The ship's running** — while it is on the water |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/27** | *iṣnaʿi'l-fulka bi-aʿyuninā* | **The ship's making** — while it is being built |

**The same construction, once for the voyage and once for the building.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

**And *bi-aʿyuninā* + *wa waḥyinā* must be recorded together:** two things are given at once — **oversight** and **instruction.** **The ship, that is, is built both under watch and by description.** I give this as an observation about the word order.

**An entry, per the method (`STYLE.md`):** no modality is produced about the construction *aʿyun* (eyes). The lexicographers and the exegetes generally explain the construction as **watching over and protection**; I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted and go no further.

### وَفَارَ ٱلتَّنُّورُ

The root **ف-و-ر**: **to boil, to gush, to overflow.** *Fāra'l-qidr* — the pot boiled; *fawr* — at once, the moment it comes to the boil (*min fawrihim* — Āl ʿImrān 3/125).

**تَنُّور** — **an oven, a baking pit.** **The word was cited in this connection in `067-mulk.md`** (Hūd 11/40 — *wa fāra'l-tannūr*). **The word occurs in only two places in the Qurʾān: Hūd 11/40 and this verse.**

**The exegetes differ over what the expression "the oven boiled over" reports:**

| Reading | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | **An oven in the literal sense** — the gushing of the water began there; a sign |
| 2 | **The earth** — the word is used figuratively for every part of the earth |
| 3 | **A particular place** — place names are transmitted |
| 4 | **An idiom** — an expression like "things came to the boil" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **And I do not repeat the place names that are transmitted, since I am not certain of their source.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`), I do not add to the narrative a detail the Qurʾān does not give.**

### فَٱسْلُكْ فِيهَا مِن كُلٍّ زَوْجَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ

**The root س-ل-ك was cited at the eighteenth verse: to send into a path, to make pass.** **The same root is used there for making water pass into the ground, here for putting living things into the ship.**

**A variant reading must be recorded:** the readings ***min kullin zawjayni'thnayn*** (with tanwīn) and ***min kulli zawjayni'thnayn*** (as a genitive construction) are transmitted.

| Reading | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *min **kullin** zawjayni'thnayn* | "**Of everything**, two of a pair" |
| *min **kulli** zawjayni'thnayn* | "**Of every pair**, two" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The outcome is close in both.**

The word **زَوْج** must be recorded: in Arabic *zawj* is **one member of a pair** — not both together, as English "pair" is. **For this reason the construction *zawjayni'thnayn* means "two spouses, that is, two of them".** The lexicographers record this nicety consistently.

### وَأَهْلَكَ إِلَّا مَن سَبَقَ عَلَيْهِ ٱلْقَوْلُ مِنْهُمْ

**The exception must be recorded:** part of the family is left outside. **And the ground for being left outside is not a kinship but *a word that has already gone forth*:** *sabaqa ʿalayhi'l-qawl.*

**I record this as an observation:** in the language of the verse, deliverance is settled **not by lineage** but **by a decree.** **Who is meant is not said here;** at Hūd 11/42-46 a son is spoken of. **This verse itself gives no name, and I add none.**

### وَلَا تُخَٰطِبْنِى فِى ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟

The root **خ-ط-ب**: **to speak, to address.** *Khiṭāb* — speech exchanged; *khuṭba* — an address; *khiṭba* — a proposal of marriage (the giving of one's word).

**The scope of the prohibition is worth recording:** *lā tukhāṭibnī* — **"do not address Me"**, that is, do not raise the matter. **The verse does not say "do not pray" or "do not ask"; it forbids the addressing itself.**

**I give this as an observation about the word order and go no further.**

---

## 23/28-29 — فَإِذَا ٱسْتَوَيْتَ أَنتَ وَمَن مَّعَكَ عَلَى ٱلْفُلْكِ فَقُلِ ٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ ٱلَّذِى نَجَّىٰنَا مِنَ ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ · وَقُل رَّبِّ أَنزِلْنِى مُنزَلًا مُّبَارَكًا وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلْمُنزِلِينَ

*fa-idhā'stawayta anta wa man maʿaka ʿalā'l-fulki fa-quli'l-ḥamdu li'llāhi'lladhī najjānā mina'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn · wa qul rabbi anzilnī munzalan mubārakan wa anta khayru'l-munzilīn*

"**When you and those with you are settled on the ship, say: 'Praise belongs to God, who delivered us from the wrongdoing people.' And say: 'My Lord, bring me down at a blessed landing; You are the best of those who bring down.'**"

### Two prayers, two directions

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the two verses:** one looks **backward**, the other **forward**.

| Verse | Prayer | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **28** | *al-ḥamdu li'llāhi'lladhī **najjānā*** — perfect | **What is left behind** — the deliverance |
| **29** | *rabbi **anzilnī** munzalan mubārakā* — imperative | **What lies ahead** — the arrival |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the tense of the two verbs:** at the moment of boarding, something is said both about **the place departed from** and about **the place still to be reached.** **And both prayers are commanded in the middle of the voyage, when nothing has yet ended.**

**And an entry on context: the second prayer is for the *landing*.** The ship has not yet moved; **but the prayer already speaks of coming down.**

### مُنزَلًا — the variant reading and the parsing

**There is a variant reading in the pronunciation of the word, and it bears on the meaning:**

| Reading | Pattern | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***munzalan*** | From Form IV, **a passive participle or a *mīmī* verbal noun** | "**The place of being brought down**" or "**the act of being brought down**" |
| ***manzilan*** | From Form I, **a noun of place** | "**A lodging, a place to alight**" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **On both, the prayer asks for the same thing:** that the place reached be blessed.

**مُّبَارَكًا** — **the root ب-ر-ك was cited at the fourteenth verse;** it was worked out at `067-mulk.md` 67/1. **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the construction is worth recording:** what is asked for is not a **large**, a **spacious** or a **safe** place — but a **blessed** one. The core of the root is **lasting and increase.** **The prayer, that is, asks not for the quality of the place but for the continuance of what will happen there.** I give this as my own reading.

**وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلْمُنزِلِين** — **the second of the five superlative constructions in the sūra** (14, 29, 72, 109, 118). **And the word order must be recorded: the construction is *inside* the prayer, not a separate sentence.** The one who asks, that is, names the source of what he asks for even as he asks.

---

## 23/30 — إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَٰتٍ وَإِن كُنَّا لَمُبْتَلِينَ

*inna fī dhālika la-āyātin wa in kunnā la-mubtalīn*

"**In this there are surely signs. And We were surely putting to the test.**"

### لَءَايَٰتٍ — the plural

**The word order must be recorded:** *āyāt* is **plural.** In the Qurʾān this pattern most often comes in the singular (*inna fī dhālika la-āyatan*). **Here it comes in the plural.**

**I record this as an observation:** the narrative told is not a single event — **the call, the objection, the ship, the deliverance, the drowning.** The plural fits the number of steps in the narrative.

### وَإِن كُنَّا لَمُبْتَلِينَ

**The lexicographers dwell on the word order:** *in* here is **the lightened form of the heavy *inna*** (*mukhaffafa mina'l-thaqīla*), and the *lām* is **the distinguishing *lām*** that marks it off. **The sentence, that is, is not a negation but an emphatic assertion:** "We were surely putting to the test."

The root **ب-ل-و / ب-ل-ي**: **to try, to test; and to wear out, to wear down.** *Ibtilāʾ* — testing. **The root was treated at `067-mulk.md` 67/2 (*li-yabluwakum ayyukum aḥsanu ʿamalā*) and at `029-ankebut.md` 29/2-3.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the place of the sentence must be recorded:** at the close of the narrative comes the word **testing**. **What is told, that is, is sealed not as a report of destruction but as an instance of a testing.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this position of the sentence.** **And the same frame was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/20 (*wa jaʿalnā baʿḍakum li-baʿḍin fitnatan a-taṣbirūn*)** — where it was recorded that the testing comes **by the hand of other people.** **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 23/31-32 — ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَا مِنۢ بَعْدِهِمْ قَرْنًا ءَاخَرِينَ · فَأَرْسَلْنَا فِيهِمْ رَسُولًا مِّنْهُمْ أَنِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مَا لَكُم مِّنْ إِلَٰهٍ غَيْرُهُۥٓ أَفَلَا تَتَّقُونَ

*thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim qarnan ākharīn · fa-arsalnā fīhim rasūlan minhum ani'budū'llāha mā lakum min ilāhin ghayruh, a-fa-lā tattaqūn*

"**Then after them We brought into being another generation. And We sent among them a messenger from among themselves: 'Serve God; you have no god other than Him. Will you not guard yourselves?'**"

### قَرْن — root: ق-ر-ن

**The concrete sense of the root: to bring two things together, to join them.** *Qarana* — he joined; *qarīn* — companion, consort; *qurūn* — horns (the two that stand side by side on the head).

**And *qarn* is recorded by the lexicographers in two senses:**

| Sense | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | **A community living at the same time** — from their being together |
| 2 | **A stretch of time** — the spans transmitted differ (forty, sixty, a hundred years) |

**I do not repeat the figures given for the span, since I am not certain of their sources.** **The usage here is in the first sense** — indeed *ākharīn* (a plural adjective) comes for a community.

**A detail of word order must be recorded:** *qarnan* is singular, its adjective *ākharīn* plural. **The lexicographers record this and explain it by *qarn* being plural in sense (a community).**

### The people is not named

**And this is a fact that must be recorded:** the sūra **does not say** the name of this people. Noah was named; from here on the sequence runs without names.

**The exegetes differ over who this people is:**

| View | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **ʿĀd** — it is transmitted that they came immediately after Noah |
| 2 | **Thamūd** — the punishment of the *ṣayḥa* (the cry) in the forty-first verse is named for Thamūd as well |
| 3 | **It has been left indefinite** — the withholding of the name is deliberate |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **And the third view has a ground in the text, which I record:** the forty-second verse says *qurūnan ākharīn* (plural), carrying the sequence on with an unspecified number of peoples. **The sūra, that is, is telling not individual peoples but a repeating pattern.**

### أَنشَأْنَا — the third and fourth occurrences of the root

**It was tabulated at the nineteenth verse.** **What belongs here is the one-word difference between the thirty-first verse and the forty-second:**

| Verse | Sentence | The difference |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim **qarnan** ākharīn* | **Singular** — one generation |
| **42** | *thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim **qurūnan** ākharīn* | **Plural** — generations |

**The rest of the sentences is word for word the same.** This is a fact that can be verified from the text.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sequence is first set out in detail with **one** people (31-41), and then shortened by being **put in the plural** (42-44). **The telling, that is, moves from the example to the rule.**

### رَسُولًا مِّنْهُمْ

**The qualification must be recorded: *minhum* — "from among themselves".** The messenger does not come from outside. **And this is exactly the point on which the objection in the twenty-fourth verse rests (*basharun mithlukum*).**

**The text, that is, itself sets down as a qualification the very matter the objection is about.** I give this as an observation. **The same line was treated at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/9 (*mā kuntu bidʿan mina'l-rusul*).**

**And the sentence of the call is word for word the same as the twenty-third verse** — the only difference being that Noah's vocative *yā qawmi* is absent here.

---

## 23/33-34 — وَقَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ مِن قَوْمِهِ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ وَكَذَّبُوا۟ بِلِقَآءِ ٱلْءَاخِرَةِ وَأَتْرَفْنَٰهُمْ فِى ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا مَا هَٰذَآ إِلَّا بَشَرٌ مِّثْلُكُمْ يَأْكُلُ مِمَّا تَأْكُلُونَ مِنْهُ وَيَشْرَبُ مِمَّا تَشْرَبُونَ · وَلَئِنْ أَطَعْتُم بَشَرًا مِّثْلَكُمْ إِنَّكُمْ إِذًا لَّخَٰسِرُونَ

*wa qāla'l-malaʾu min qawmihi'lladhīna kafarū wa kadhdhabū bi-liqāʾi'l-ākhirati wa atrafnāhum fi'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum yaʾkulu mimmā taʾkulūna minhu wa yashrabu mimmā tashrabūn · wa la-in aṭaʿtum basharan mithlakum innakum idhan la-khāsirūn*

"The notables of his people who disbelieved, who denied the meeting of the hereafter, and **on whom We had bestowed prosperity in the life of this world**, said: '**This is nothing but a human being like yourselves: he eats of what you eat and drinks of what you drink. If you obey a human being like yourselves, then you will surely be the losers.**'"

### The three conditions of the objection — how the sentence is built

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse:** the speaking party is introduced by **three qualifications**:

| Qualification | Expression | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *kafarū* | **Disbelief** |
| 2 | *kadhdhabū bi-liqāʾi'l-ākhira* | **Denying the hereafter** |
| 3 | ***atrafnāhum** fi'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā* | **Having been given prosperity** |

**The third stands apart from the others, and this is a fact that must be recorded:** the agent of the first two qualifications is they themselves (*kafarū*, *kadhdhabū*); **the agent of the third is the text** — *atrafnāhum*, "We gave them prosperity".

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this change of voice in the verb:** in describing the condition of the objectors, **what they themselves did** and **what was done to them** are set side by side. **And prosperity is counted not as a crime but as an attribute.**

The root **ت-ر-ف**: **to live in abundance, to grow giddy with plenty.** *Tarafa* — he lived in abundance; *mutraf* — spoiled by prosperity. **The root was treated at `034-sebe.md` 34/34 and at `056-vakia.md` 56/45 (*innahum kānū qabla dhālika mutrafīn*).** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the root will come back once more in the sūra:** in the sixty-fourth verse, *ḥattā idhā akhadhnā **mutrafīhim** bi'l-ʿadhāb.*

| Verse | Sentence | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **33** | *wa **atrafnāhum** fi'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā* | **It is given** |
| **64** | *akhadhnā **mutrafīhim** bi'l-ʿadhāb* | **It is taken** |

**The same root, once in the giving and once in the taking.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### يَأْكُلُ مِمَّا تَأْكُلُونَ مِنْهُ وَيَشْرَبُ مِمَّا تَشْرَبُونَ

**The objection goes one step further than the one in the twenty-fourth verse, and this must be recorded:**

| Verse | Objection | How concrete |
|---|---|---|
| **24** | *basharun mithlukum* + *yurīdu an yatafaḍḍala* | **Sharing the kind, and intention** |
| **33** | *basharun mithlukum* + ***yaʾkulu … wa yashrab*** | **Two everyday acts** |
| **47** | *a-nuʾminu li-basharayni mithlinā* + *wa qawmuhumā lanā ʿābidūn* | **Social standing** |

**Three objections, three separate peoples, the same core: *basharun mithlukum*.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**And the two verbs of the thirty-third verse — eating and drinking — are in exactly the same field as the objection at `025-furkan.md` 25/7** (*yaʾkulu'l-ṭaʿām*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the answer to this objection will come in the fifty-first verse:** *yā ayyuha'l-rusulu **kulū** mina'l-ṭayyibāt.* **The act of eating, that is, having been the subject of the objection, becomes the subject of a command.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be treated separately at the fifty-first verse.

### وَلَئِنْ أَطَعْتُم بَشَرًا مِّثْلَكُمْ إِنَّكُمْ إِذًا لَّخَٰسِرُونَ

**The word order must be recorded:** the sentence begins with **the *lām* of the oath** (*la-in*), and **its apodosis** is reinforced with *innakum … la-khāsirūn*. **The other side, that is, speaks in the pattern of an oath.**

The root **خ-س-ر**: **to diminish, to lose; to give short measure.** **The root was treated at `103-asr.md` 103/2 (*inna'l-insāna la-fī khusr*) and in `083-mutaffifin.md`.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the fate of the word must be recorded:** the same root will return in the hundred and third verse of the sūra — *ulāʾika'lladhīna **khasirū** anfusahum.* **The other side says that those who obey will come to loss; the sūra takes the word and turns it back on them.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation.

---

## 23/35-38 — أَيَعِدُكُمْ أَنَّكُمْ إِذَا مِتُّمْ وَكُنتُمْ تُرَابًا وَعِظَٰمًا أَنَّكُم مُّخْرَجُونَ · هَيْهَاتَ هَيْهَاتَ لِمَا تُوعَدُونَ · إِنْ هِىَ إِلَّا حَيَاتُنَا ٱلدُّنْيَا نَمُوتُ وَنَحْيَا وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمَبْعُوثِينَ · إِنْ هُوَ إِلَّا رَجُلٌ ٱفْتَرَىٰ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ كَذِبًا وَمَا نَحْنُ لَهُۥ بِمُؤْمِنِينَ

*a-yaʿidukum annakum idhā mittum wa kuntum turāban wa ʿiẓāman annakum mukhrajūn · hayhāta hayhāta limā tūʿadūn · in hiya illā ḥayātuna'l-dunyā namūtu wa naḥyā wa mā naḥnu bi-mabʿūthīn · in huwa illā rajulun'ftarā ʿalā'llāhi kadhiban wa mā naḥnu lahū bi-muʾminīn*

"'**Does he promise you that when you have died and become dust and bones you will be brought out? Far, far off is what you are promised! There is no life but our life in this world: we die and we live, and we are not to be raised. He is nothing but a man who has invented a lie against God, and we are not going to believe him.**'"

### هَيْهَاتَ — a noun of verbal force

**The word is not a noun but an *ism fiʿl*** — a word that carries the meaning of a verb but is not inflected like a verb. **Its meaning: "how far off!"**

**A variant reading is transmitted in the vowel at the end of the word** (*hayhāta* / *hayhāti* / *hayhātu*); **it does not change the meaning.**

**And the word order must be recorded: the word is repeated twice.** In Arabic such repetition reports **emphasis**. **What is rejected, that is, is counted not far but "very far".**

**I record this as an observation:** the other side does not say that the resurrection is **impossible** — it says that it is **far off.** **And the particle *lām* (*limā tūʿadūn*) must be recorded:** the lexicographers record that *limā* here does not mean "for", but shows to whom the distance belongs.

### نَمُوتُ وَنَحْيَا

**The lexicographers differ over the sentence, and the disagreement changes the meaning:**

| Reading | Meaning | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "**Some of us die, some of us live**" — the continuing of the generations | The next sentence rejecting the resurrection |
| 2 | "**We die and (after us, in our children) we live**" | The same |
| 3 | "**We live and we die**" — the *wāw* does not report order | The *wāw* in Arabic reporting simple conjunction |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **What is common to all three is this:** the sentence **confines life to this world.**

**And the same objection will return word for word in the eighty-second verse of the sūra:** *a-idhā mitnā wa kunnā turāban wa ʿiẓāman a-innā la-mabʿūthūn.* **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and the eighty-first verse will name it openly:** *bal qālū mithla mā qāla'l-awwalūn* — **"they said the same as those before them said."**

**The sūra, that is, repeats in the eighty-second verse, in the mouths of those it addresses, the sentence it placed in the mouth of a people in the thirty-fifth, and it records this as a finding.** This weave can be verified from the wording of the two verses.

### ٱفْتَرَىٰ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ كَذِبًا

The root **ف-ر-ي**: **to split, to cut a hide; and to invent.** *Iftirāʾ* — Form VIII: **to invent, to fasten upon someone.** **The root was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/4 (*in hādhā illā ifkun'ftarāh*).**

**And its difference from the counterpart charge in al-Furqān must be recorded:**

| Place | Charge | What it looks at |
|---|---|---|
| `025-furkan.md` 25/4 | *ifkun'ftarāhu wa **aʿānahū ʿalayhi qawmun ākharūn*** | **He had help** — the source |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/38** | *rajulun'ftarā **ʿalā'llāhi** kadhiban* | **He ascribed it to God** — the fastening |

**Two charges from the same root, in two different directions.**

---

## 23/39-41 — قَالَ رَبِّ ٱنصُرْنِى بِمَا كَذَّبُونِ · قَالَ عَمَّا قَلِيلٍ لَّيُصْبِحُنَّ نَٰدِمِينَ · فَأَخَذَتْهُمُ ٱلصَّيْحَةُ بِٱلْحَقِّ فَجَعَلْنَٰهُمْ غُثَآءً فَبُعْدًا لِّلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

*qāla rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn · qāla ʿammā qalīlin la-yuṣbiḥunna nādimīn · fa-akhadhathumu'l-ṣayḥatu bi'l-ḥaqqi fa-jaʿalnāhum ghuthāʾan, fa-buʿdan li'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn*

"**He said: 'My Lord, help me, for they have denied me.' He said: 'In a little while they will be full of regret.' Then the cry seized them in truth, and We made them scum. Away with the wrongdoing people!**"

### رَبِّ ٱنصُرْنِى بِمَا كَذَّبُونِ — a word-for-word repetition

**It is the same as the twenty-sixth verse without the difference of a single letter.** Two messengers, two peoples, the same prayer. **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And the answer the two prayers receive is different, and this must be recorded:**

| Verse | Prayer | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| **26** | *rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn* | **27** — *fa-awḥaynā ilayhi ani'ṣnaʿi'l-fulka* — **a task is given** |
| **39** | *rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn* (the same) | **40** — *ʿammā qalīlin la-yuṣbiḥunna nādimīn* — **a piece of news is given** |

**The same prayer, two different responses.** I record this as an observation: in the first a preparation is asked of the messenger; **in the second nothing is asked of him.**

### عَمَّا قَلِيلٍ

**The construction: *ʿan* + *mā* (redundant) + *qalīl*.** The lexicographers record that the *mā* here is **redundant and reinforcing**. The sense: **"after a little while."**

**نَادِمِين** — the root **ن-د-م**: **regret.** The lexicographers also record the branch of the root meaning "to come after a thing" (*nadīm* — a table companion).

**And the form of the news is worth recording:** what is reported is not the punishment itself but **the state of the other side** — *nādimīn.* **What is promised, that is, is not a punishment but a regret.**

### ٱلصَّيْحَة

The root **ص-ي-ح**: **to cry out loudly.** *Ṣayḥa* — a cry, a din. **The root was treated at `036-yasin.md` 36/29, 36/49 and 36/53, and in `054-kamer.md`.**

**بِٱلْحَقِّ** — this must be recorded: the seizing is qualified **with truth**. **The punishment, that is, is named not as an arbitrary display of power but as a deserved outcome.**

### غُثَآء

The root **غ-ث-و / غ-ث-ي**: **the debris a torrent carries down, the frothy refuse that gathers on the water.**

**The word was worked out at `087-ala.md` 87/5 (*fa-jaʿalahū ghuthāʾan aḥwā*)** — **and this very verse was cited there as the word's other occurrence.** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the difference between the two verses:**

| Place | What *ghuthāʾ* is | The process |
|---|---|---|
| `087-ala.md` 87/5 | **Pasture** — greening and then drying | **A natural cycle** — its adjective *aḥwā* (blackened) |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/41** | **A people** | **A punishment** — with no adjective |

**The same word, once for a plant and once for a community.** And at `039-zumer.md` 39/21 and `056-vakia.md` 56/65 the same idea was given with a different word (*ḥuṭām* — crumbled fragments).

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the picture in the word:** *ghuthāʾ* is not a thing that **has ceased to exist** — it is a thing **that stands on the water but is not held.** The same finding recorded for *habāʾ* (dust) at `025-furkan.md` 25/23 holds here too: **not annihilation, but not remaining in hand.**

### فَبُعْدًا لِّلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

**The pattern: an absolute object with its verb elided.** *Buʿdan* — "(let there be) distance". **In Arabic it is a pattern of imprecation.**

**And the pattern will come once more in the forty-fourth verse, with a difference of one word:**

| Verse | Sentence | Of whom |
|---|---|---|
| **41** | *fa-buʿdan li'l-qawmi'**l-ẓālimīn*** | **The wrongdoers** — definite, that people |
| **44** | *fa-buʿdan li-qawmin **lā yuʾminūn*** | **A people who do not believe** — indefinite, general |

**Two differences must be recorded: the first is definite (*al-qawm*), the second indefinite (*qawmin*); the first is qualified by wrongdoing, the second by not believing.** **At the end of the sequence, that is, the verdict passes from a people to an attribute.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text, and **it is what the principle recorded in `STYLE.md` — that a verdict is attached to an attribute and not to a group — comes to in this sūra.**

---

## 23/42-44 — ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَا مِنۢ بَعْدِهِمْ قُرُونًا ءَاخَرِينَ · مَا تَسْبِقُ مِنْ أُمَّةٍ أَجَلَهَا وَمَا يَسْتَـْٔخِرُونَ · ثُمَّ أَرْسَلْنَا رُسُلَنَا تَتْرَا كُلَّ مَا جَآءَ أُمَّةً رَّسُولُهَا كَذَّبُوهُ فَأَتْبَعْنَا بَعْضَهُم بَعْضًا وَجَعَلْنَٰهُمْ أَحَادِيثَ فَبُعْدًا لِّقَوْمٍ لَّا يُؤْمِنُونَ

*thumma anshaʾnā min baʿdihim qurūnan ākharīn · mā tasbiqu min ummatin ajalahā wa mā yastaʾkhirūn · thumma arsalnā rusulanā tatrā, kullamā jāʾa ummatan rasūluhā kadhdhabūh, fa-atbaʿnā baʿḍahum baʿḍan wa jaʿalnāhum aḥādīth, fa-buʿdan li-qawmin lā yuʾminūn*

"**Then after them We brought into being other generations. No community outruns its term, nor can they put it back. Then We sent Our messengers one after another. Whenever its messenger came to a community, they denied him. So We made them follow one after another, and We made them tales. Away with a people who do not believe!**"

### تَتْرَا — root: و-ت-ر

**The original of the word is *watrā*; the initial *wāw* has turned into a *tāʾ*** — the lexicographers record this change consistently.

**The concrete sense of the root: *witr* — odd, not even.** And from this arises the sense of **coming one after another.**

**And the lexicographers' principal entry here is this:** *tatrā* means **to come in succession but not adjoining.** *Muwātara* — coming after another, **with an interval in between.** **The word, that is, carries both continuity and interval at once.**

**I give this as an entry about the language and rely on the lexicographers' description.** **And the choice of this word in this verse is worth recording:** the messengers are named not as **an unbroken chain** but as **a series repeated at intervals.**

**A variant reading:** ***tatrā*** (with alif, a diptote) and ***tatran*** (with tanwīn). **It does not change the meaning.**

### كُلَّ مَا جَآءَ أُمَّةً رَّسُولُهَا كَذَّبُوهُ

**The word order must be recorded:** *kullamā* — "whenever". **The conditional particle reports that there is no exception.**

**And the agreement of the pronoun must be recorded:** *ummatan* is singular, *kadhdhabūhu* plural. **The lexicographers explain this by *umma* being plural in sense (a community).**

### وَجَعَلْنَٰهُمْ أَحَادِيثَ

**This construction was worked out at `034-sebe.md` 34/19.** **I do not repeat it here.** A summary of what was recorded there:

> The original sense of the root **ح-د-ث** is "to be new, to come about afterwards". *Aḥādīth* means **the things that go on being told.** **And the build of the sentence is this: a community is turned into a narration.** At the core of the root lies being new; **a people's turning into a "ḥadīth" is its losing its actual existence and becoming something reconstituted afresh at each telling.**

**And the following finding recorded in `034-sebe.md` attaches directly here:** *"the measure of a community is, in the end, what is told about it. The verse names this as a punishment."*

**What belongs here is the difference of setting between the two verses:**

| | `034-sebe.md` 34/19 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/44** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | **Sabaʾ** — a single named people | **A sequence of unnamed peoples** |
| The cause | *bāʿid bayna asfārinā* — **their own request** | *kadhdhabūhu* — **denying the messenger** |
| The punishment beside it | *mazzaqnāhum kulla mumazzaq* — **being torn apart** | *fa-atbaʿnā baʿḍahum baʿḍan* — **being set in a row** |

**The same punishment, in two different frames.**

### فَأَتْبَعْنَا بَعْضَهُم بَعْضًا

The root **ت-ب-ع**: **to go after, to follow.** Form IV (*atbaʿa*): **to make follow after.**

**And the word order is worth recording:** *tatrā* (the messengers one after another) and *atbaʿnā baʿḍahum baʿḍan* (the peoples one after another) give **the same idea with two different roots.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being that the two expressions stand in the same verse:** the verse builds **two parallel series** — **the series of messengers arriving** and **the series of peoples departing.** And the two stand side by side in one sentence.

### The block as a whole — what block III shows

| Verses | Who | What happened |
|---|---|---|
| 23-30 | **Noah** — by name | The call, the objection, the ship, the deliverance |
| 31-41 | **An unnamed people** | The same call, the same objection, the cry |
| 42-43 | **Unnamed generations** | The rule of the appointed term is set down |
| 44 | **All the messengers** | The sequence is gathered into one sentence |

**The narrowing direction of the block must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** the telling goes **from detail to summary** — an eight-verse narrative, then an eleven-verse narrative, then a two-verse rule, then a one-verse gathering. **And the gathering sentence is attached to an attribute:** *li-qawmin lā yuʾminūn.*

---

## 23/45-46 — ثُمَّ أَرْسَلْنَا مُوسَىٰ وَأَخَاهُ هَٰرُونَ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَسُلْطَٰنٍ مُّبِينٍ · إِلَىٰ فِرْعَوْنَ وَمَلَإِي۟هِۦ فَٱسْتَكْبَرُوا۟ وَكَانُوا۟ قَوْمًا عَالِينَ

*thumma arsalnā mūsā wa akhāhu hārūna bi-āyātinā wa sulṭānin mubīn · ilā firʿawna wa malaʾihī fa'stakbarū wa kānū qawman ʿālīn*

"**Then We sent Moses and his brother Aaron (Hārūn) with Our signs and a clear warrant: to Pharaoh and his notables. They grew arrogant; they were a people who held themselves high.**"

### وَأَخَاهُ هَٰرُونَ

**The word order must be recorded: Aaron is named not by his name first but by the description *brother*** — *akhāhu hārūn*.

**I give this as an observation:** in this sūra most of the prophets are unnamed; **Aaron's name is given, but a relation is placed in front of it.** And this relation will find its counterpart in the language of the objection in the forty-seventh verse: *a-nuʾminu li-**basharayni** mithlinā* — **in the dual.** The other side addresses the two of them together.

### سُلْطَٰنٍ مُّبِين

The root **س-ل-ط**: **to get on top of a thing, to have mastery, to hold power.** *Sulṭān* — it means both **power** and **proof**; the lexicographers explain the second sense as "the argument that silences its opponent and prevails".

**And *āyāt* and *sulṭān* must be recorded together:** two separate things are named. **The lexicographers explain this in two ways:**

| Reading | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Āyāt* are the miracles, and *sulṭān* is the **force of proof** they carry — the conjunction is explicative |
| 2 | They are two separate things: the miracles, and **a clear argument** |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### وَكَانُوا۟ قَوْمًا عَالِينَ

The root **ع-ل-و**: **to rise, to be above.**

**And the word order must be recorded: the sentence is built with *kānū* (a perfect, reporting continuity).** The arrogance, that is, is not a reaction that began with the coming of the messengers — **it is a state that existed beforehand.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the two verbs:** first *fa'stakbarū* (they grew arrogant — the reaction to the call), then *wa kānū qawman ʿālīn* (they were already a people who held themselves high — the prior state). **After telling the reaction, the sentence lays down the ground of the reaction.**

**And the root will return twice more in the sūra:**

| Verse | Word | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **46** | *qawman **ʿālīn*** | **Pharaoh's company** |
| **91** | *la-**ʿalā** baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ* | **The supposed gods** — prevailing over one another |
| **116** | *fa-**taʿāla**'llāhu'l-maliku'l-ḥaqq* | **God** |

**The same root, with three different subjects.** This is a distribution that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation: **the verb of rising shifts place across the sūra and is at last attached to a single place.**

---

## 23/47-49 — فَقَالُوٓا۟ أَنُؤْمِنُ لِبَشَرَيْنِ مِثْلِنَا وَقَوْمُهُمَا لَنَا عَٰبِدُونَ · فَكَذَّبُوهُمَا فَكَانُوا۟ مِنَ ٱلْمُهْلَكِينَ · وَلَقَدْ ءَاتَيْنَا مُوسَى ٱلْكِتَٰبَ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَهْتَدُونَ

*fa-qālū a-nuʾminu li-basharayni mithlinā wa qawmuhumā lanā ʿābidūn · fa-kadhdhabūhumā fa-kānū mina'l-muhlakīn · wa laqad ātaynā mūsā'l-kitāba laʿallahum yahtadūn*

"They said: '**Are we to believe two human beings like ourselves, while their people are servants to us?**' They denied them, and became of those destroyed. **We gave Moses the Book, assuredly — that they might be guided.**"

### أَنُؤْمِنُ لِبَشَرَيْنِ مِثْلِنَا — the third objection

**This is the third *basharun mithlukum* objection in the sūra**, and it is set apart from the earlier ones by one difference: **a social ground is added here.**

| Verse | Objection | The ground added |
|---|---|---|
| **24** | *mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum* | *yurīdu an yatafaḍḍala ʿalaykum* — **intention** |
| **33** | *mā hādhā illā basharun mithlukum* | *yaʾkulu … wa yashrab* — **everyday acts** |
| **47** | *a-nuʾminu li-basharayni mithlinā* | ***wa qawmuhumā lanā ʿābidūn*** — **standing** |

**The third is worth recording:** here the objection rests not on the kind but on **the social order of rank.**

**And the particle must be recorded: *nuʾminu li-basharayn*.** In Arabic there is a nuance between *āmana bi-* (to believe in a thing) and *āmana li-* (to trust someone, to accept his word), and the lexicographers record it. **The particle here is *lām*: what is at issue is accepting the word of two men.**

### وَقَوْمُهُمَا لَنَا عَٰبِدُونَ

**The root ع-ب-د has a particular feature here, and it must be recorded.**

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: *ṭarīqun muʿabbad* — a road levelled and made smooth by being trodden.** From this comes the sense of **submitting, serving.**

**And the word is used here not for a service directed to God but for one directed to human beings:** *lanā ʿābidūn.* **The word, that is, is not confined to the sense of worship; it is used for service and subordination as well.** The lexicographers record this.

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the other side builds its own standing by looking at **who serves whom.** **And this stands in contrast with the description of the *muʾminūn* in the first verse of the sūra:** there identity is settled by a list of attributes, here by a hierarchy. I record this contrast as my own reading.

### فَكَذَّبُوهُمَا فَكَانُوا۟ مِنَ ٱلْمُهْلَكِينَ

**The brevity of the narrative is worth recording:** the narrative of Moses is one of the longest accounts in the Qurʾān; **here it ends in three verses**, and none of the events is told — no staff, no sea, no exodus.

**I give this as an observation, its ground being the block as a whole:** the sūra lines up the narratives not in order to **tell** them but in order to **show the repeating pattern.** Here too, accordingly, there are only three elements: the sending, the objection, the outcome.

**And the verb must be recorded: *kānū mina'l-muhlakīn* — "they became of those destroyed".** A passive participle pattern. **Entry into a group is reported, that is** — the group formed by the peoples named before.

### وَلَقَدْ ءَاتَيْنَا مُوسَى ٱلْكِتَٰبَ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَهْتَدُونَ

**The word order must be recorded:** the forty-eighth verse had reported the destruction; **the forty-ninth turns back and names the giving of the Book.**

**And the pronoun must be recorded: *laʿallahum yahtadūn* — who are "they"?** The lexicographers differ:

| Reading | Who *hum* are |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The Children of Israel** — the community the Book was given to |
| 2 | **Pharaoh's people** — but they had been destroyed |
| 3 | **General** — everyone the Book addresses |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

---

## 23/50 — وَجَعَلْنَا ٱبْنَ مَرْيَمَ وَأُمَّهُۥٓ ءَايَةً وَءَاوَيْنَٰهُمَآ إِلَىٰ رَبْوَةٍ ذَاتِ قَرَارٍ وَمَعِينٍ

*wa jaʿalnā'bna maryama wa ummahū āyatan wa āwaynāhumā ilā rabwatin dhāti qarārin wa maʿīn*

"**We made the son of Mary and his mother a sign, and We gave the two of them shelter on a height with a place to settle and with running water.**"

### ءَايَةً — singular

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse:** two persons are named (*ibna maryama wa ummahū*), **but *āya* comes in the singular** — *āyatan*, not the plural.

**The lexicographers give several explanations for this singular:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The two together are one sign** — neither can be thought of apart from the other |
| 2 | The singular reports **the kind**; it does not attend to number |
| 3 | The sign proper is **the event of the birth**; the two of them are the parties to that single event |

**I pass the explanations on; I do not impose a preference.** **The first explanation is the closest to the text, and I record it as an observation, its ground being that the pronoun is dual:** the continuation of the sentence has *āwaynā**humā*** (the two of them). **The sentence, that is, keeps the duality in number and unites it in the attribute.**

### وَجَعَلْنَا — the choice of the verb *jaʿala*

**The distinction between *jaʿala* and *khalaqa* was recorded at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/3 and cited at the thirteenth verse:** *jaʿala* reports **the putting of a thing from one state into another.**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** the verse speaks not of the **creation** of the two persons but of **what they were made into.** **The two are set into the condition of a sign.**

### ءَاوَيْنَٰهُمَآ — root: أ-و-ي

**The concrete sense of the root: to take refuge in a place, to be sheltered.** *Maʾwā* — a shelter; *īwāʾ* — the giving of shelter. **The root was treated at `053-necm.md` 53/15 (*jannatu'l-maʾwā*) and at `079-naziat.md` 79/39.** And the verb *īwāʾ* occurs regularly in the Qurʾān in the setting of **being taken into protection** (al-Ḍuḥā 93/6 — *a-lam yajidka yatīman fa-āwā*; treated in `093-duha.md`).

**I record this as an observation:** the verb reports not a **move** but a **sheltering.**

### رَبْوَة

The root **ر-ب-و**: **to increase, to rise, to swell.** **The root was worked out at `002-bakara.md` 2/275 (*ribā*)** and treated again at `030-rum.md` 30/39 and at `041-fussilet.md` 41/39 (*ihtazzat wa rabat*). **I do not repeat it here.**

***Rabwa* — a high place, a height.** The word occurs at al-Baqara 2/265 as well (*ka-mathali jannatin bi-rabwatin*).

**A variant reading:** ***rabwa*** / ***rubwa*** / ***ribwa***. **It does not change the meaning.**

### ذَاتِ قَرَارٍ وَمَعِينٍ — two adjectives

**قَرَار** — **the root ق-ر-ر was treated at the thirteenth verse.** **And a link within the sūra is built here, and it must be recorded:**

| Verse | Construction | The place of what |
|---|---|---|
| **13** | *fī **qarārin** makīn* | The place where **the forming** began |
| **50** | *rabwatin dhāti **qarārin** wa maʿīn* | The place **taken refuge in** |

**The same word, once the place where the body is formed, once the place where two people are sheltered.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the core sense of the word:** *qarār* is **the place where one stops, where one comes to rest.** **Both verses describe a place of protection** — one unseen, the other geographical.

**مَعِين** — the lexicographers differ over the origin of the word:

| View | Root | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **ع-ي-ن** | **Water seen by the eye, running on the surface** — *ʿayn* (a spring, an eye) |
| 2 | **م-ع-ن** | **Flowing abundantly** — *maʿn*, to flow |

**Both views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The outcome is the same on both:** running, reachable water. **The word occurred at `056-vakia.md` 56/18 (*kaʾsin min maʿīn*) and at `067-mulk.md` 67/30 (*bi-māʾin maʿīn*).**

**And the pairing of the two adjectives is worth recording:** *qarār* reports **standing still**, *maʿīn* **flowing.** **The place is fixed, the water runs.** I record this as an observation about the language.

### The place is not named

**The verse does not say where the height is.** **A few place names are transmitted in the classical commentaries; I do not pass them on, since I am not certain of their sources.** **Per the method (`STYLE.md`), I do not add to the narrative a detail the Qurʾān does not give.**

---

## 23/51 — يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلرُّسُلُ كُلُوا۟ مِنَ ٱلطَّيِّبَٰتِ وَٱعْمَلُوا۟ صَٰلِحًا إِنِّى بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ عَلِيمٌ

*yā ayyuha'l-rusulu kulū mina'l-ṭayyibāti wa'ʿmalū ṣāliḥā, innī bimā taʿmalūna ʿalīm*

"**Messengers! Eat of the good things and do righteous deeds. I know what you do.**"

### Whom the address is to

**The sentence addresses a plural: *yā ayyuha'l-rusul* — "messengers".** **And this is the only address in the Qurʾān directed to the messengers collectively.**

**The exegetes differ over who is addressed:**

| # | View | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **All the messengers** — a common command spoken to each in his own time | The plural address |
| 2 | **A single messenger** (the one in the setting) — the plural for honour or for generalisation | The preceding verses naming the messengers one by one |
| 3 | **The communities, by way of the messengers** — the address is to the messengers, the ruling is general | The content of the command applying to everyone |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

### The order of the command — eating, then deeds

**And this is the most striking place in the verse.**

**The word order must be recorded:** *kulū* (eat) comes **first**, *wa'ʿmalū ṣāliḥā* (do righteous deeds) **second**.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sūra's own text:** in the thirty-third verse the expression the other side used in objecting to the messenger was exactly this — ***yaʾkulu** mimmā taʾkulūna minh.* **The act of eating was counted a defect there.**

**Eighteen verses later the same verb becomes the first word of a command directed at all the messengers.**

| Verse | Who says it | Verb | Its office |
|---|---|---|---|
| **33** | **The objectors** | *yaʾkulu mimmā taʾkulūna minh* | **A defect** — the proof that refutes the claim to be a messenger |
| **51** | **The text** | ***kulū** mina'l-ṭayyibāt* | **A command** — the order given to the messengers |

**This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.**

**And the same method was treated between `025-furkan.md` 25/7 and 25/20.** What was recorded there holds here too:

> **The answer does not reject the description in the objection — it confirms it and generalises it.** Not "it is not so", that is, but "it always was so".

**I compare the method of the two sūras:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/7 → 25/20 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/33 → 23/51** |
|---|---|---|
| The objection | *yaʾkulu'l-ṭaʿāma wa yamshī fi'l-aswāq* | *yaʾkulu mimmā taʾkulūna minh* |
| The mood of the answer | **Report** — *la-yaʾkulūna'l-ṭaʿām* (it always was so) | **Command** — *kulū* (do it thus) |
| The distance between | **13 verses** | **18 verses** |
| Whom the answer addresses | **The objectors** | **The messengers** |

**The difference is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** al-Furqān meets the objection with a **report** — "the messengers were always like this". **al-Muʾminūn turns the same verb into a command** — "eat". **One is a defence, then, the other an order.**

### مِنَ ٱلطَّيِّبَٰت

The root **ط-ي-ب**: **to be pleasant, to be clean, to be good.** *Ṭayyib* — pleasant, clean, lawful. **The root was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/168 and 2/172 (*kulū min ṭayyibāti mā razaqnākum*).**

**And there is an overlap that must be recorded:** al-Baqara 2/172 directs the same command **to those who believe**; **this verse directs the same command to the messengers.**

| Place | Addressee | Command |
|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` 2/172 | *yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū* | *kulū min ṭayyibāti mā razaqnākum* |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/51** | *yā ayyuha'l-rusul* | *kulū mina'l-ṭayyibāt* |

**The same command, to two different addressees.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts, and I record it as an observation: **on this item no difference is set between the messenger and the believer.**

### وَٱعْمَلُوا۟ صَٰلِحًا

***Ṣāliḥ* — root ص-ل-ح: to be unspoiled, to be sound and serviceable.** *Iṣlāḥ* — putting right; *ṣulḥ* — the repairing of what is broken. **The word has occurred in many places across this series.**

**And the word order must be recorded: *ṣāliḥan* is indefinite** — "a righteous (deed)". Not definite. **The command, that is, does not give a list.**

**إِنِّى بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ عَلِيمٌ** — the close of the sentence is attached to the **deed**, not to the eating. **And *bimā taʿmalūna* has been brought forward** (*taqdīm*): "what you do — I know."

---

## 23/52 — وَإِنَّ هَٰذِهِۦٓ أُمَّتُكُمْ أُمَّةً وَٰحِدَةً وَأَنَا۠ رَبُّكُمْ فَٱتَّقُونِ

*wa inna hādhihī ummatukum ummatan wāḥidatan wa ana rabbukum fa'ttaqūn*

"**This community of yours is one single community, and I am your Lord. So guard yourselves against Me.**"

### The variant reading

**Three readings are transmitted for the particle at the head of the sentence, and the difference bears on the meaning:**

| Reading | Parsing | The sense of the sentence |
|---|---|---|
| ***wa inna*** (with kasra, with shadda) | It begins a new sentence | "**And truly** this community of yours is one single community" |
| ***wa anna*** (with fatḥa, with shadda) | It attaches to an element in the preceding sentence | "…and (know) this: your community is one single community" |
| ***wa in*** (without shadda, the lightened form) | Emphasis | The sense is close to the first |

**All three readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **It produces no radical change in the ruling.**

### أُمَّة — root: أ-م-م

**The concrete sense of the root: to turn towards, to aim at; and origin, mother.** *Amma* — he turned towards; *imām* — the one who goes in front, the one turned towards; *umm* — mother; *umma* — a community gathered towards a common direction.

**The word was treated at `042-sura.md` 42/13 and at `002-bakara.md` 2/213.**

**And the parsing of the word here must be recorded:** *ummatan wāḥidatan* is **in the accusative** — the lexicographers count it a **circumstantial accusative**. **The sentence, that is, is closer to "your community — as one single community — is your community" than to "your community is one community".** I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.

### فَٱتَّقُونِ — and the form of the same sentence in al-Anbiyāʾ

**This sentence occurs once more in the Qurʾān, almost word for word: `021-enbiya.md` 21/92.** **The only difference between the two verses is in the last word:**

| Place | Sentence | Last word |
|---|---|---|
| `021-enbiya.md` **21/92** | *inna hādhihī ummatukum ummatan wāḥidatan wa ana rabbukum* | ***fa'ʿbudūn*** — **"so serve Me"** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/52** | *wa inna hādhihī ummatukum ummatan wāḥidatan wa ana rabbukum* | ***fa'ttaqūn*** — **"so guard yourselves against Me"** |

**This verse was treated at `021-enbiya.md` 21/92-93; I rely on that treatment.** **And the *following* verse of the two sūras overlaps as well** — al-Anbiyāʾ 21/93: *wa **taqaṭṭaʿū amrahum baynahum** kullun ilaynā rājiʿūn*; al-Muʾminūn 23/53: *fa-**taqaṭṭaʿū amrahum baynahum** zuburā.* **In both sūras, that is, the sentence of unity is followed by the same sentence of breaking apart.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the wording of the two texts.

**And the choice of word here has a ground within the sūra, which must be recorded:** the question *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* has occurred twice in this sūra, from the mouths of two messengers (23, 32). **The fifty-second verse brings the same root in the imperative.** The sūra, that is, repeats the messengers' question as its own verdict.

### Where the disagreement comes from — the passage to verse 53

**The fifty-second verse reports unity; the fifty-third reports the breaking apart.** **The passage between them is the very question treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/213 and `042-sura.md` 42/13-14.**

**It was recorded at `002-bakara.md` 2/213:**

> **The cause of the division, that is, is not given as ignorance.** The text says it plainly: *min baʿdi mā jāʾathumu'l-bayyināt* — **after** the clear proofs had come. **The cause is given not as a lack of knowledge but as a disposition held against one another.**

**At `042-sura.md` 42/14 the same ruling was repeated in almost the same words:** *wa mā tafarraqū illā min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-ʿilmu baghyan baynahum.*

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.** **The three sūras build the same order:**

| Place | Unity | What comes | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` 2/213 | *kāna'l-nāsu ummatan wāḥida* | **Prophets and a book** | *ikhtalafa fīhi* — *baghyan baynahum* |
| `042-sura.md` 42/13-14 | *aqīmū'l-dīna wa lā tatafarraqū fīh* | **al-ʿIlm** | *mā tafarraqū illā min baʿdi…* |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/52-53** | *ummatukum ummatan wāḥida* | **The messengers** (51) | *fa-taqaṭṭaʿū amrahum baynahum* |

**In all three the order is the same: unity → knowledge → division.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the three texts.

**And the difference in al-Muʾminūn must be recorded:** al-Baqara and al-Shūrā name the **cause** of the division (*baghy* — envious rivalry). **al-Muʾminūn gives no cause;** in its place it describes the **form** of the division: *zuburan* and *kullu ḥizbin bimā ladayhim fariḥūn.* **One tells why, that is, the other how.**

---

## 23/53 — فَتَقَطَّعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَهُم بَيْنَهُمْ زُبُرًا كُلُّ حِزْبٍۭ بِمَا لَدَيْهِمْ فَرِحُونَ

*fa-taqaṭṭaʿū amrahum baynahum zuburā, kullu ḥizbin bimā ladayhim fariḥūn*

"**But they cut their affair apart among themselves into pieces. Every party rejoices in what it has.**"

### فَتَقَطَّعُوا۟ — word order

The root **ق-ط-ع**: **to cut, to sever.** **The pattern is Form V (*taqaṭṭaʿa*), and the lexicographers record that this pattern reports two things at once here: *takalluf* (doing it with their own hands) and *muṭāwaʿa* (being broken apart).**

**And the word order must be recorded:** the verb is transitive — *taqaṭṭaʿū **amrahum***. **What is cut apart, that is, is not they but *their affair*.** They are not being broken apart; **they are breaking apart the one affair in their hands.**

**I give this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is the presence of the object.** And the qualification *baynahum* (among themselves) shows that the breaking apart took the form of **a division of shares**.

### زُبُرًا — root: ز-ب-ر

**The root was worked out at `054-kamer.md` 54/43 and 54/52.** **I do not repeat it here.** A summary of what was recorded there:

> The root **ز-ب-ر**: **to incise on a hard surface; and a mass of iron, a well lined with stone.** *Zubur* — the plural of *zabūr* or of *zibr*: **written pages, books.** **And in `054-kamer.md` the difference between ز-ب-ر and س-ط-ر was tabulated:** ز-ب-ر is incising, and it reports **permanence — not being erased.**

**And in `054-kamer.md` it was shown that the root builds a question-and-answer link within that sūra** (a question at 54/43, an answer at 54/52). **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is the meaning of the word in this verse, and the lexicographers differ:**

| Reading | What *zubur* is | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Books** — each party took a book of its own | The "written page" branch of the root; and the setting being religion |
| 2 | **Pieces** — the plural of *zubra*; *zubratu'l-ḥadīd*, a mass or piece of iron (it occurs at al-Kahf 18/96) | The verb (*taqaṭṭaʿū*) reporting a breaking into pieces |
| 3 | **Groups, parties** | *kullu ḥizbin* in the continuation of the sentence |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**There is a variant reading as well:** the readings ***zuburan*** (زُبُرًا — with *ḍamma* on the *bāʾ*, the plural of *zabūr/zibr*) and ***zubara*** (زُبَرًا — with *fatḥa* on the *bāʾ*, the plural of *zubra*) are transmitted. **This variant reading bears directly on the disagreement above:** the *fatḥa* reading supports the second sense, the *ḍamma* reading the first.

**And the lexicographers differ over its parsing as well:** is *zuburan* a **circumstantial accusative** (in pieces), or a **substitute** for *amr*? **Both parsings are transmitted.**

**I record this much as my own reading, its ground being the analysis of the root in `054-kamer.md`:** at the core of the root is **incising on a hard surface.** **Whichever sense is taken, the word reports something that cannot easily be undone** — whether an incised text or a broken-off piece of iron. **And set beside the verb (*taqaṭṭaʿū*), the permanence of what has been done comes to the fore.** This is a reading, and it is not binding.

### كُلُّ حِزْبٍۭ بِمَا لَدَيْهِمْ فَرِحُونَ

**The sentence occurs once more in the Qurʾān, in exactly the same wording: al-Rūm 30/32.** **It was treated at `030-rum.md` 30/32** (*mina'lladhīna farraqū dīnahum wa kānū shiyaʿan, kullu ḥizbin bimā ladayhim fariḥūn*). **I rely on that treatment.**

**The two verses can be compared:**

| | `030-rum.md` 30/32 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/53** |
|---|---|---|
| The verb before it | *farraqū **dīnahum*** — they divided **their religion** | *taqaṭṭaʿū **amrahum*** — they cut apart **their affair** |
| The name of the groups | *kānū **shiyaʿan*** — sects | *zuburā* — pieces / books |
| The shared sentence | *kullu ḥizbin bimā ladayhim fariḥūn* | **Word for word the same** |

**The same concluding sentence, after two different verbs.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

**حِزْب** — the root **ح-ز-ب**: **to gather together; and a definite portion of a thing.** *Ḥizb* — a band, a party; and a section of a book. The lexicographers also record the branch of the root meaning "distress, hardship" (*ḥazabahu'l-amr*).

### فَرِحُون — and the distinction between ف-ر-ح / م-ر-ح

**The distinction between the roots was worked out at `040-gafir.md` 40/75.** **I do not repeat it here.** What was given there:

| Word | Root | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| *tafraḥūn* | **ف-ر-ح** | **To rejoice, to be gladdened** |
| *tamraḥūn* | **م-ر-ح** | **To be giddy, to walk with a swagger** |

**And the finding recorded in `040-gafir.md` was this:**

> **Rejoicing is not condemned in itself — rejoicing without warrant is condemned.** The second verb, by contrast, comes without qualification, because the word *maraḥ* already carries excess within it.

**I rely on that treatment.** **And what belongs here is this:** in this verse it is not *maraḥ* but ***fariḥ*** that is used — **so what is condemned is not joy itself.** Indeed at `030-rum.md` 30/1-5 the same root was used of **the believers**: *wa yawmaʾidhin **yafraḥu**'l-muʾminūn.*

**This distinction was tabulated in `030-rum.md`**, where the root's three occurrences (30/4, 30/32, 30/36) were gathered under the heading **"joy changing according to what it is attached to"**. **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the qualification in the sentence:** what is condemned is not *faraḥ* but ***bimā ladayhim** fariḥūn* — **rejoicing "in what they have".** And the word *ladayhim* (what is beside them) must be recorded: **joy is being measured by what is held in hand.** The verse points at the object of the joy.

### The verse as a whole

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the two sentences:** the verse first tells the **breaking apart** (*taqaṭṭaʿū*), then tells that **the breaking apart continues** (*fariḥūn* — an active participle, an ongoing state). **And the second sentence explains why the first is not being repaired:** each party is content with what it has. **The breaking apart, that is, is not experienced as a loss.**

---

## 23/54 — فَذَرْهُمْ فِى غَمْرَتِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ حِينٍ

*fa-dharhum fī ghamratihim ḥattā ḥīn*

"**So leave them in their own flood until a time.**"

### غَمْرَة — root: غ-م-ر

**The concrete sense of the root: water's covering a thing completely, closing over it and taking it in.** *Ghamara'l-māʾu* — the water covered it; *ghamr* — abundant water, a deep place; *ghimr* — rancour (what covers the heart); *mughāmara* — throwing oneself into a thing.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.**

**The picture in the word is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** *ghamra* is a state **one is inside of and from which the outside cannot be seen.** **The word, that is, names not an error but a *being surrounded*.**

**And the word will return once more in the sūra, nine verses later:**

| Verse | Sentence | Where the *ghamra* is |
|---|---|---|
| **54** | *fa-dharhum **fī ghamratihim*** | **They** are inside it |
| **63** | *bal **qulūbuhum fī ghamratin** min hādhā* | **Their hearts** are inside it |

**The same word, twice, nine verses apart.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**And the difference must be recorded:** in the fifty-fourth verse what is surrounded is **the persons**; in the sixty-third it is **their hearts.** **The second occurrence, that is, narrows the location of the first.**

### حَتَّىٰ حِينٍ — and its link with the twenty-fifth verse

**These two words occurred in the twenty-fifth verse in the mouth of the other side:** *fa-tarabbaṣū bihī ḥattā ḥīn* — "wait and watch him for a time".

| Verse | Who says it | Command | Concerning whom |
|---|---|---|---|
| **25** | **Noah's people** | *fa-tarabbaṣū bihī* — **watch** | Concerning **the messenger** |
| **54** | **The text** | *fa-dharhum* — **leave** | Concerning **them** |

**The same two words, on two different sides.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and I record it as an observation: **the command to wait changes direction twenty-nine verses later.**

**حِين** — **an indefinite stretch of time.** The lexicographers record that the word reports no fixed span. **In neither verse is the span stated.**

### فَذَرْهُمْ — the meaning of the verb

The root **و-ذ-ر**: **to leave, to abandon.** **The lexicographers record that the perfect of this verb is not used** — only the imperfect and the imperative are current.

**And the scope of the command must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** the command is not **"do not trouble yourself with them"** but **"leave them inside their own state"**. The qualification *fī ghamratihim* shows this: the place they are left in is their own condition.

**The same line was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/43 (*a-fa-anta takūnu ʿalayhi wakīlā*) and in the limit of responsibility tabulated at `028-kasas.md` 28/56.** **This verse is a link in that line.**

---

## 23/55-56 — أَيَحْسَبُونَ أَنَّمَا نُمِدُّهُم بِهِۦ مِن مَّالٍ وَبَنِينَ · نُسَارِعُ لَهُمْ فِى ٱلْخَيْرَٰتِ بَل لَّا يَشْعُرُونَ

*a-yaḥsabūna annamā numidduhum bihī min mālin wa banīn · nusāriʿu lahum fi'l-khayrāt, bal lā yashʿurūn*

"**Do they suppose that, in the wealth and sons with which We supply them, We are hurrying good things to them? No — they do not perceive.**"

### نُمِدُّهُم — root: م-د-د

**The concrete sense of the root: to extend, to draw out, to feed continuously.** *Madd* — extending; *midād* — ink (what flows steadily to the pen); *imdād* — supplying without interruption.

**The pattern is Form IV, and the lexicographers record a distinction between *madda* and *amadda*:** *amadda* is used mostly for **good and support**, *madda* for **extending a term and granting respite**. **I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted;** there are exceptions to this distinction in the Qurʾān as well.

**And the verb's coming in the imperfect must be recorded:** *numidduhum* — **an ongoing giving.**

### مَالٍ وَبَنِين

**The two words occur together regularly in the Qurʾān:** al-Kahf 18/46 (*al-mālu wa'l-banūna zīnatu'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā*), Sabaʾ 34/35 (*naḥnu aktharu amwālan wa awlādan*; treated in `034-sebe.md`), Nūḥ 71/12 (treated in `071-nuh.md`), al-Qalam 68/14 (treated in `068-kalem.md`).

**And the whole of this question was treated at `034-sebe.md` 34/35-37:** the answer given to **counting wealth and children a measure of nearness**. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is how the question is built.**

### How the question is built — two verses forming one sentence

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the two verses:** the fifty-fifth and fifty-sixth verses are **a single sentence**; the verse boundary does not divide the sentence.

**The skeleton of the sentence is this:** *a-yaḥsabūna anna… **nusāriʿu lahum fi'l-khayrāt***. **The question, that is, is not about "We are giving wealth"; it is about the assumption that "to give wealth is to hurry good things to them".**

**I give this as an observation about the word order, its ground being that the predicate of *anna* is in the second verse.** And the lexicographers differ over the parsing: whether *annamā* is joined (*kāffa*) or separate (*anna* + relative *mā*); both parsings are transmitted. **In either case the ruling of the sentence comes out at the same place.**

### نُسَارِعُ لَهُمْ فِى ٱلْخَيْرَٰت — and its link with the sixty-first verse

**And one of the most striking verbal weaves of the sūra is built here.**

The root **س-ر-ع**: **to be swift.** **The pattern is Form III (*sāraʿa*): to hurry, to hasten as though racing.**

**The same verb will occur once more, five verses later:**

| Verse | Sentence | Agent | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| **56** | *nusāriʿu lahum fi'l-khayrāt* | **We** (supposedly) | Good things are hurried **to them** — **what they suppose** |
| **61** | *ulāʾika **yusāriʿūna** fi'l-khayrāt* | **They** — those described at 57-60 | **They themselves** hurry to good things — **what is** |

**The same verb, with the same construction (*fi'l-khayrāt*), twice, five verses apart.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra and is read from the wording of the text.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this word-for-word repetition:** in the fifty-sixth verse the one hurrying to the good things is **the giver**, and the place hurried to is **in front of them**; in the sixty-first the ones hurrying to the good things are **the servants.** **Five verses after describing a delusion, that is, the sūra shows the right subject of the same expression.**

**And the difference is precisely a difference of subject:** one supposes that something is being hurried to him, the other is himself hurrying.

### بَل لَّا يَشْعُرُونَ

**The particle *bal* was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/11:** **it drops what has just been said and brings the real cause.** **I rely on that treatment.**

The root **ش-ع-ر**: **to notice something fine, to sense it.** *Shaʿr* — hair (from its fineness); *shāʿir* — the one who notices what is fine; *mashʿar* — a place marked and known; *shuʿūr* — perception.

**And the choice of verb must be recorded:** the verse does not say *lā yaʿlamūn* (they do not know) — ***lā yashʿurūn*** (they do not perceive). **The picture in the root is the noticing of something fine.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this sense of the root:** what is lacking is not knowledge but **noticing**. **They see that wealth and children are being given; they do not perceive what the giving means.**

### The close of block V

**The section from the fifty-first verse to the fifty-sixth is a whole, and its order is worth recording:**

| Verse | What |
|---|---|
| 51 | **A command shared by the messengers** — eat and do righteous deeds |
| 52 | **The oneness of the community** |
| 53 | **The breaking apart**, and the contentment of the groups |
| 54 | **Being left** — until a time |
| 55-56 | **The misreading of prosperity** |

**I give this as my own reading:** the block descends from unity to breaking apart, from breaking apart to contentment, from contentment to delusion. **And at every step the other side holds a *good* opinion:** rejoicing in what it has (53), counting prosperity a favour (55-56). **What the block describes, that is, is not a punishment but a misreading.**

---

## 23/57-59 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ هُم مِّنْ خَشْيَةِ رَبِّهِم مُّشْفِقُونَ · وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِـَٔايَٰتِ رَبِّهِمْ يُؤْمِنُونَ · وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِرَبِّهِمْ لَا يُشْرِكُونَ

*inna'lladhīna hum min khashyati rabbihim mushfiqūn · wa'lladhīna hum bi-āyāti rabbihim yuʾminūn · wa'lladhīna hum bi-rabbihim lā yushrikūn*

"**Those who tremble in awe of their Lord; those who believe in the signs of their Lord; those who associate nothing with their Lord…**"

### The second list of attributes — the return of the pattern

**The pattern of the sūra's first eleven verses returns here unchanged:** *alladhīna hum* + fronted complement + predicate.

**The two lists must be set out in a table:**

| | **The first list (1-11)** | **The second list (57-61)** |
|---|---|---|
| Opening | *qad aflaḥa'l-muʾminūn* — **the verdict first** | *inna'lladhīna hum…* — **the verdict at the end** |
| Number of items | **Six** (2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9) | **Four** (57, 58, 59, 60) |
| The kind of items | **Acts** — prayer, speech, property, body, covenant | **States** — trembling, faith, tawḥīd, the quaking of the heart |
| Closing | *ulāʾika humu'l-wārithūn* (10) | *ulāʾika yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt* (61) |
| The kind of closing | **A place** — *al-Firdaws* | **A movement** — *yusāriʿūn*, *sābiqūn* |
| Shared by every item | *hum*, the pronoun of separation | *hum*, the pronoun of separation (57, 58, 59 — **absent at 60**) |

**I record one difference separately:** the sixtieth verse is **the one item of the list that does not carry the pronoun *hum*** — *wa'lladhīna yuʾtūna mā ātaw.* **This is a detail that can be verified from the text**, and it will be taken up at the sixtieth verse.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the table above:** the first list enumerates **the works done**, the second **what is inside while the works are done.** **And the first list ends with an arrival (an inheritance), the second with a running (a race).** **One names the outcome, that is, the other the movement.**

### مِّنْ خَشْيَةِ رَبِّهِم مُّشْفِقُون — two words

**خَشْيَة — root خ-ش-ي:** the description the lexicographers give is **a holding back that is felt knowingly**. *Khashya* has to do with knowing the greatness of what is feared; thus at Fāṭir 35/28 it is said *innamā yakhsha'llāha min ʿibādihi'l-ʿulamāʾ*. **The root was treated at `035-fatir.md` 35/28 and at `067-mulk.md` 67/12 (*yakhshawna rabbahum bi'l-ghayb*).**

**And a note of caution must be entered here:** the root of this word (**خ-ش-ي**) and the root of the word *khāshiʿūn* in the second verse (**خ-ش-ع**) **are separate roots.** Their sounds are close and their senses are neighbours; **but I build no derivational link between them.**

**The same note of caution was entered in `080-abese.md` and in `083-mutaffifin.md` for roots close to one another in sound:** *"closeness of sound is not identity of root."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**مُشْفِقُون — root ش-ف-ق:** the core sense the lexicographers give is **thinness**. *Shafaq* — the thin light between day and night; *shafaqa* — the thinness of a thing. **And *ishfāq* is described by the lexicographers as follows: a fear with love in it, or a love with fear in it.** **The root was treated at `084-insikak.md` 84/16 (*fa-lā uqsimu bi'l-shafaq*).**

**The build of the construction must be recorded:** *min khashyati rabbihim mushfiqūn* — **the source of the fear (*khashya*) and the state that is felt (*ishfāq*) are named separately.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being that both words have at their core not "shuddering" but "thinness / knowing":** the verse is not speaking of terror. **Both words are words that describe a sharpening of attention.**

### وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِـَٔايَٰتِ رَبِّهِمْ يُؤْمِنُونَ

**And the word order must be recorded: *bi-āyāti rabbihim* has been brought forward.** Not "they believe", that is, but "in the signs of their Lord — they believe".

**And a contrast will be built with the sixty-sixth verse:** *qad kānat āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum fa-kuntum ʿalā aʿqābikum tankiṣūn.* **The same word (*āyāt*), with two different bearings.**

### وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِرَبِّهِمْ لَا يُشْرِكُونَ

**And the repetition of the word *rabb* must be recorded:** in **all four** of the four items, *rabbihim* occurs — *khashyati **rabbihim***, *āyāti **rabbihim***, *bi-**rabbihim***, *ilā **rabbihim*** (60).

**This is a fact that can be counted from the text, and it has no counterpart in the first list:** the word *rabb* does not occur in a single one of the six items of the first list.

| | **The first list** | **The second list** |
|---|---|---|
| The word *rabb* | **It does not occur at all** | **In all four of the four items** |
| The subject of the items | **Acts** — prayer, property, body | **A relation** — always looking in one direction |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this count:** the second list builds its items **by attaching them to one addressed.** **The first list enumerates the acts on their own.** The difference between the two lists shows here as well.

---

## 23/60 — وَٱلَّذِينَ يُؤْتُونَ مَآ ءَاتَوا۟ وَّقُلُوبُهُمْ وَجِلَةٌ أَنَّهُمْ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ رَٰجِعُونَ

*wa'lladhīna yuʾtūna mā ātaw wa qulūbuhum wajilatun annahum ilā rabbihim rājiʿūn*

"**Those who give what they give with their hearts trembling because they are returning to their Lord…**"

### The variant reading — it changes the meaning

**The variant reading transmitted in this verse bears directly on the meaning:**

| Reading | Verb | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***yuʾtūna mā ātaw*** | **إيتاء** — to give (Form IV) | "**They give what they give**" — spending |
| ***yaʾtūna mā ataw*** | **إتيان** — to come / to do (Form I) | "**They do what they do**" — deeds |

**The second reading is transmitted in the sources, and on that reading the verse describes not spending but deeds in general.** **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what the two readings have in common must be recorded:** on both, the real question is not the act itself but **the state of the heart that accompanies the act.**

### وَقُلُوبُهُمْ وَجِلَةٌ — the core of the verse

The root **و-ج-ل**: **to tremble with fear, to shudder.** *Wajal* — the trembling in the heart.
