**The word occurs regularly in the Qurʾān together with the heart:** al-Anfāl 8/2 (*wajilat qulūbuhum*), al-Ḥijr 15/52 and 15/53, al-Ḥajj 22/35, al-Zumar 39/23 (treated in `039-zumer.md`).

**And the striking place in the verse is this, and I record it as my own reading:** the sentence describes a fear felt **while doing a good work.** **Not while committing a sin.**

**My ground is the build of the sentence:** *yuʾtūna mā ātaw* (they give) and *qulūbuhum wajila* (their hearts tremble) take place **at the same time** — the second is a circumstantial clause.

**And the ground of the fear is given in the verse:** *annahum ilā rabbihim rājiʿūn* — **"that they are returning to their Lord."**

### أَنَّهُمْ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ رَٰجِعُونَ — its parsing

**The lexicographers differ over the parsing of the construction, and the difference bears on the meaning:**

| Reading | What stands before *anna* (understood) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***li-anna*** (cause) | "Their hearts tremble **because they are returning to their Lord**" |
| 2 | ***min anna*** / *bi-anna* | Close to the same direction |
| 3 | The object of the verb *wajila* | "They **fear** that they are returning to their Lord" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**The third reading is worth recording, because its outcome differs:** on the first reading what is feared is **that the deed will not be enough**, on the third **the return itself.**

**And the line recorded at `035-fatir.md` 35/28 and `067-mulk.md` 67/12 holds here too:** in the Qurʾān *khashya* is named together with knowledge, *wajal* with the expectation of a meeting.

### The place of this item in the list

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the arrangement of the list:** the first three of the four items reported **states** (trembling, faith, not associating partners). **The fourth brings a *deed*** (*yuʾtūna*) **and places a state beside the deed.**

**The list, that is, in descending from states to a deed, sets the state inside the deed.** And this is the reverse of the first list: **there the acts were enumerated and their fields fixed; here the states are enumerated and at the end attached to a deed.**

---

## 23/61 — أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ يُسَٰرِعُونَ فِى ٱلْخَيْرَٰتِ وَهُمْ لَهَا سَٰبِقُونَ

*ulāʾika yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāti wa hum lahā sābiqūn*

"**Those are the ones who hurry to good things, and it is they who are foremost in them.**"

### يُسَٰرِعُونَ فِى ٱلْخَيْرَٰت — the weave with the fifty-sixth verse

**This construction occurred word for word in the fifty-sixth verse and was tabulated there.** **I do not repeat it here;** I recall it briefly: in fifty-six the agent of the verb is **the giver**, here **the servants.** **The same expression, five verses apart, with two different subjects.**

### وَهُمْ لَهَا سَٰبِقُونَ — a disagreement over the parsing

The root **س-ب-ق**: **to go before, to be ahead in a race.** **The root was treated in detail at `056-vakia.md` 56/10 (*wa'l-sābiqūna'l-sābiqūn*)** — where it was recorded that the word **ceases to report an act and turns into the name of a class.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The lexicographers differ over the parsing of the construction here:**

| Reading | The work of *lahā* | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The complement of *sābiqūn* | "They are the ones who go foremost in hurrying **to them** (the good things)" |
| 2 | The *lām* here carries the sense of *ilā* | "They are the ones who go foremost **towards them**" |
| 3 | The *lām* reports belonging | "**The good things are theirs**; they are the ones who go foremost" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the word order must be recorded: *lahā* has been brought forward** — *wa hum **lahā** sābiqūn*, "in them — foremost". **This is the same pattern of fronting as in the six items of the first list.** The list, that is, keeps its own word order in its closing sentence as well.

### The closings of the two lists

| | **The first list** | **The second list** |
|---|---|---|
| The closing sentence | *ulāʾika humu'l-wārithūn* (10) | *ulāʾika yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt* (61) |
| The predicate | **A noun** — *al-wārithūn* | **A verb** — *yusāriʿūn* |
| The time | **What is to be received in the future** | **What is being done now** |
| Its continuation | *alladhīna yarithūna'l-firdaws* — **a place** | *wa hum lahā sābiqūn* — **a rank** |

**Both closings begin with *ulāʾika*, and both gather the list up and attach it to a verdict.** This is a feature shared by the two lists and can be verified in them.

**I give this as my own reading:** the first list states **the place arrived at**, the second **the speed of going.** **The sūra has thereby described deliverance from two different directions:** what will be received, and how the going is done.

---

## 23/62 — وَلَا نُكَلِّفُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا وَلَدَيْنَا كِتَٰبٌ يَنطِقُ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَهُمْ لَا يُظْلَمُونَ

*wa lā nukallifu nafsan illā wusʿahā wa ladaynā kitābun yanṭiqu bi'l-ḥaqqi wa hum lā yuẓlamūn*

"**We do not burden any soul beyond its capacity. With Us is a book that speaks the truth, and they will not be wronged.**"

### وَلَا نُكَلِّفُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا

**This sentence was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/286 (*lā yukallifu'llāhu nafsan illā wusʿahā*).** **I do not repeat it here.** What was recorded there:

> **وُسْع** — root **و-س-ع**: breadth. **The measure, that is, is the person's own breadth.** This sentence is a direct answer to the anxiety produced by verse 284, and it is the final expression of the principle of ease (2/185) seen again and again in the legal sections of that sūra.

**I rely on that treatment.** **What belongs here is the difference of setting between the two verses, and it must be recorded:**

| | `002-bakara.md` 2/286 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/62** |
|---|---|---|
| The subject of the verb | *yukallifu**'llāhu*** — **the name is explicit** | *nukallifu* — **first person plural** |
| What it comes after | **The anxiety produced by 2/284** — will what is within us be reckoned too | **The race in verse 61** — hurrying to good things |
| What work it does | **It meets the anxiety** | **It sets the limit of the race** |
| Its continuation | *lahā mā kasabat wa ʿalayhā mā'ktasabat* | *wa ladaynā kitābun yanṭiqu bi'l-ḥaqq* |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the place of the verse within the sūra:** the sixty-first verse had spoken of a **race** — *yusāriʿūn*, *sābiqūn*. **Immediately after the race a limit is set:** no one is charged with what is beyond his capacity.

**The sentence, that is, prevents those in front from being made the measure of those left behind.** This reading is my own and is not binding.

The root **ك-ل-ف**: **to take on a task with difficulty, hardship.** *Takalluf* — doing a thing under strain; *kulfa* — trouble. **The pattern is Form II: *kallafa* — to lay a task upon someone.**

### وَلَدَيْنَا كِتَٰبٌ يَنطِقُ بِٱلْحَقِّ — and a comparison with al-Jāthiya 45/29

**This construction was treated at `045-casiye.md` 45/29 (*hādhā kitābunā yanṭiqu ʿalaykum bi'l-ḥaqq*).** What was recorded there:

> The root **ن-ط-ق**: to speak, to give voice. **The choice of verb is striking: the book does not "write" or "show" — it *speaks*.** … **A document is normally read; the reading party interprets it. The verse removes this intermediary: the document speaks for itself.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **And the comparison of the two verses is the real work of this section, because the difference between them lies in a single word:**

| | `045-casiye.md` 45/29 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/62** |
|---|---|---|
| The sentence | *hādhā kitābunā yanṭiqu **ʿalaykum** bi'l-ḥaqq* | *ladaynā kitābun yanṭiqu bi'l-ḥaqq* |
| The difference | ***ʿalaykum*** — **"against you"** | **Absent** — no such particle comes |
| The position of the book | *hādhā* — **here, opened** | *ladaynā* — **with Us**, not yet opened |
| To whom it is said | **To the second person** — at the moment of reckoning | **About the third person** — in this world |
| The scene | **The resurrection** — *tarā kulla ummatin jāthiya* | **This world** — the discussion of the race and the burden |
| Its continuation | *innā kunnā nastansikhu mā kuntum taʿmalūn* | *wa hum lā yuẓlamūn* |

**The difference is a single word, and it changes the direction of the meaning.** **In Arabic *ʿalā* here reports being against;** this was recorded in `045-casiye.md`.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the difference of scene between the two verses:** in al-Jāthiya the book is **opened and speaking against the one addressed** — there the matter is finished. **In al-Muʾminūn the book is still *with Us*, and only that "it speaks the truth" is reported** — against whom is not said.

**And this fits the work of the two verses:** the sentence in al-Jāthiya is a verdict; **the sentence in al-Muʾminūn is an assurance.** Indeed its continuation is *wa hum lā yuẓlamūn* — "they will not be wronged."

**And the construction *bi'l-ḥaqq* must itself be recorded:** the book speaks **the truth**. **It is reported, that is, that there is no difference between the record and what happened.** The finding recorded for the word *nastansikh* (making a copy) in `045-casiye.md` answers to this: **the original is the life lived; the book is its copy.**

---

## 23/63 — بَلْ قُلُوبُهُمْ فِى غَمْرَةٍ مِّنْ هَٰذَا وَلَهُمْ أَعْمَٰلٌ مِّن دُونِ ذَٰلِكَ هُمْ لَهَا عَٰمِلُونَ

*bal qulūbuhum fī ghamratin min hādhā wa lahum aʿmālun min dūni dhālika hum lahā ʿāmilūn*

"**No — their hearts are engulfed as regards this. And they have works besides that, which they keep on doing.**"

### فِى غَمْرَةٍ — the word's second occurrence

**The root was worked out at the fifty-fourth verse.** **What belongs here is the narrowing between the two occurrences, and it was tabulated there;** in brief: at 54 what is surrounded is **the persons**, at 63 **their hearts.**

**And the qualification *min hādhā* must be recorded:** *hādhā* ("this") is a demonstrative pronoun, and **what it is is not said.**

**The lexicographers give the referent of the pronoun in several ways:**

| Reading | What *hādhā* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The Qurʾān** — the text set out in the sūra |
| 2 | **The attributes enumerated at 57-61** — the verses immediately before |
| 3 | **The reckoning and the return** — *ilā rabbihim rājiʿūn* in verse 60 |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the word order must be recorded: the particle *min* here is explained by the lexicographers as "as regards…, in respect of…".** The engulfment, that is, is not general but **on a particular matter.**

### وَلَهُمْ أَعْمَٰلٌ مِّن دُونِ ذَٰلِكَ

***Min dūni dhālika* — "other than that, apart from that".**

**The lexicographers differ over what the sentence reports:**

| Reading | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1 | They have works **outside the attributes enumerated** — they do not possess those attributes |
| 2 | There are deeds **written for them, which they will do** |
| 3 | **Lower** works — *dūn* here means "below" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And a detail of the word order is worth recording:** the end of the sentence is *hum lahā ʿāmilūn* — **a fronted complement + an active participle.** **This is the pattern of the sūra's two lists of attributes** (*hum lahā sābiqūn*, 61; *hum fī ṣalātihim khāshiʿūn*, 2).

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the same pattern is used here **for the other side.** **The sūra, that is, applies its own pattern of attributes to those left outside the list as well.** This can be verified from the wording of the three verses.

---

## 23/64-65 — حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَآ أَخَذْنَا مُتْرَفِيهِم بِٱلْعَذَابِ إِذَا هُمْ يَجْـَٔرُونَ · لَا تَجْـَٔرُوا۟ ٱلْيَوْمَ إِنَّكُم مِّنَّا لَا تُنصَرُونَ

*ḥattā idhā akhadhnā mutrafīhim bi'l-ʿadhābi idhā hum yajʾarūn · lā tajʾarū'l-yawma innakum minnā lā tunṣarūn*

"**Until, when We seize those of them who live in ease with the punishment, at once they bellow for help. 'Do not bellow today: you will not be helped against Us.'**"

### حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا — the three turns in the sūra

**This pattern opens three different scenes three times in the sūra:**

| Verse | Sentence | What it begins |
|---|---|---|
| **64** | *ḥattā idhā akhadhnā mutrafīhim bi'l-ʿadhāb* | **The seizing in this world** |
| **77** | *ḥattā idhā fataḥnā ʿalayhim bāban dhā ʿadhābin shadīd* | **The cutting off of hope** |
| **99** | *ḥattā idhā jāʾa aḥadahumu'l-mawt* | **The moment of death** |

**All three occurrences can be verified from the text.** And I record this as my own reading: **each time, the pattern marks the moment at which an ongoing state is cut off.** *Ḥattā* is the particle that draws the limit of a going.

### مُتْرَفِيهِم — the return of the root

**The root ت-ر-ف was treated at the thirty-third verse.** **And the link tabulated there is completed here:** at 33 *atrafnāhum* (We gave), at 64 *mutrafīhim* (those who live in ease) — **the same root, once in the giving and once in the taking.**

**And the word order must be recorded:** what is seized with the punishment is **not the whole people but *mutrafīhim*** — those among them who possess ease.

**I give this as an observation, its ground being that the word comes in a genitive construction:** *mutrafī**him*** — "those of them who live in ease". **The verse, that is, marks off one part from within a group.** The same distinction had been made for *al-malaʾ* in the twenty-fourth, thirty-third and forty-sixth verses.

### يَجْـَٔرُونَ — root: ج-أ-ر

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: the bellowing of cattle; and, generally, to shout aloud calling for help.** *Juʾār* — bellowing.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs once more in the Qurʾān, at al-Naḥl 16/53 (*thumma idhā massakumu'l-ḍurru fa-ilayhi tajʾarūn*).

**And the choice of verb is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being that the root rests on an animal's cry:** the verse does not say *yadʿūn* (they call) or *yaṣrakhūn* (they scream). **The word chosen is the word used for a sound that has no words in it.**

**And the sixty-fifth verse forbids this sound: *lā tajʾarū'l-yawm*.** **The form of the prohibition resembles the pattern treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/14** — there too the cry (*thubūr*) was not forbidden; they were told to multiply it. **The difference here must be recorded:** al-Furqān multiplies the cry, **al-Muʾminūn cuts it off** and gives a ground: *innakum minnā lā tunṣarūn.*

**And the word order of the ground must be recorded: *minnā* has been brought forward** — "against Us — you will not be helped". **The impossibility of help, that is, is explained not by the absence of a helper but by who the other party is.**

---

## 23/66-67 — قَدْ كَانَتْ ءَايَٰتِى تُتْلَىٰ عَلَيْكُمْ فَكُنتُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَعْقَٰبِكُمْ تَنكِصُونَ · مُسْتَكْبِرِينَ بِهِۦ سَٰمِرًا تَهْجُرُونَ

*qad kānat āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum fa-kuntum ʿalā aʿqābikum tankiṣūn · mustakbirīna bihī sāmiran tahjurūn*

"**My signs were being recited to you, and you were drawing back on your heels — puffed up with pride against it, talking nonsense by night.**"

### قَدْ كَانَتْ ءَايَٰتِى تُتْلَىٰ عَلَيْكُمْ — and the weave with verse 105

**This sentence will return almost word for word in the hundred and fifth verse of the sūra:**

| Verse | Sentence | Where it is said |
|---|---|---|
| **66** | *qad kānat āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* — **in the indicative** | **In this world** — the scene of the seizing with punishment |
| **105** | *a-lam takun āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* — **in the interrogative** | **In the hereafter** — inside the Fire |

**The same sentence, once as a statement and once as a question.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sentence is said twice, and in both the same thing is recalled. **The sūra, that is, shows the same reminder being made at two different times** — one while there is still time, the other after it has passed.

### عَلَىٰٓ أَعْقَٰبِكُمْ تَنكِصُون

The root **ن-ك-ص**: **to go backwards, to turn back.** The lexicographers record the sense of the root as "to draw back from a work one has begun".

**أَعْقَاب** — the plural of *ʿaqib*: **heels.** The root **ع-ق-ب**: the back, the end of a thing. *ʿĀqiba* — outcome; *taʿqīb* — coming after; *ʿuqbā* — the end.

**The picture in the idiom must be recorded:** *ʿalā aʿqābikum tankiṣūn* — **to go backwards on one's heels.** That is, **not to turn and flee**; to withdraw while the face stays turned the same way.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this picture in the idiom:** the movement is not a turning away but **a drawing back.** Moving off while still looking at what is being said.

**The same idiom occurs elsewhere in the Qurʾān as well** — Āl ʿImrān 3/144 (*inqalabtum ʿalā aʿqābikum*), al-Anfāl 8/48, al-Māʾida 5/21.

### مُسْتَكْبِرِينَ بِهِ — the disagreement over the pronoun's referent

**The exegetes differ over what the pronoun *bihī* in the sentence refers back to, and this is the most disputed place in the verse:**

| Reading | What *bihī* is | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The Qurʾān / the signs** | "Puffed up with pride against it" |
| 2 | **The Ḥaram / the Sacred Mosque** | "Puffed up with pride by it (by the guardianship of the Ḥaram)" |
| 3 | **The Prophet** | "Puffed up with pride concerning him" |
| 4 | **The night talk (*samar*)** | *Bihī* attaches to the following word |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**It is transmitted that the second reading has a historical ground:** the Meccans counted the service of the Kaʿba a mark of superiority. **I pass this historical frame on as it is transmitted.**

### سَٰمِرًا — root: س-م-ر

**The concrete sense of the root: to converse by night.** *Samar* — the darkness of the night and the talk carried on in that darkness; *sāmir* — one who talks by night; *summār* — night talkers.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.**

**The word order must be recorded: *sāmiran* is singular, yet it comes as a circumstantial accusative to a plural.** The lexicographers explain this by counting it **a generic noun**: a singular form reporting a group.

**And the scene the word carries is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being the "night" sense of the root:** the verse describes not a debate but **a gathering for talk at night.** Not a place where anything is decided, **a place where things are said.**

### تَهْجُرُون — the variant reading and the root

The root **ه-ج-ر**: **to part, to abandon; and to speak deliriously (*hujr*).** **The root was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/30 (*ittakhadhū hādhā'l-qurʾāna mahjūrā*), at `073-muzzemmil.md` 73/10 and at `074-muddessir.md` 74/5.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**The variant reading must be recorded, and it bears on the meaning:**

| Reading | Pattern | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| ***tahjurūn*** | Form I | "**You abandon it**" or "**you speak deliriously**" |
| ***tuhjirūn*** | Form IV | "**You speak foully**" — *ihjār*, to utter obscenity |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And at `025-furkan.md` 25/30 the two branches of this root were tabulated:**

| Reading | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Abandoned** — not recited, not put into practice |
| 2 | **Spoken of with nonsense** — from the *hujr* branch of the root |

**I rely on that treatment.** **And what belongs here is the comparison of the two verses:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/30 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/67** |
|---|---|---|
| Who says it | **The messenger** — a complaint to his Lord | **The text** — addressing the one spoken to |
| Pattern | *mahjūrā* — **a passive participle** | *tahjurūn* — **an imperfect verb** |
| Object | *hādhā'l-qurʾān* — **named openly** | **Not named** — not even a pronoun |
| What it reports | **A state** — the condition of the Book | **An act** — one done continually |

**The same root, in two sūras, around the same subject.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

---

## 23/68-70 — أَفَلَمْ يَدَّبَّرُوا۟ ٱلْقَوْلَ أَمْ جَآءَهُم مَّا لَمْ يَأْتِ ءَابَآءَهُمُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · أَمْ لَمْ يَعْرِفُوا۟ رَسُولَهُمْ فَهُمْ لَهُۥ مُنكِرُونَ · أَمْ يَقُولُونَ بِهِۦ جِنَّةٌۢ بَلْ جَآءَهُم بِٱلْحَقِّ وَأَكْثَرُهُمْ لِلْحَقِّ كَٰرِهُونَ

*a-fa-lam yaddabbarū'l-qawla am jāʾahum mā lam yaʾti ābāʾahumu'l-awwalīn · am lam yaʿrifū rasūlahum fa-hum lahū munkirūn · am yaqūlūna bihī jinnatun bal jāʾahum bi'l-ḥaqqi wa aktharuhum li'l-ḥaqqi kārihūn*

"**Have they not pondered the word? Or has something come to them that did not come to their forefathers of old? Or did they not recognise their messenger, so that they reject him? Or do they say 'there is madness in him'? No — he brought them the truth, but most of them are averse to the truth.**"

### Four questions — how the pattern is built

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the three verses:** one question (*a-fa-lam*), and after it **three *am* questions.** In Arabic *am* brings an alternative to the first question.

| Order | Verse | Question | What it tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 68 | *a-fa-lam yaddabbarū'l-qawl* | **Have they not pondered** — cause: inattention |
| 2 | 68 | *am jāʾahum mā lam yaʾti ābāʾahum* | **Has something new come** — cause: unfamiliarity |
| 3 | 69 | *am lam yaʿrifū rasūlahum* | **Do they not recognise him** — cause: strangeness |
| 4 | 70 | *am yaqūlūna bihī jinna* | **Do they call him mad** — cause: unfitness |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the four questions:** four possible grounds are counted off one by one and **not one of them is confirmed.** Then comes *bal* — **the particle that cancels what went before and sets down the real cause** (treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/11).

**And the real cause set down is not a lack of knowledge:** *wa aktharuhum li'l-ḥaqqi **kārihūn*** — **"most of them are averse to the truth."**

**This is the same line treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/213 and `042-sura.md` 42/14:** the cause of the division is not knowledge but disposition. **I rely on that treatment.**

### يَدَّبَّرُوا۟ — root: د-ب-ر

**The concrete sense of the root: the back, the end of a thing.** *Dubur* — the back; *adbara* — he turned his back; *tadabbur* — **to look at the back, at the end, of a thing.**

**The word occurs in exactly this setting in several places in the Qurʾān:** al-Nisāʾ 4/82 (*a-fa-lā yatadabbarūna'l-qurʾān*), Muḥammad 47/24 (treated in `047-muhammed.md`), Ṣād 38/29 (*li-yaddabbarū āyātih*; treated in `038-sad.md`).

**And the object of the verb here must be recorded: *al-qawl* — "the word".** Not the Qurʾān, not the signs. **The question, that is, looks not at the name of the text but at the thing said itself.**

**I record this as an observation:** the choice of word does not ask the one addressed **to accept the source** — **only to think what is said through to the end.**

**The pattern must be recorded:** *yaddabbarū*, its original being *yatadabbarū*; the *tāʾ* has been assimilated to the *dāl*. **Among the variant readings *yatadabbarū* is also transmitted.** It does not change the meaning.

### أَمْ جَآءَهُم مَّا لَمْ يَأْتِ ءَابَآءَهُمُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ

**This construction occurred in the twenty-fourth verse in the mouth of Noah's people:** *mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī **ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn**.* **And it was tabulated there.**

**What belongs here is this, and I give it as my own reading:** in the twenty-fourth verse it was **the ground of the refusal** — "we did not hear of it among our forefathers". **In the sixty-eighth the same expression is turned into a question** — "or has something come that did not come to their forefathers?"

**The question, that is, tests the assumption the ground rests on.** And the sūra has already refuted that assumption from the twenty-third verse onwards: **the same call has been repeated since Noah.**

### أَمْ لَمْ يَعْرِفُوا۟ رَسُولَهُمْ

The root **ع-ر-ف**: **to recognise.** The lexicographers record a distinction between *maʿrifa* and *ʿilm*: *maʿrifa* is **the recognising again of something already known**; *ʿilm* is general.

**And *rasūlahum* — "their own messenger".** The possessive pronoun must be recorded: the messenger belongs to them, **he did not come from outside.** **The same qualification was set in the thirty-second verse:** *rasūlan **minhum***.

**I record this as an observation:** the question tests not the messenger's identity but **whether they recognise him.** **And it cannot be said that they do not, because the messenger is one of them.** The question is therefore put in order to remain unanswered.

### أَمْ يَقُولُونَ بِهِۦ جِنَّةٌ

**This construction was used for Noah in the twenty-fifth verse — in exactly the same two words:** *bihī jinna.* **And it was tabulated there.**

**What belongs here is this:** the fourth question is taken from the narrative the sūra itself has told. **The sentence used in putting the question, that is, was given thirty-five verses earlier in the mouth of Noah's people.**

### بَلْ جَآءَهُم بِٱلْحَقِّ وَأَكْثَرُهُمْ لِلْحَقِّ كَٰرِهُونَ

**The word order must be recorded:** the word *al-ḥaqq* occurs **twice** — once as what is brought, once as what is disliked. **And the second has been brought forward:** *wa aktharuhum **li'l-ḥaqqi** kārihūn.*

The root **ك-ر-ه**: **to dislike, not to want; and compulsion (*ikrāh*).** The lexicographers record under this root both **an unwillingness that comes from within** and **a compulsion that comes from without**.

**And the qualification *aktharuhum* must be recorded: "most of them".** Not all.

**This is an instance, verifiable from the text, of the principle recorded in `STYLE.md` that no blanket ruling is issued.** **A table was built for the same point at the end of `037-saffat.md`** (the entries on *aktharu'l-awwalīn*). **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 23/71 — وَلَوِ ٱتَّبَعَ ٱلْحَقُّ أَهْوَآءَهُمْ لَفَسَدَتِ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتُ وَٱلْأَرْضُ وَمَن فِيهِنَّ بَلْ أَتَيْنَٰهُم بِذِكْرِهِمْ فَهُمْ عَن ذِكْرِهِم مُّعْرِضُونَ

*wa lawi'ttabaʿa'l-ḥaqqu ahwāʾahum la-fasadati'l-samāwātu wa'l-arḍu wa man fīhinn, bal ataynāhum bi-dhikrihim fa-hum ʿan dhikrihim muʿriḍūn*

"**If the truth had followed their desires, the heavens and the earth and all in them would have gone to ruin. No — We brought them their own reminder, and they turn away from their own reminder.**"

### ٱلْحَقّ — the disagreement over the referent

**The exegetes differ over what *al-ḥaqq*, the subject of the sentence, is, and the difference changes how the proof works:**

| Reading | What *al-Ḥaqq* is | The sense of the sentence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **God** — *al-Ḥaqq* is one of the names | "If God had followed their desires…" |
| 2 | **The Qurʾān / the revelation** | "If the revelation had come according to their desires…" |
| 3 | **The truth, what is real, what is right** — in the abstract | "If the real had been subject to their desires…" |
| 4 | **Justice / order** | "If the order had been set up according to their desires…" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And in all four the logic of the proof is the same, and this must be recorded:** **an order subject to desire ceases to be an order.** For desires are many and contradict one another; they cannot all be followed at once.

### لَفَسَدَتِ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتُ وَٱلْأَرْضُ — the form of the proof

The root **ف-س-د**: **to spoil, to lose one's order.** *Fasād* — the falling apart of an order; *ifsād* — corrupting. **The word was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/11-12 and at `038-sad.md` 38/28.**

**Another proof built with the same verb was treated at `021-enbiya.md` 21/22:** *law kāna fīhimā ālihatun illā'llāhu **la-fasadatā***, "had there been in the two of them gods besides God, the two would have gone to ruin." **I rely on that treatment.**

**The two proofs can be compared:**

| | `021-enbiya.md` **21/22** | **al-Muʾminūn 23/71** |
|---|---|---|
| The hypothetical condition | **More than one god** | **The truth following desire** |
| The consequence | *la-fasadatā* — **the two would go to ruin** | *la-fasadati'l-samāwātu wa'l-arḍu **wa man fīhinn*** |
| Scope | **Sky and earth** | **Sky, earth and all in them** |
| What it is built against | **Association of partners** | ***Hawā*** |

**The difference must be recorded: the proof of al-Anbiyāʾ is built against *shirk*, that of al-Muʾminūn against *hawā*.** And the sentence in al-Anbiyāʾ ends with *subḥāna'llāhi rabbi'l-ʿarshi ʿammā yaṣifūn*; **in al-Muʾminūn the same sentence of *tanzīh* will come twenty verses later, in the ninety-first verse.**

**And al-Muʾminūn's own counterpart will come in the ninety-first verse:** *law kāna maʿahū min ilāhin idhan la-dhahaba kullu ilāhin bimā khalaqa wa la-ʿalā baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ.* **The sūra, that is, builds two hypothetical proofs:** one by way of *hawā* (71), the other by way of multiplicity (91). **This is a pairing that can be verified within the sūra.**

### أَهْوَآء — root: ه-و-ي

**The root was treated in detail at `045-casiye.md` 45/23 (*a-fa-raʾayta mani'ttakhadha ilāhahū hawāh*)** — where the two readings of the construction *ilāhahū hawāh* were tabulated. **And the same sentence was taken up again at `025-furkan.md` 25/43**, where the analysis in al-Jāthiya was relied on. **I do not repeat it here.**

**The summary of the root is this:** **ه-و-ي**: **to fall, to go down; and to feel desire.** Both branches are recorded.

**What belongs here is that the word comes in the plural: *ahwāʾ*.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this plural:** in al-Jāthiya *hawāh* was **singular** — the desire of one person. **Here it is plural: the desires of a community.** **And the working of the proof depends precisely on this plural:** a single desire can be followed; **desires that contradict one another cannot all be followed at once.** This is the ground of the ruin.

| Place | Word | Number | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `045-casiye.md` 45/23 | *hawāh* | **Singular** | **Individual** — being sealed |
| `025-furkan.md` 25/43 | *hawāh* | **Singular** | **Individual** — the limit of responsibility |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/71** | *ahwāʾahum* | **Plural** | **Cosmic** — the ruin of the heavens and the earth |

**Three occurrences, the same root, the scale growing.** This is a series that can be verified from the three texts.

### بَلْ أَتَيْنَٰهُم بِذِكْرِهِمْ

**And the second half of the sentence sets a separate question.**

**ذِكْرِهِمْ** — the possessive pronoun must be recorded: **"their reminder".** It does not say the Qurʾān or the revelation.

**The exegetes differ over the sense of the construction:**

| Reading | What *dhikruhum* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **A reminder belonging to them** — the reminder sent down for them |
| 2 | **Their own honour, their being remembered** — the sense of *dhikr* as "renown, standing" (at al-Zukhruf 43/44, *wa innahū la-dhikrun laka wa li-qawmik*) |
| 3 | **A book coming in their own tongue** |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**The second reading is worth recording, because it sharpens the sentence:** if what is brought is their **own standing**, then what they turn away from is what is in their favour.

### فَهُمْ عَن ذِكْرِهِم مُّعْرِضُون — the weave with the third verse

**And here another verbal weave of the sūra is completed.**

**In the third verse the item of the list ran:** *wa'lladhīna hum **ʿani'l-laghwi muʿriḍūn*** — "those who turn away from idle speech". **The same pattern returns here unchanged:** *fa-hum **ʿan dhikrihim muʿriḍūn***.

| Verse | Pattern | What is turned away from | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **3** | *hum **ʿani'l-laghwi** muʿriḍūn* | **Idle speech** | **An attribute** |
| **71** | *hum **ʿan dhikrihim** muʿriḍūn* | **Their own reminder** | **A fault** |

**Exactly the same word order, the same active participle, two different objects.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and it was recorded at the third verse as well.

**I give this as my own reading:** the sūra takes a pattern from its own list of attributes and uses it for the other side — **and changes the object.** **The verb is the same; what settles the verdict is what is being turned away from.**

---

## 23/72 — أَمْ تَسْـَٔلُهُمْ خَرْجًا فَخَرَاجُ رَبِّكَ خَيْرٌ وَهُوَ خَيْرُ ٱلرَّٰزِقِينَ

*am tasʾaluhum kharjan fa-kharāju rabbika khayr, wa huwa khayru'l-rāziqīn*

"**Or are you asking them for a payment? Your Lord's return is better; and He is the best of providers.**"

### The fifth *am* question

**The verse is the continuation of the series of *am* questions that began in the sixty-eighth verse** — the fifth question. **And its difference from the earlier ones must be recorded:** the first four tested the condition of the other side; **this one tests the condition of the messenger.**

**I record this as an observation:** one more possible obstacle is eliminated — **the messenger's having an interest of his own.**

### خَرْج and خَرَاج — the variant reading and the difference

**Not the root ح-ر-ج but خ-ر-ج: to go out.** *Kharj* — what is taken out, the price paid; *kharāj* — tax, revenue, yield.

**A variant reading is transmitted:**

| Reading | The two words |
|---|---|
| 1 | *kharjan* … *fa-**kharāju** rabbik* |
| 2 | *kharājan* … *fa-**kharāju** rabbik* — **both in the same pattern** |
| 3 | *kharjan* … *fa-**kharju** rabbik* — **both in the same pattern** |

**All three readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the lexicographers dwell on the difference between the two words in the first reading:**

| Word | The sense the lexicographers give |
|---|---|
| **خَرْج** | **A thing paid once**, a fee |
| **خَرَاج** | **A revenue that comes continually**, yield, tax |

**I pass this distinction on as it is transmitted.** **And on the first reading the sentence builds a comparison:** what might be asked for is **a single payment**, what is given in return is **continual.**

### The same question in other sūras

**This question is a repeated pattern in the Qurʾān:**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| `025-furkan.md` 25/57 | *mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin illā man shāʾa an yattakhidha ilā rabbihī sabīlā* |
| `042-sura.md` 42/23 | *lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi ajran illā'l-mawaddata fi'l-qurbā* |
| `068-kalem.md` 68/46 | *am tasʾaluhum ajran fa-hum min maghramin muthqalūn* |
| `052-tur.md` 52/40 | *am tasʾaluhum ajran fa-hum min maghramin muthqalūn* |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/72** | *am tasʾaluhum kharjan fa-kharāju rabbika khayr* |

**In all five places the same work is done:** it is recorded that the messenger has no interest of his own. **This pattern was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/57**, where the form of the exception was recorded: **what was asked for was not a thing that leaves the giver's hand.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the difference of al-Muʾminūn must be recorded:** al-Furqān and al-Shūrā build an **exception** (*illā*); al-Ṭūr and al-Qalam name the **burden** on the other side; **al-Muʾminūn, for its part, makes a comparison** — *fa-kharāju rabbika khayr.* **The refusal of the demand, that is, is grounded in the alternative's being better.**

### وَهُوَ خَيْرُ ٱلرَّٰزِقِين

**The third of the five superlative constructions in the sūra** (14, 29, 72, 109, 118).

The root **ر-ز-ق**: **what reaches a living being and is drawn benefit from.** The lexicographers do not confine the root to property; knowledge, health and children are counted *rizq* as well.

**And the construction's link with *al-khayr* must be recorded:** the verse uses the word *khayr* twice — once as a comparative (*khayrun*), once as a superlative (*khayru'l-rāziqīn*). **The same word, in two patterns.**

---

## 23/73-74 — وَإِنَّكَ لَتَدْعُوهُمْ إِلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ · وَإِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱلْءَاخِرَةِ عَنِ ٱلصِّرَٰطِ لَنَٰكِبُونَ

*wa innaka la-tadʿūhum ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm · wa inna'lladhīna lā yuʾminūna bi'l-ākhirati ʿani'l-ṣirāṭi la-nākibūn*

"**You are indeed calling them to a straight path. And those who do not believe in the hereafter are turning aside from the path.**"

### صِرَٰط مُّسْتَقِيم

**The construction was treated in detail in `001-fatiha.md`.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the difference of definiteness between the two verses, and it must be recorded:**

| Verse | Construction | Definiteness |
|---|---|---|
| **73** | *ṣirāṭin mustaqīm* | **Indefinite** |
| **74** | *ʿani**'l-ṣirāṭ*** | **Definite** |

**The seventy-fourth verse takes back with the article *alif-lām* the path named in the verse before.** In Arabic this is an operation called ***ʿahd dhikrī***: a thing first mentioned indefinitely is then repeated as definite. **The lexicographers record this consistently.**

### نَٰكِبُون — root: ن-ك-ب

**The concrete sense of the root: to swerve to one side, to slip sideways.** *Mankib* — **the shoulder** (the side of the trunk); *nakbāʾ* — the wind that blows from between the cardinal directions; *nakba* — a calamity (falling off the road).

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.**

**And a comparison with *tankiṣūn* in the sixty-sixth verse is worth recording, because the two words are close in sound but are separate roots:**

| Word | Root | The movement |
|---|---|---|
| *tankiṣūn* (66) | **ن-ك-ص** | **To go backwards** — on the same road, in the reverse direction |
| *nākibūn* (74) | **ن-ك-ب** | **To swerve to one side** — to go off the road |

**That the two roots are separate is certain, and I build no derivational link between them.** **The note of caution entered for roots close in sound in `080-abese.md` and `083-mutaffifin.md` holds here as well.**

**But that the two words stand in the same sūra, eight verses apart, is a datum, and I record it as an observation:** the sūra names **two distinct ways** of leaving the road — drawing back, and swerving aside. **The first is staying on the road and not advancing; the second is leaving the road.**

### وَإِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱلْءَاخِرَة

**And the condition must be recorded:** **not believing in the hereafter** is given as the ground of the swerving.

**This is on the same line as the finding recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/11:** *bal kadhdhabū bi'l-sāʿa* — **"that is not the real matter: they denied the Hour."** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The two sūras make the same diagnosis:** beneath the objections that are voiced lies **an unvoiced rejection of the hereafter.** **And al-Muʾminūn had done the same in the thirty-third verse:** *wa kadhdhabū bi-liqāʾi'l-ākhira.*

| Verse | Sentence | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **33** | *kadhdhabū bi-liqāʾi'l-ākhira* | **An unnamed people** |
| **74** | *lā yuʾminūna bi'l-ākhira* | **Those addressed** |

**The same diagnosis, in two places.**

---

## 23/75-76 — وَلَوْ رَحِمْنَٰهُمْ وَكَشَفْنَا مَا بِهِم مِّن ضُرٍّ لَّلَجُّوا۟ فِى طُغْيَٰنِهِمْ يَعْمَهُونَ · وَلَقَدْ أَخَذْنَٰهُم بِٱلْعَذَابِ فَمَا ٱسْتَكَانُوا۟ لِرَبِّهِمْ وَمَا يَتَضَرَّعُونَ

*wa law raḥimnāhum wa kashafnā mā bihim min ḍurrin la-lajjū fī ṭughyānihim yaʿmahūn · wa laqad akhadhnāhum bi'l-ʿadhābi fa-ma'stakānū li-rabbihim wa mā yataḍarraʿūn*

"**Were We to have mercy on them and lift the affliction that is upon them, they would persist in their transgression, wandering blindly. We seized them with the punishment, assuredly; and they did not bow to their Lord, nor do they entreat.**"

### What the two verses build together

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the two verses:** the seventy-fifth verse tries the case of **mercy**, the seventy-sixth the case of **punishment**. **In both cases the outcome is the same.**

| Verse | What is tried | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **75** | *raḥimnāhum wa kashafnā mā bihim min ḍurr* — **were the affliction lifted** | *la-lajjū fī ṭughyānihim* — **the obstinacy would continue** |
| **76** | *akhadhnāhum bi'l-ʿadhāb* — **they were seized with the punishment** | *fa-ma'stakānū … wa mā yataḍarraʿūn* — **they did not bow** |

**And the difference must be recorded:** the seventy-fifth verse is **hypothetical** (*law* — a condition that did not occur); the seventy-sixth reports **something that did happen** (*wa laqad*).

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the setting of these two moods side by side:** one tells an untried possibility, the other a tried fact. **And the two come out at the same result.** **The verse, that is, shows that the bearing does not depend on outward circumstances.**

### لَّلَجُّوا۟ — root: ل-ج-ج

**The concrete sense of the root: to hold out in a matter and keep at it; and the mingling of sounds, a din.** *Lajja* — he persisted; *lujja* — the deep middle of the sea (where the waves mingle together; at al-Naml 27/44, *lujjatan*); *lajāj* — obstinacy.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.**

**The link between the two branches of the root is worth recording, and I give this as an observation about the language:** both **the middle of the sea** and **obstinacy** come from the same root. The lexicographers build the link by way of **mingling and being sunk into**. **The word describes a person's sinking deeper into the state he is already in.**

### طُغْيَٰنِهِمْ يَعْمَهُون

The root **ط-غ-ي / ط-غ-و**: **to exceed the bound, to overflow.** *Ṭughyān* — the overflowing of water (al-Ḥāqqa 69/11 — *lammā ṭagha'l-māʾ*; treated in `069-hakka.md`). **The root was treated at `079-naziat.md` 79/17 and 79/37, and at `096-alak.md` 96/6.**

The root **ع-م-ه**: **to flounder in bewilderment, not to know what to do.** The lexicographers record a distinction between *ʿamā* (blindness of the eye) and *ʿamah* (bewilderment of discernment).

**And the construction occurs regularly in the Qurʾān:** al-Baqara 2/15 (*wa yamudduhum fī ṭughyānihim yaʿmahūn*), al-Anʿām 6/110, al-Aʿrāf 7/186, Yūnus 10/11.

**And the link between al-Baqara 2/15 and this verse is worth recording:** there the verb is *yamudduhum* (We extend), here *la-lajjū* (they would persist). **That verse was treated in `002-bakara.md`.**

### فَمَا ٱسْتَكَانُوا۟ — a disagreement over the root of the word

**The lexicographers differ over which root the word comes from:**

| View | Root | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **س-ك-ن** | *Istakāna* — Form X: **to grow still, to submit.** The alif is for lengthening |
| 2 | **ك-ي-ن / ك-و-ن** | From *kāna* — **to be brought low, to be made small**; *al-kawn* (a low position) |

**Both views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **On both the sense is close: to submit, to be brought low.**

**And the root س-ك-ن occurred in the eighteenth verse of this sūra** (*fa-askannāhu fi'l-arḍ*). **If the first view is right, the same root is used twice in the sūra, once for water and once for a human being.** I give this as an entry; **since the disagreement over the root is unresolved, I draw no conclusion from it.**

### وَمَا يَتَضَرَّعُونَ — the change of tense

The root **ض-ر-ع**: **to be brought low, to be weak and submissive.** *Ḍarʿ* — the hanging of the udder; *taḍarruʿ* — Form V: **to entreat by lowering oneself.**

**And the word order must be recorded, and this is the finest point of the verse:** the two verbs come **in two different tenses.**

| Verb | Mood | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| *ma'stakānū* | **Perfect**, negated | **At that moment** they did not bow — a finished fact |
| *wa mā yataḍarraʿūn* | **Imperfect**, negated | **Still** they do not entreat — ongoing |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this difference of mood:** the verse reports first the reaction at that moment and then **the ongoing state.** **The punishment has passed, that is, but the bearing has not stopped.**

**And the same line is treated at al-Anʿām 6/43** (*fa-lawlā idh jāʾahum baʾsunā taḍarraʿū*). **In this commentary a similar word order was treated at `067-mulk.md` 67/21 (*bal lajjū fī ʿutuwwin wa nufūr*)** — there too the obstinacy came after an opportunity. **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 23/77 — حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا فَتَحْنَا عَلَيْهِم بَابًا ذَا عَذَابٍ شَدِيدٍ إِذَا هُمْ فِيهِ مُبْلِسُونَ

*ḥattā idhā fataḥnā ʿalayhim bāban dhā ʿadhābin shadīdin idhā hum fīhi mublisūn*

"**Until, when We open upon them a gate of severe punishment, at once they are stripped of hope in it.**"

### فَتَحْنَا عَلَيْهِم بَابًا — the choice of verb

The root **ف-ت-ح**: **to open.** **The root was treated in detail in `048-fetih.md` on the occasion of that sūra's name.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the use of the verb here is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** in the Qurʾān the pattern *fataḥnā ʿalayhim* is mostly used for **a favour** — al-Anʿām 6/44 (*fataḥnā ʿalayhim abwāba kulli shayʾ*), al-Aʿrāf 7/96 (*la-fataḥnā ʿalayhim barakātin mina'l-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ*).

**Here the same pattern is used for *a gate of punishment*.**

| Place | What is opened | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| al-Anʿām 6/44 | *abwāba kulli shayʾ* — **the gates of everything** | **A favour** |
| al-Aʿrāf 7/96 | *barakātin mina'l-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ* | **Blessings** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/77** | *bāban dhā ʿadhābin shadīd* | **Punishment** |

**The same pattern, the reverse content.** This is a contrast that can be verified from the three texts.

**And the continuation of al-Anʿām 6/44 must be recorded, because it lies on the same line:** there, after the gates of favour are opened, *akhadhnāhum baghtatan* follows. **In both verses, that is, the gate that opens is a turning point.**

**And the difference of singular and plural is worth recording:** in al-Anʿām *abwāb* (gates, plural), here *bāban* (a gate, singular and indefinite). **I give this as an observation about the word order.**

### مُبْلِسُون — root: ب-ل-س

**The sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: to have one's hope cut off, to be left helpless and bewildered; to find nothing to say.** *Iblās* — Form IV.

**And the lexicographers record that the name *Iblīs* is derived from this root** — "the one whose hope is cut off". **I pass this explanation on as it is transmitted; there are other views on the origin of the word** (a view that it comes from a non-Arabic name is transmitted) **and I do not impose a preference.**

**The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān:** al-Anʿām 6/44 (*fa-idhā hum **mublisūn***), al-Rūm 30/12 and 30/49 (treated in `030-rum.md`), al-Zukhruf 43/75 (treated in `043-zuhruf.md`).

**And the overlap between al-Anʿām 6/44 and this verse must be recorded:** both verses begin with *fataḥnā ʿalayhim* and both end with *mublisūn*. **This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.**

### إِذَا … إِذَا — two occurrences of *idhā*

**The word order must be recorded:** there are two separate occurrences of *idhā* in the sentence. **The first is the conditional particle** (*idhā fataḥnā*), **the second the *idhā fujāʾiyya*** — the particle reporting "all at once" (*idhā hum fīhi mublisūn*).

**The same structure was used in the sixty-fourth verse as well:** *ḥattā **idhā** akhadhnā mutrafīhim bi'l-ʿadhābi **idhā** hum yajʾarūn.*

**I compare the two verses:**

| | **64** | **77** |
|---|---|---|
| The condition | *akhadhnā mutrafīhim bi'l-ʿadhāb* | *fataḥnā ʿalayhim bāban dhā ʿadhābin shadīd* |
| The sudden result | *idhā hum **yajʾarūn*** — **bellowing** | *idhā hum fīhi **mublisūn*** — **hope cut off** |
| Sound | **Present** — bellowing | **Absent** — *iblās*, finding nothing to say |

**The difference is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** in the sixty-fourth verse there was a sound; **in the seventy-seventh the sound too is cut off.** At the core of the word *iblās* lies **finding nothing to say.** **Block VI closes with the cutting off of sound.**

---

## 23/78 — وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِىٓ أَنشَأَ لَكُمُ ٱلسَّمْعَ وَٱلْأَبْصَٰرَ وَٱلْأَفْـِٔدَةَ قَلِيلًا مَّا تَشْكُرُونَ

*wa huwa'lladhī anshaʾa lakumu'l-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾidah, qalīlan mā tashkurūn*

"**It is He who built up for you hearing and sight and hearts. How little you give thanks!**"

### The triad of hearing, sight and heart — the fifth link

**At `032-secde.md` 32/9 this triad was treated for the fourth time and a table of four links was built.** **The fifth link is here, and I extend the table:**

| Place | Sentence | Verb | The work of the triad |
|---|---|---|---|
| `046-ahkaf.md` 46/26 | *wa **jaʿalnā lahum** samʿan wa abṣāran wa afʾidatan fa-mā aghnā ʿanhum…* | *jaʿala* | **Given — it availed nothing** |
| `045-casiye.md` 45/23 | *wa **khatama** ʿalā samʿihī wa qalbihī wa **jaʿala** ʿalā baṣarihī ghishāwatan* | *khatama* / *jaʿala* | **Closed** — a seal and a veil |
| `041-fussilet.md` 41/20 | *shahida ʿalayhim samʿuhum wa abṣāruhum wa **julūduhum*** | *shahida* | **They bore witness** — the third is *skin* |
| `032-secde.md` 32/9 | *wa **jaʿala lakum** … qalīlan mā tashkurūn* | *jaʿala* | **Given — no thanks were given** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/78** | *wa huwa'lladhī **anshaʾa lakum** … qalīlan mā tashkurūn* | ***anshaʾa*** | **Built up — no thanks were given** |

**In all five places the same three gates are enumerated; in each place in a different condition.**

**The meaning of the ordering was recorded at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/26 and holds here too:** it goes from outside inward — **what is heard → what is seen → what is weighed within.** The root of the word **أَفْـِٔدَة** (**ف-ء-د**, the burning of an ember; *fuʾād*, the kindling side of the heart) was worked out there; **I do not repeat it here.**

**And *samʿ* is singular, *abṣār* and *afʾida* plural.** The lexicographers explain the difference by hearing being of a single kind; **I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.** **In all five of the five verses the same singular-plural arrangement is kept** (in Fuṣṣilat it differs because the third is *julūd*).

### al-Sajda 32/9 and al-Muʾminūn 23/78 — the difference of verb

**These two verses are the closest to one another of the five links: both end with the same sentence (*qalīlan mā tashkurūn*).** **The difference between them lies in the verb, and it must be recorded:**

| | `032-secde.md` 32/9 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/78** |
|---|---|---|
| The verb | ***jaʿala*** — put from one state into another | ***anshaʾa*** — **set up, built** |
| The build of the sentence | *wa jaʿala lakum* — **a conjunction to the preceding sentence** | *wa huwa'lladhī anshaʾa lakum* — **an independent nominal sentence** |
| What precedes | *sawwāhu wa nafakha fīhi min rūḥih* — **the forming of the human being** | *idhā hum fīhi mublisūn* (77) — **the scene of punishment** |
| What follows | *wa qālū a-idhā ḍalalnā fi'l-arḍ* — **the objection about resurrection** | *wa huwa'lladhī dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍ* (79) — **two further proofs** |

**The difference of verb is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** *jaʿala* reports **putting what exists into a state**; *anshaʾa* **setting up, standing upright.** **This sense of the root was worked out in `056-vakia.md`**, where it was recorded: **the verb reports a *rebuilding*.**

**And the root has five occurrences in this sūra (14, 19, 31, 42, 78) — tabulated at the nineteenth verse.** **The fifth occurrence is here, and it is the last row of the table:** the sūra tells of the organs with the same verb it used for the human being, the gardens and the generations.

### قَلِيلًا مَّا تَشْكُرُون

**The word order was treated at `032-secde.md` 32/9 and holds here too:** *qalīlan* has been **brought forward** (*taqdīm*); **and *mā* here is either a reinforcer of the negation or a particle of the verbal noun; both parsings are transmitted.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the place of the sentence within the sūra:** the seventy-seventh verse had ended with the cutting off of sound (*mublisūn*). **The seventy-eighth opens a new block by enumerating three gates that were given.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the two verses:** immediately after the scene of punishment, **the organs of sense and understanding** are named. **A link is built between the two verses: at the place where hope is cut off, the instruments that went unused are recalled.** This is a reading, and it is not binding.

---

## 23/79-80 — وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِى ذَرَأَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَإِلَيْهِ تُحْشَرُونَ · وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِى يُحْىِۦ وَيُمِيتُ وَلَهُ ٱخْتِلَٰفُ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَارِ أَفَلَا تَعْقِلُونَ

*wa huwa'lladhī dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍi wa ilayhi tuḥsharūn · wa huwa'lladhī yuḥyī wa yumītu wa lahu'khtilāfu'l-layli wa'l-nahār, a-fa-lā taʿqilūn*

"**It is He who scattered you over the earth, and to Him you will be gathered. It is He who gives life and gives death, and the alternation of night and day belongs to Him. Will you not use your reason?**"

### Three occurrences of *wa huwa'lladhī* — the repetition of the pattern

**The seventy-eighth, seventy-ninth and eightieth verses all three begin with the same pattern:** *wa huwa'lladhī.* **This is a fact that can be verified from the text.**

| Verse | What is given | The closing |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | **Hearing, sight, hearts** | *qalīlan mā tashkurūn* — **thanks** |
| **79** | **Being scattered over the earth** | *wa ilayhi tuḥsharūn* — **gathering** |
| **80** | **Giving life, giving death, night and day** | *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* — **reason** |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being that the three closings differ:** three verses are built on the same pattern but attached to three different places — **to thanks, to gathering, and to reason.** **The same proof, that is, is carried to three different conclusions.**

### ذَرَأَكُمْ — root: ذ-ر-أ

**The concrete sense of the root: to scatter as one scatters seed, to multiply.** The lexicographers describe the root as lying somewhere between **creating and scattering**.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān: al-Aʿrāf 7/179 (*wa laqad dharaʾnā li-jahannama*), al-Naḥl 16/13, al-Shūrā 42/11 (*yadhraʾukum fīh*; it occurred in `042-sura.md`), al-Mulk 67/24 (*alladhī dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍ*; treated in `067-mulk.md`).

**And the overlap between al-Mulk 67/24 and this verse must be recorded:** the two sentences are **almost exactly the same.**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| `067-mulk.md` 67/24 | *qul huwa'lladhī **dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍi wa ilayhi tuḥsharūn*** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/79** | *wa huwa'lladhī **dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍi wa ilayhi tuḥsharūn*** |

**The only difference is at the beginning: al-Mulk has the command *qul* (say), here it is absent.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

**And a detail of the word order is worth recording:** *fi'l-arḍ* (scattering over the earth) and *ilayhi tuḥsharūn* (being gathered to Him) **are opposite directions.** **A dispersal and a gathering.**

The root **ح-ش-ر**: **to bring together what is scattered.** **The root was worked out in `059-hasr.md` on the occasion of that sūra's name.** **I do not repeat it here.**

### وَلَهُ ٱخْتِلَٰفُ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَار

The root **خ-ل-ف**: **to be left behind; to take another's place.** *Ikhtilāf* — Form VIII: **coming one after another; and falling into disagreement.** Both senses come out of the root.

**And the sense here is the first: the alternation of night and day.**

**The same phenomenon was told with a different word at `025-furkan.md` 25/62:** *wa huwa'lladhī jaʿala'l-layla wa'l-nahāra **khilfatan** li-man arāda an yadhdhakkara aw arāda shukūrā.* **And it was treated there.**

**The two verses can be compared:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/62 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/80** |
|---|---|---|
| The word | *khilfatan* — **a noun** | *ikhtilāf* — **a verbal noun** |
| For what | *li-man arāda an yadhdhakkar* — **for one who wishes to take heed** | *lahū* — **it belongs to Him** |
| The closing | Two occurrences of *arāda* — **will** | *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* — **reason** |

**The same root, put to two different tasks in two sūras:** al-Furqān reports an opportunity, al-Muʾminūn an ownership.

**And *lahū* has been brought forward (*taqdīm*), and this must be recorded:** in Arabic the fronting of the predicate reports **specification** — "it belongs to Him alone".

### أَفَلَا تَعْقِلُونَ — the first of three questions

The root **ع-ق-ل**: **to bind.** *ʿIqāl* — the cord that binds a camel's knee; *ʿaql* — the bond that holds a person from swerving. **The root was treated at `036-yasin.md` 36/62 and `002-bakara.md` 2/44.**

**And this question is the herald of the sūra's series of three questions between verses eighty-four and eighty-nine.** There three separate closings will come: *a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn* (85), *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* (87), *fa-annā tusḥarūn* (89).

**The four closings must be recorded together:**

| Verse | Closing | What it calls to |
|---|---|---|
| **80** | *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* | **To binding** — reason |
| **85** | *a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn* | **To remembering** |
| **87** | *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* | **To guarding oneself** |
| **89** | *fa-annā tusḥarūn* | **Not a call but a finding** — how are you bewitched |

**This series can be verified from the text, and I interpret it as my own reading:** the three calls descend from reason to remembering, from remembering to guarding; **the fourth is not a call but a finding.** **The series closes not with a question but with an astonishment.**

---

## 23/81-83 — بَلْ قَالُوا۟ مِثْلَ مَا قَالَ ٱلْأَوَّلُونَ · قَالُوٓا۟ أَءِذَا مِتْنَا وَكُنَّا تُرَابًا وَعِظَٰمًا أَءِنَّا لَمَبْعُوثُونَ · لَقَدْ وُعِدْنَا نَحْنُ وَءَابَآؤُنَا هَٰذَا مِن قَبْلُ إِنْ هَٰذَآ إِلَّآ أَسَٰطِيرُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ

*bal qālū mithla mā qāla'l-awwalūn · qālū a-idhā mitnā wa kunnā turāban wa ʿiẓāman a-innā la-mabʿūthūn · laqad wuʿidnā naḥnu wa ābāʾunā hādhā min qablu in hādhā illā asāṭīru'l-awwalīn*

"**No — they said the same as those before them said: 'When we are dead and have become dust and bones, are we then to be raised? We were promised this before, we and our forefathers. This is nothing but the tales of the ancients.'**"

### بَلْ قَالُوا۟ مِثْلَ مَا قَالَ ٱلْأَوَّلُونَ — the sūra's own diagnosis

**And this is one of the most striking sentences of the sūra, because the text names the work it is itself doing.**

**In the thirty-fifth verse an unnamed people had said:** *a-yaʿidukum annakum idhā mittum wa kuntum turāban wa ʿiẓāman annakum mukhrajūn.*

**In the eighty-second verse those addressed say:** *a-idhā mitnā wa kunnā turāban wa ʿiẓāman a-innā la-mabʿūthūn.*

**The two sentences can be compared:**

| | **23/35** | **23/82** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | **The notables of an unnamed people** | **Those addressed** |
| The pattern | *a-yaʿidu**kum*** — **an objection in the third person** ("does he promise you") | *a-idhā **mitnā*** — **the first person** ("when we die") |
| The shared wording | *idhā mittum wa kuntum **turāban wa ʿiẓāman*** | *idhā mitnā wa kunnā **turāban wa ʿiẓāman*** |
| The last word | *mukhrajūn* — **brought out** | *mabʿūthūn* — **raised** |

**The shared portion is word for word.** **And the sūra says so openly in the eighty-first verse:** *bal qālū mithla mā qāla'l-awwalūn.*

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this exact overlap:** the sūra repeats here, in the mouths of those it addresses, a sentence it placed forty-seven verses earlier in the mouth of a people **and itself notes the repetition.** **The text, that is, does not merely tell the narrative as an example — it also itself establishes the overlap between the example and the present situation.**

### أَسَٰطِيرُ ٱلْأَوَّلِين

The root **س-ط-ر**: **to write line by line, to set in rows.** *Saṭr* — a line, a row; *asṭār* — lines. ***Asāṭīr*** — counted the plural of *usṭūra*: **stories strung together and told.**

**The root was treated at `054-kamer.md` 54/53 (*wa kullu ṣaghīrin wa kabīrin mustaṭar*) in comparison with ز-ب-ر** and worked out at `068-kalem.md` 68/1 (*wa'l-qalami wa mā yasṭurūn*). **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the construction occurred word for word at `025-furkan.md` 25/5:** *wa qālū **asāṭīru'l-awwalīna**'ktatabahā fa-hiya tumlā ʿalayhi bukratan wa aṣīlā.* **And it was tabulated there as the second of four objections.**

**The two verses can be compared:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/5 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/83** |
|---|---|---|
| What it is said of | **The Qurʾān** — its compilation is described | **The promise of resurrection** |
| Addition | *iktatabahā fa-hiya tumlā ʿalayh* — **the claim of dictation** | **None** |
| What precedes | *ifkun'ftarāh* — **the claim of invention** | *laqad wuʿidnā naḥnu wa ābāʾunā* — **its age** |

**The same construction, aimed at two different targets.**

**And the distribution of the word *al-awwalīn* across the sūra must be recorded:** 24 (*ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn*), 68 (*ābāʾahumu'l-awwalīn*), 81 (*al-awwalūn*), 83 (*asāṭīru'l-awwalīn*). **Four occurrences, all about "those who came before".**

**I record this as an observation:** the other side uses *awwalīn* as a **proof** (24, 83); the sūra uses it as a **diagnosis** (68, 81). **The same word, in two different offices.**

### لَقَدْ وُعِدْنَا نَحْنُ وَءَابَآؤُنَا هَٰذَا مِن قَبْلُ

**The logic of the objection must be recorded:** the **age** of the promise is counted the proof that it will not come about.

**I give this as an observation:** the same ground was used **the other way round** in the twenty-fourth verse — there its being unheard of was the fault, here its **having been heard many times** is the fault.

| Verse | Ground | What it rests on |
|---|---|---|
| **24** | *mā samiʿnā bi-hādhā fī ābāʾinā'l-awwalīn* | **Rejected because it is new** |
| **83** | *laqad wuʿidnā naḥnu wa ābāʾunā hādhā min qabl* | **Rejected because it is old** |

**The two grounds are the opposite of one another, and both are inside the sūra.** This is a contrast that can be verified from the text, and I interpret it as my own reading: **the refusal takes its ground from the circumstances** — the ground changes, the refusal does not. **This is the same as the finding recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/4-8.**

---

## 23/84-89 — Three questions, three answers

*qul li-mani'l-arḍu wa man fīhā in kuntum taʿlamūn · sa-yaqūlūna li'llāh, qul a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn · qul man rabbu'l-samāwāti'l-sabʿi wa rabbu'l-ʿarshi'l-ʿaẓīm · sa-yaqūlūna li'llāh, qul a-fa-lā tattaqūn · qul man bi-yadihī malakūtu kulli shayʾin wa huwa yujīru wa lā yujāru ʿalayhi in kuntum taʿlamūn · sa-yaqūlūna li'llāh, qul fa-annā tusḥarūn*

**قُل لِّمَنِ ٱلْأَرْضُ وَمَن فِيهَآ إِن كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ · سَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّهِ قُلْ أَفَلَا تَذَكَّرُونَ · قُلْ مَن رَّبُّ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ ٱلسَّبْعِ وَرَبُّ ٱلْعَرْشِ ٱلْعَظِيمِ · سَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّهِ قُلْ أَفَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · قُلْ مَنۢ بِيَدِهِۦ مَلَكُوتُ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ وَهُوَ يُجِيرُ وَلَا يُجَارُ عَلَيْهِ إِن كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ · سَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّهِ قُلْ فَأَنَّىٰ تُسْحَرُونَ**

"Say: '**To whom belong the earth and all in it, if you have knowledge?**' They will say: '**To God.**' Say: '**Will you not then take heed?**' Say: '**Who is the Lord of the seven heavens and the Lord of the mighty throne?**' They will say: '**God.**' Say: '**Will you not then guard yourselves?**' Say: '**In whose hand is the dominion of everything, who protects and against whom no one is protected, if you have knowledge?**' They will say: '**God.**' Say: '**How then are you bewitched?**'"

### The structure of the block — three tiers, one pattern

**Six verses make up three equal steps.** **Each step is in four parts, and this can be verified from the text:**

| Step | Question | Answer | The response |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** (84-85) | *li-**man**i'l-arḍu wa man fīhā* — **ownership** | *li'llāh* | *a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn* |
| **2** (86-87) | *man **rabbu**'l-samāwāti'l-sabʿ* — **lordship** | *li'llāh* | *a-fa-lā tattaqūn* |
| **3** (88-89) | *man **bi-yadihī malakūtu** kulli shayʾ* — **dominion** | *li'llāh* | *fa-annā tusḥarūn* |

**And there is a widening across the three questions, and I record this as my own reading, its ground being the objects of the questions:**

**One. The scope grows:** the earth → the seven heavens and the throne → **everything.**

**Two. The form of the question changes:** the first is *li-man* (to whom does it belong), the second and third *man* (who). **The first asks about ownership, that is, the others about identity.**

**Three. The responses descend:** taking heed → guarding oneself → astonishment. **Together with the *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* of 23/80 this makes four steps, and it was tabulated there.**

### سَيَقُولُونَ لِلَّهِ — the variant reading

**And here is the best known variant reading of the sūra.**

| Verse | The common reading | The other reading |
|---|---|---|
| **85** | *sa-yaqūlūna **li'llāh*** | — (agreed) |
| **87** | *sa-yaqūlūna **li'llāh*** | *sa-yaqūlūna **Allāh*** |
| **89** | *sa-yaqūlūna **li'llāh*** | *sa-yaqūlūna **Allāh*** |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **And the grammatical ground of the difference must be recorded, because the disagreement arises precisely from it:**

**In the eighty-fourth verse the question is in the pattern *li-man* (to whom does it belong); the answer matching that wording is *li'llāh* (to God).** **In the eighty-sixth and eighty-eighth verses the question is in the pattern *man* (who); the answer matching that wording is *Allāh*.**

**Hence:**

| Reading | Its ground |
|---|---|
| ***Allāh*** (87, 89) | **Matching the wording** — a *man* question is answered with *Allāh* |
| ***li'llāh*** (87, 89) | **Matching the sense** — since all three questions do the same work, the answer is unified |

**Both grounds are recorded by the lexicographers.** **It is transmitted that in the script of the muṣḥaf the spelling *li'llāh* stands in these verses, and that both readings are counted compatible with that script;** I do not go into the detail.

### مَلَكُوت — the pattern

The root **م-ل-ك**: **to possess, to rule.** *Mulk* — sovereignty; *malik* — sovereign; *malakūt* — **the widest form of sovereignty.**

**The lexicographers dwell on the ending *-ūt*:** in Arabic this ending, seen in words such as *raḥamūt*, *rahabūt* and *jabarūt*, reports **intensification**. **I pass this explanation on as it is transmitted.**

**The word occurs in four places in the Qurʾān:** al-Anʿām 6/75, al-Aʿrāf 7/185, Yā-Sīn 36/83 (treated in `036-yasin.md`), and this verse.

**And at `036-yasin.md` 36/83 (*fa-subḥāna'lladhī bi-yadihī malakūtu kulli shayʾ*) almost exactly the same construction occurs:**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| `036-yasin.md` 36/83 | *fa-subḥāna'lladhī **bi-yadihī malakūtu kulli shayʾin** wa ilayhi turjaʿūn* |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/88** | *man **bi-yadihī malakūtu kulli shayʾin** wa huwa yujīru…* |

**The shared portion is word for word.** **And the difference must be recorded:** in Yā-Sīn the construction stands inside a sentence of **glorification**, here inside a **question**. **The same expression, once a verdict and once a question.**

**And in `036-yasin.md` it was recorded that that verse is the last verse of the sūra.** I rely on that treatment.

### وَهُوَ يُجِيرُ وَلَا يُجَارُ عَلَيْهِ — an Arabian institution

The root **ج-و-ر**: **to be side by side, to be a neighbour; and to swerve (to slip aside from the road).** *Jār* — neighbour; *ijāra* — **taking someone under one's protection.**

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.**

**And the verb here points to a historical institution, and this must be recorded:** in the society into which the Qurʾān was revealed, ***jiwār*** (protection) was an established practice. A tribe or a man of influence would **take under his protection** someone who sought refuge with him, and that person was inviolable. **The protecting party was called *mujīr*, the protected one *jār*.**

**And the institution had two sides:** the one giving protection could guard the person under his protection; **but a stronger man could break that protection.**

**The verse negates precisely this second side:** *wa lā yujāru ʿalayh* — **"no protection can be given against Him."**

**I give this as an entry of historical background, and its ground is the institution itself:** the sentence uses a legal relation from the daily life of the one addressed. **And it removes exactly the limit of that relation.** I give the explanation resting on the known working of the institution.

**The same word occurs at al-Jinn 72/22 as well** (*lā yujīranī mina'llāhi aḥad*; treated in `072-cin.md`) **and at al-Aḥqāf 46/31-32** (treated in `046-ahkaf.md`).

### فَأَنَّىٰ تُسْحَرُونَ

**And the block closes not with a question but with an astonishment.**

***Annā* — "how, from where".** In Arabic this particle carries both "how" and "from where", and the lexicographers record both.

The root **س-ح-ر**: **to make a thing appear other than it is, to deceive.** The lexicographers record the root's link with **saḥar** (the last part of the night — the hour at which night and day are mingled and not told apart). **And *saḥr* — the region of the lungs and liver; the word is said to be derived from this as well.**

**The root was treated at `054-kamer.md` 54/2 (*siḥrun mustamirr*) and at `074-muddessir.md` 74/24 (*in hādhā illā siḥrun yuʾthar*).**

**And the voice of the verb must be recorded: *tusḥarūn* — passive.** "How are you bewitched" — **the one who bewitches is not named.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the passive voice:** the sentence points to no agent. **And the sūra had done this before:** *bal qulūbuhum fī ghamratin* (63) — there too what surrounds them was not named.

**And the logic of the sentence is worth recording:** all three questions have been answered correctly. **The astonishment, that is, arises not from ignorance but from *the mismatch between what is known and what is done*.** This can be verified from the build of the three steps.

---

## 23/90-92 — بَلْ أَتَيْنَٰهُم بِٱلْحَقِّ وَإِنَّهُمْ لَكَٰذِبُونَ · مَا ٱتَّخَذَ ٱللَّهُ مِن وَلَدٍ وَمَا كَانَ مَعَهُۥ مِنْ إِلَٰهٍ إِذًا لَّذَهَبَ كُلُّ إِلَٰهٍۭ بِمَا خَلَقَ وَلَعَلَا بَعْضُهُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ سُبْحَٰنَ ٱللَّهِ عَمَّا يَصِفُونَ · عَٰلِمِ ٱلْغَيْبِ وَٱلشَّهَٰدَةِ فَتَعَٰلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ

*bal ataynāhum bi'l-ḥaqqi wa innahum la-kādhibūn · ma'ttakhadha'llāhu min waladin wa mā kāna maʿahū min ilāhin idhan la-dhahaba kullu ilāhin bimā khalaqa wa la-ʿalā baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ, subḥāna'llāhi ʿammā yaṣifūn · ʿālimi'l-ghaybi wa'l-shahādati fa-taʿālā ʿammā yushrikūn*

"**No — We brought them the truth, and they are liars. God has not taken a child, nor is there any god with Him. Had it been so, each god would have gone off with what he created, and some of them would have risen over others. God is far above what they describe. He knows the unseen and the seen; He is exalted above what they associate with Him.**"

### How the hypothetical proof is built

**The proof works in this way:** more than one god is supposed, and then **the consequence of that supposition itself** is shown.

**And the consequence is given in two sentences:**

| Consequence | Expression | What it reports |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *la-dhahaba kullu ilāhin **bimā khalaqa*** | **Separation** — each takes off what he created |
| 2 | *wa la-**ʿalā** baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ* | **Conflict** — one rises over another |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the two sentences:** first **division**, then **the attempt to set up a hierarchy.** **The two together mean the disappearance of an order.**

**And its pairing with the proof in the seventy-first verse must be recorded:**

| Verse | Hypothetical condition | Consequence | The word |
|---|---|---|---|
| **71** | *lawi'ttabaʿa'l-ḥaqqu **ahwāʾahum*** | *la-fasadati'l-samāwātu wa'l-arḍ* | ***Fasād*** — ruin |
| **91** | *wa mā kāna maʿahū **min ilāh*** | *la-dhahaba kullu ilāhin … wa la-ʿalā baʿḍuhum* | ***Dhahāb* and *ʿuluww*** — going off, and rising above |

**Two proofs, in the same sūra, on the same pattern.** This is a pairing that can be verified within the sūra. **And it was recorded at the seventy-first verse:** the sūra builds two hypothetical proofs — one by way of *hawā*, the other by way of multiplicity.

### وَلَعَلَا بَعْضُهُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ

**The root ع-ل-و was treated at the forty-sixth verse, and its three occurrences were tabulated there** (46, 91, 116). **What belongs here is this:** in the forty-sixth verse Pharaoh's company were *qawman ʿālīn*. **Here the same verb is used of the supposed gods.**

**I record this as an observation:** the same root, once for human beings rising over one another, once for supposed gods rising over one another. **And in the hundred and sixteenth verse the same root will be used of God, as *taʿālā*.**

### سُبْحَٰنَ ٱللَّهِ عَمَّا يَصِفُونَ and فَتَعَٰلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ

**Two sentences come one after the other, and their structures are the same:** *[a word of tanzīh] + ʿammā + [an imperfect verb].*

| Verse | The word of tanzīh | Verb | From what |
|---|---|---|---|
| **91** | *subḥāna'llāh* | *yaṣifūn* — **they describe** | **Describing** |
| **92** | *fa-taʿālā* | *yushrikūn* — **they associate** | **Associating** |

**The second sentence comes with *fa* — that is, as the consequence of the first.** **And the verbs differ:** one is **speech** (describing), the other **act** (associating).

**A counterpart of these two sentences was treated in `037-saffat.md`** (37/159 — *subḥāna'llāhi ʿammā yaṣifūn*; 37/180 — *subḥāna rabbika rabbi'l-ʿizzati ʿammā yaṣifūn*), **where it was recorded that the second is an extension of the first.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded:** in al-Ṣāffāt both sentences ended with *yaṣifūn*; **in al-Muʾminūn the second turns to *yushrikūn*.** **The move, that is, is from speech to act.**

### عَٰلِمِ ٱلْغَيْبِ وَٱلشَّهَٰدَة

**The construction occurs regularly in the Qurʾān:** al-Anʿām 6/73, al-Tawba 9/94 and 9/105, al-Raʿd 13/9, al-Sajda 32/6 (it occurred in `032-secde.md`), al-Zumar 39/46 (it occurred in `039-zumer.md`), al-Ḥashr 59/22 (treated in `059-hasr.md`), al-Taghābun 64/18 (treated in `064-tegabun.md`), al-Jumuʿa 62/8 (it occurred in `062-cuma.md`).

The root **غ-ي-ب**: **to disappear from sight, to be veiled.** The root **ش-ه-د**: **to be present, to see, to bear witness.**

**And the work of the construction must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being the opposition of the two words:** the pair covers **the whole of what is known.** **Knowledge, that is, is not confined to one field.**

**And the lexicographers differ over its parsing:** if *ʿālimi* is read **in the genitive** it is an adjective to the name *Allāh* in the preceding verse; **if in the nominative** it is the predicate of a new sentence. **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### The close of block VII

**The block from seventy-eight to ninety-two is a whole, and its order is worth recording:**

| Verse | What |
|---|---|
| 78-80 | **Three proofs** — the organs, the scattering, life and death |
| 81-83 | **The objection** — and the finding that the objection is old |
| 84-89 | **Three questions, three answers** — what is accepted is enumerated |
| 90-92 | **The conclusion** — the denial of a child and of a partner |

**I give this as my own reading:** the block proceeds from **what the one addressed accepts.** **In all three questions the answer is given from their own mouths** (*sa-yaqūlūna li'llāh*). **And the conclusion is drawn out of those answers.** **The proof, that is, brings in no knowledge from outside** — it carries the addressee's own admission through to the end.

---

## 23/93-95 — قُل رَّبِّ إِمَّا تُرِيَنِّى مَا يُوعَدُونَ · رَبِّ فَلَا تَجْعَلْنِى فِى ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ · وَإِنَّا عَلَىٰٓ أَن نُّرِيَكَ مَا نَعِدُهُمْ لَقَٰدِرُونَ

*qul rabbi immā turiyannī mā yūʿadūn · rabbi fa-lā tajʿalnī fi'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn · wa innā ʿalā an nuriyaka mā naʿiduhum la-qādirūn*

"Say: '**My Lord, if You should show me what they are promised — my Lord, then do not place me among the wrongdoing people.**' **We are certainly able to show you what We promise them.**"

### How the prayer is built

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the two verses:** the prayer is **conditional** (*immā turiyannī*), and **what is asked for is not that it should not be shown.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the apodosis of the conditional clause:** the answer is not *fa-lā turinī* (do not show me) — it is ***fa-lā tajʿalnī fi'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn*** (do not place me among them). **The prayer asks, that is, not about the event but about the position at the moment of the event.**

**إِمَّا** — its original is *in* (the conditional) + *mā* (redundant, reinforcing). **And the doubled *nūn* at the end of the verb (*turiyanna*) is the heavy *nūn* of emphasis:** the condition is reinforced once more. **The condition, that is, is stressed twice — by the particle and by the ending of the verb.**

### رَبِّ … رَبِّ — the repetition of the vocative

**And the word order must be recorded:** the vocative *rabbi* occurs **twice** — once at the head of the conditional clause (93), once at the head of the apodosis (94).

**The lexicographers explain this repetition as *taḍarruʿ* (the intensifying of the entreaty);** **I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.** **And the repetition marks the verse boundary too:** two separate verses are the two halves of a single sentence.

### The chain of prayers in the sūra

**And this is the first link of a chain that begins in the sūra's last block:**

| Verse | Prayer | What is asked |
|---|---|---|
| **93-94** | *rabbi fa-lā tajʿalnī fi'l-qawmi'l-ẓālimīn* | **Position** — not to be among them |
| **97** | *rabbi aʿūdhu bika min hamazāti'l-shayāṭīn* | **Protection** — from promptings |
| **98** | *wa aʿūdhu bika rabbi an yaḥḍurūn* | **Protection** — from their being present |
| **118** | *rabbi'ghfir wa'rḥam wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | **Forgiveness and mercy** |

**All four prayers begin with the vocative *rabbi*.** **And the last verse of the sūra is the last link of this chain.** This is a series that can be verified from the text.

---

## 23/96 — ٱدْفَعْ بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ ٱلسَّيِّئَةَ نَحْنُ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا يَصِفُونَ

*idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya aḥsanu'l-sayyiʾa, naḥnu aʿlamu bimā yaṣifūn*

"**Repel the evil with what is best. We know best what they describe.**"

### The word order of the sentence

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse:** the object (*al-sayyiʾa*) **has been thrown to the end.** The normal order would be *idfaʿi'l-sayyiʾata bi'llatī hiya aḥsan*.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this postponement:** the sentence says first **how it is to be repelled**, and then **what is to be repelled.** **The emphasis, that is, is on the instrument, not on what is met.**

### بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَن — the qualified noun left unsaid

**The construction is a relative clause, and the noun it qualifies has been elided.** The lexicographers usually supply it as *bi'l-khaṣlati'llatī hiya aḥsan* (with the quality that is best) or *bi'l-dafʿati'llatī hiya aḥsan*.

**And the elision itself is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** the verse does not say **what is to be done.** **It sets only a measure: *aḥsan* — that which is best.** **For this reason the command does not give a list of behaviours; it gives a measure to be worked out afresh in each case.**
