**The same sentence occurs in a fuller form at Fuṣṣilat 41/34:** *idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya aḥsanu fa-idhā'lladhī baynaka wa baynahū ʿadāwatun ka-annahū waliyyun ḥamīm.*

**The two verses can be compared:**

| | **Fuṣṣilat 41/34** | **al-Muʾminūn 23/96** |
|---|---|---|
| What precedes | *wa lā tastawi'l-ḥasanatu wa lā'l-sayyiʾa* — **they are not equal** | *wa innā ʿalā an nuriyaka…* (95) — **a statement of power** |
| What follows | *fa-idhā'lladhī baynaka wa baynahū ʿadāwatun…* — **an outcome is reported** | *naḥnu aʿlamu bimā yaṣifūn* — **knowledge is reported** |
| What is promised | **The turning of enmity into friendship** | **Nothing** |

**The difference is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** Fuṣṣilat promises an **outcome** along with the command; **al-Muʾminūn promises no outcome.** In its place it says *naḥnu aʿlamu* — **"We know."**

**The ground of the command here, that is, is not the expectation that the other side will change; it is the knowledge that what is said is on record.**

### نَحْنُ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا يَصِفُون

**And the construction comes from the same root (و-ص-ف) as *ʿammā yaṣifūn* in the ninety-first verse. There are four verses between the two.**

| Verse | Sentence | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **91** | *subḥāna'llāhi ʿammā yaṣifūn* | **The descriptions given of God** |
| **96** | *naḥnu aʿlamu bimā yaṣifūn* | **The same descriptions — it is reported that they are known** |

**The same verb, with two different responses:** one *tanzīh*, the other knowledge. **This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.**

---

## 23/97-98 — وَقُل رَّبِّ أَعُوذُ بِكَ مِنْ هَمَزَٰتِ ٱلشَّيَٰطِينِ · وَأَعُوذُ بِكَ رَبِّ أَن يَحْضُرُونِ

*wa qul rabbi aʿūdhu bika min hamazāti'l-shayāṭīn · wa aʿūdhu bika rabbi an yaḥḍurūn*

"**And say: 'My Lord, I take refuge with You from the promptings of the satans. And I take refuge with You, my Lord, from their being present with me.'**"

### Two acts of taking refuge — and the difference between them

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the core of the two verses:** *aʿūdhu bika* is repeated **twice**, and **the second taking of refuge is not the continuation of the first but something different from it.**

| Verse | What refuge is taken from | How concrete |
|---|---|---|
| **97** | *hamazāti'l-shayāṭīn* — **their promptings** | **An act** |
| **98** | *an yaḥḍurūn* — **their being present** | **Their mere presence** |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the object of the second taking of refuge:** the second request asks protection not from an act but from **being present.** **The taking of refuge, that is, draws back a step: not only from the effect but from the nearness as well.**

### هَمَزَات — root: ه-م-ز

**The root was worked out in `104-humeze.md`.** **I do not repeat it here.** The description given there:

> **همز — root ه-م-ز.** Its primary sense in the dictionary is **to press, to squeeze, to break, to prod.** To press down on a thing and crush it. **The name of the letter *hamza* in the Arabic alphabet comes from this as well:** a sound produced by a constriction and a break in the throat.

**I rely on that treatment.** **What belongs here is that the word is plural and a verbal noun.**

***Hamazāt* — the plural of *hamza*; the plural of a *maṣdar marra* (a single instance of the act).** **The word reports individual prods**, that is, not a continuous state.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this form of the pattern:** what refuge is taken from is not a siege but **prods that come one at a time.** **And the sense "to press down and crush" recorded in `104-humeze.md` gives the picture of a prod that has a place in the body** — light, but a physical contact.

### أَن يَحْضُرُون — the absence of a passive

The root **ح-ض-ر**: **to be present, to be at hand.** *Ḥāḍir* — the one present; *muḥḍar* — the one brought.

**The root was treated in three places in `037-saffat.md` (37/57, 127, 158)**, where it was tabulated that the pattern is used in opposite directions. **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the *nūn* at the end of the verb must be recorded:** *an yaḥḍurūn* — its original is *an yaḥḍurūni*; the final *yāʾ* (the first-person pronoun) has been dropped to fit the verse-ending. **The same operation will be seen at the ninety-ninth verse (*irjiʿūn*) and at the hundred and eighth (*lā tukallimūn*).**

**This is a pattern of sound repeated in the last section of the sūra, and it can be verified from the text.**

### The place of the two verses within the sūra

**And an entry on context:** the ninety-sixth verse was a command turned **outward** (*idfaʿ* — repel). **The ninety-seventh and ninety-eighth are two prayers turned inward.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the order of the two verses:** immediately after the command to repel evil with what is best, two protections are asked **for the one who does the repelling.** **While the command is given, that is, the risk to the one who carries it out is named as well.**

---

## 23/99-100 — حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا جَآءَ أَحَدَهُمُ ٱلْمَوْتُ قَالَ رَبِّ ٱرْجِعُونِ · لَعَلِّىٓ أَعْمَلُ صَٰلِحًا فِيمَا تَرَكْتُ كَلَّآ إِنَّهَا كَلِمَةٌ هُوَ قَآئِلُهَا وَمِن وَرَآئِهِم بَرْزَخٌ إِلَىٰ يَوْمِ يُبْعَثُونَ

*ḥattā idhā jāʾa aḥadahumu'l-mawtu qāla rabbi'rjiʿūn · laʿallī aʿmalu ṣāliḥan fīmā taraktu, kallā, innahā kalimatun huwa qāʾiluhā, wa min warāʾihim barzakhun ilā yawmi yubʿathūn*

"**Until, when death comes to one of them, he says: 'My Lord, send me back — that I may do a righteous deed in what I have left behind!' No. It is a word he speaks, no more. And behind them is a barrier until the day they are raised.**"

### حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا — the third turn

**It was tabulated at the sixty-fourth verse:** the pattern occurs three times in the sūra (64, 77, 99). **This is the third and the most final of them** — because the others tell of a punishment, this one tells of **death.**

### رَبِّ ٱرْجِعُونِ — a plural imperative

**And this is the most disputed place in the verse:** the address is singular (*rabbi* — "my Lord"), **the imperative plural** (*irjiʿūn* — "send me back", addressed to more than one).

**The lexicographers explain this mismatch in several ways:**

| Reading | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | **A plural of respect (*taʿẓīm*)** — in Arabic the plural is used for honour |
| 2 | **A plural for repetition** — it stands in place of saying "send me back, send me back, send me back" |
| 3 | *Rabbi* is a vocative; ***irjiʿūn* is addressed to the angels** — to those who take the soul |
| 4 | The words are spoken in a moment of bewilderment, out of order |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the *nūn* at the end of the verb arises, as recorded at the ninety-eighth verse, from the dropping of the first-person *yāʾ*:** its original is *irjiʿūnī.*

### لَعَلِّىٓ أَعْمَلُ صَٰلِحًا فِيمَا تَرَكْتُ

***Fīmā taraktu* — "in what I have left".** The lexicographers differ over the construction:

| Reading | What *mā taraktu* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The property he left behind** — to do good with it |
| 2 | **The works he left undone** — what he neglected |
| 3 | **The world he has left** — in general |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

### كَلَّا إِنَّهَا كَلِمَةٌ هُوَ قَآئِلُهَا

**كَلَّا** — a particle of rejection and rebuke. **The lexicographers explain the word as "no, it is not so"** and record that in the Qurʾān it mostly occurs in the rejection of a request or a claim. **The word was treated in `075-kiyame.md`, `080-abese.md` and `102-tekasur.md`.**

**إِنَّهَا كَلِمَةٌ هُوَ قَآئِلُهَا** — the lexicographers differ over the sense of the sentence:

| Reading | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 1 | "**This is only a word he speaks**" — that is, it has no effect, an empty word |
| 2 | "**He will go on saying it**" — the active participle *qāʾiluhā* reports continuance |
| 3 | "**This word tells against him**" — saying it does not lift the responsibility |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the word order must be recorded:** *huwa qāʾiluhā* — **a nominal sentence and an active participle.** Had a verb been used (*yaqūluhā*) it would have reported a single act; **the active participle reports that the word has *stuck to the one who says it*.** I give this as an observation about the word order.

### وَمِن وَرَآئِهِم بَرْزَخٌ إِلَىٰ يَوْمِ يُبْعَثُونَ

**And this sentence must be treated separately.**

#### بَرْزَخ — and a comparison with al-Furqān 25/53

**The word was worked out at `025-furkan.md` 25/53.** The description given there:

> **بَرْزَخ** — **the screen between two things, an intervening zone.** The lexicographers state that the word is **a separator that comes between two things and keeps them from mingling.**

**And it had occurred at `055-rahman.md` 55/20 as well (*baynahumā barzakhun lā yabghiyān*).** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The word occurs in three places in the Qurʾān, and the three must be compared:**

| Place | Sentence | Between what | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| `055-rahman.md` 55/20 | *baynahumā barzakhun lā yabghiyān* | **Two waters** — sweet and salt | **A barrier in space** |
| `025-furkan.md` 25/53 | *wa jaʿala baynahumā barzakhan wa ḥijran maḥjūrā* | **Two waters** | **A barrier in space** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/100** | *wa min warāʾihim barzakhun **ilā yawmi yubʿathūn*** | **Death and resurrection** | **A barrier in time** |

**The same word: in the first two a barrier in space, in the third a barrier in time.** This is a difference that can be verified from the three texts.

**And the wording that marks the difference must be recorded: *ilā yawmi yubʿathūn* — "until the day they are raised".** **In al-Furqān and al-Raḥmān the barrier had no *end date*; here it has one.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this difference of particle (*bayna* / *ilā*):** the barrier between the two waters stands **between two sides** and is without term; **the barrier here lasts up to a time.** **The word does the same work, that is — it prevents passage — but its measure changes.**

#### A methodological note — on life in the barzakh

**And a limit must be set here, and I record it plainly:**

**Under this verse I build no description of the character of life in the barzakh.** On the state of the grave, the condition of souls, what happens there, **I transmit no report and I do not speculate.**

**I stay within the wording of the verse, and the verse says three things:**

| # | What | The wording |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **There is a barrier** | *Barzakh* |
| 2 | **It is behind them** | *min warāʾihim* |
| 3 | **It lasts until a time** | *ilā yawmi yubʿathūn* |

**Beyond this the verse gives no information.** **The rules recorded in `STYLE.md` — "do not ascribe a saying you are not sure of" and "do not present your own inference as a transmitted report" — are applied here exactly.**

**The lexicographers enter a note on the construction *min warāʾihim*, and I pass it on:** *warāʾ* in Arabic can mean both **behind** and **in front** — so it can also be read "before them". **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### The request "send me back" — a comparison with three verses across this series

**The same request was treated in two other places in this series, and the two were tabulated at `032-secde.md` 32/12.** **The third is here, and I extend the table:**

| | `035-fatir.md` 35/37 | `032-secde.md` 32/12 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/99-100** |
|---|---|---|---|
| The verb | *akhrijnā* — **bring us out** | *irjiʿnā* — **send us back** | *irjiʿūn* — **send me back** (**plural**) |
| Where it is said | **Inside the Fire** — *yaṣṭarikhūna fīhā* | **In the presence** — *ʿinda rabbihim* | **At the moment of death** — *jāʾa aḥadahumu'l-mawt* |
| Who says it | **A group** — plural | **A group** — plural | **A single person** — *aḥadahum* |
| For what | *naʿmal ṣāliḥan **ghayra'lladhī kunnā naʿmal*** | *naʿmal ṣāliḥan **innā mūqinūn*** | *aʿmalu ṣāliḥan **fīmā taraktu*** |
| The answer given | *a-wa-lam nuʿammirkum…* — **a term had been given** | **No answer** — verse 13 says something else | ***kallā*** — **a direct refusal** |
| What follows | *fa-mā li'l-ẓālimīna min naṣīr* | *wa law shiʾnā la-ātaynā kulla nafsin hudāhā* | ***Barzakh*** — a barrier is reported |

**All three requests ask for the same act: *naʿmal ṣāliḥan* / *aʿmalu ṣāliḥan*.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the three texts.

**And the finding recorded in `032-secde.md` goes one step further here.** What was written there was this:

> **The request in Fāṭir puts forward the mending of *deeds*, the request in al-Sajda puts forward *knowledge*.** Both make a ground of what is claimed to have been lacking in this world — and both grounds are invalidated by the verse itself.

**I record al-Muʾminūn's difference as my own reading, its ground being the table above:**

**One. A difference of time.** The requests in Fāṭir and al-Sajda are spoken **in the hereafter.** **The request in al-Muʾminūn is at the moment of death** — that is, at the earliest point.

**Two. A difference of number.** The others are from the mouth of a group; **this one from the mouth of a single person** (*aḥadahum*).

**Three. The form of the answer.** Fāṭir gives a ground ("a term had been given"), al-Sajda shifts the answer elsewhere, **al-Muʾminūn refuses in a single word: *kallā*.**

**Four. And only al-Muʾminūn reports a *barrier*.** In the other two verses the reason why there will be no return was explained by a ground; **here a structure is set down: the barzakh.**

**And this request will return once more within the sūra, in the hundred and seventh verse:** *rabbanā akhrijnā minhā.* **In the same sūra, that is, the request is made twice** — once at the moment of death, once inside the Fire. **Both are refused.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be treated separately at the hundred and seventh verse.

---

## 23/101 — فَإِذَا نُفِخَ فِى ٱلصُّورِ فَلَآ أَنسَابَ بَيْنَهُمْ يَوْمَئِذٍ وَلَا يَتَسَآءَلُونَ

*fa-idhā nufikha fi'l-ṣūri fa-lā ansāba baynahum yawmaʾidhin wa lā yatasāʾalūn*

"**When the trumpet is blown, on that day there will be no ties of kinship between them, nor will they ask after one another.**"

### نُفِخَ فِى ٱلصُّور

The root **ن-ف-خ**: **to blow.** **صُور** — the horn that is blown, the trumpet.

**The word and the matter were treated at `079-naziat.md` 79/6-7, `069-hakka.md` 69/13 and `036-yasin.md` 36/51.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**And the passive of the verb must be recorded: *nufikha* — the one who blows is not named.** **The same point was recorded in `079-naziat.md`.**

### فَلَآ أَنسَابَ بَيْنَهُمْ — and a comparison with al-Furqān 25/54

The root **ن-س-ب**: **to attach a thing to an origin.** *Nasab* — a tie of descent; *nisba* — a relation.

**The word was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/54** (*fa-jaʿalahū nasaban wa ṣihrā*). What was recorded there:

> **Two ties must be recorded:** *nasab* — **kinship that comes by birth**; *ṣihr* — **kinship established by marriage.** … the preceding verse described **two waters that do not mingle**; this verse describes **two lines that join.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **And the two verses must be compared, because the same word is used in two opposite directions:**

| | `025-furkan.md` 25/54 | **al-Muʾminūn 23/101** |
|---|---|---|
| The sentence | *wa huwa'lladhī khalaqa mina'l-māʾi basharan fa-jaʿalahū **nasaban wa ṣihrā*** | *fa-lā **ansāba** baynahum yawmaʾidhin* |
| The verb | ***jaʿala*** — **He established** | ***lā*** — **there is none left** |
| How many ties | **Two** — *nasab* and *ṣihr* | **One** — only *ansāb* |
| Number | **Singular** — *nasaban* | **Plural** — *ansāba* |
| Time | **In this world** — the discussion of creation | ***yawmaʾidhin*** — on that day |

**The two verses must be read together, and I record this as my own reading, its ground being the use of the same word with two different verbs:** al-Furqān says that the human being created from water is woven with **ties of descent and of marriage** — the ties, that is, are a part of the creation. **al-Muʾminūn says that those same ties will be lifted on a day.**

**And it must be recorded: what is described is not the tie's *ceasing to exist* but its *ceasing to work*** — indeed the verse continues *wa lā yatasāʾalūn*. **The question, that is, is not the existence of the tie of kinship but the impossibility of asking anything by way of it.**

**And the *ṣihr* of al-Furqān is not named here.** I give this as an entry about the wording.

### وَلَا يَتَسَآءَلُونَ — and the disagreement with al-Ṣāffāt 37/27

The root **س-أ-ل**: **to ask, to request.** **The pattern is Form VI (*tasāʾala*): to ask one another.**

**And the same verb occurs in the scenes of the resurrection in the Qurʾān both affirmed and negated:**

| Place | Sentence | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| `037-saffat.md` 37/27 | *wa aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin **yatasāʾalūn*** | **They ask** — in the Fire |
| `037-saffat.md` 37/50 | *fa-aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin **yatasāʾalūn*** | **They ask** — in the garden |
| `052-tur.md` 52/25 | *wa aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin **yatasāʾalūn*** | **They ask** |
| `074-muddessir.md` 74/40 | *fī jannātin **yatasāʾalūn*** | **They ask** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/101** | *wa **lā** yatasāʾalūn* | **They do not ask** |

**This makes an apparent contradiction, and the exegetes dwell on it. The explanations given are these:**

| # | Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Different times** — at the moment the trumpet is blown they do not ask; afterwards they do | The verse sets the qualification *yawmaʾidhin* (on that day) |
| 2 | **Different contents** — there is no **asking** for something by way of a tie of kinship; asking after one another there is | The sense of the verb *tasāʾul* as "requesting" |
| 3 | **Different parties** — those who do not ask are some, those who ask are others | The difference of setting |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.** **And in `037-saffat.md` it was recorded that the same verb is repeated in two tables (the Fire and the garden);** I rely on that treatment.

### A link within the sūra

**And I record an observation:** in the hundred and eleventh verse the sūra will describe the relation between the one who mocked and the one mocked; **in the hundred and twelfth a question will be asked** (*kam labithtum*). **The state of "not asking" is therefore not absolute** — indeed the sūra itself sets up a scene of speech a few verses later.

**This is a datum within the sūra supporting the first explanation, and I record it as an observation; I do not impose a preference.**

---

## 23/102-103 — فَمَن ثَقُلَتْ مَوَٰزِينُهُۥ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُفْلِحُونَ · وَمَنْ خَفَّتْ مَوَٰزِينُهُۥ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ خَسِرُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُمْ فِى جَهَنَّمَ خَٰلِدُونَ

*fa-man thaqulat mawāzīnuhū fa-ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn · wa man khaffat mawāzīnuhū fa-ulāʾika'lladhīna khasirū anfusahum fī jahannama khālidūn*

"**Those whose scales are heavy — those are the ones who attain deliverance. And those whose scales are light — those are the ones who have lost their own selves; in Hell they remain.**"

### مَوَٰزِين — the word and the disagreement over it

The root **و-ز-ن**: **to weigh.** *Mīzān* — a balance; *wazn* — weight, weighing.

**The word was treated in detail at `101-karia.md` 101/6-9 (*fa-ammā man thaqulat mawāzīnuh · wa ammā man khaffat mawāzīnuh*)**, where it was recorded that it is joined with the *dharra* of al-Zilzāl. **I do not repeat it here.**

**And this verse is almost a word-for-word repetition of the two verses in al-Qāriʿa:**

| Place | Heavy | Light |
|---|---|---|
| `101-karia.md` 101/6-9 | *fa-ammā man **thaqulat mawāzīnuh** fa-huwa fī ʿīshatin rāḍiya* | *wa ammā man **khaffat mawāzīnuh** fa-ummuhū hāwiya* |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/102-103** | *fa-man **thaqulat mawāzīnuhū** fa-ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn* | *wa man **khaffat mawāzīnuhū** fa-ulāʾika'lladhīna khasirū anfusahum* |

**The shared wording is word for word; what differs is the concluding sentences.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the two texts.

**And the difference must be recorded:** al-Qāriʿa gives the outcome **as a place** (*ʿīshatin rāḍiya* / *hāwiya*); **al-Muʾminūn gives it as *a name*** (*al-mufliḥūn* / *khasirū anfusahum*).

### ٱلْمُفْلِحُون — the return to the sūra's first verse

**And here the clearest weave of the sūra is completed.**

**The root ف-ل-ح was treated at the first verse.** **The root's three occurrences in the sūra are these:**

| Verse | Sentence | Mood | Who |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *qad **aflaḥa**'l-muʾminūn* | **Perfect** — done | **The believers** |
| **102** | *fa-ulāʾika humu'l-**mufliḥūn*** | **Active participle** — an attribute | **Those whose scales are heavy** |
| **117** | *innahū **lā yufliḥu**'l-kāfirūn* | **Imperfect, negated** | **The disbelievers** |

**All three occurrences can be verified from the text.** **And I record this as my own reading, its ground being this threefold distribution:**

**The first verse gives the verdict** — the believers have attained. **The hundred and second shows how the verdict is to be understood** — at the scales. **The hundred and seventeenth sets down its negative.**

**The sūra, that is, attaches at its end the verdict it gave at its opening to a measure.** And the measure is not the attributes enumerated in the first eleven verses — **it is the scales.**

**And a distinction between *falāḥ* and *fawz* was recorded in `059-hasr.md`:**

> *Falāḥ* was used in the ninth verse for **those guarded against covetousness** — that is, an inner working in this world. *Fawz* comes here, in the verse that reports the outcome. **One names the process, the other the arrival.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **And both words are present in this sūra:** *falāḥ* (1, 102, 117) and *fawz* (111 — *annahum humu'l-fāʾizūn*). **The distinction drawn in `059-hasr.md`, that is, can be tested in this sūra too:** *falāḥ* stands at the two ends of the sūra and in the scene of the scales, *fawz* in the recompense of those who were patient.

### خَسِرُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُم — the return to the thirty-fourth verse

**The root خ-س-ر was treated at the thirty-fourth verse.** **There the other side had said:** *wa la-in aṭaʿtum basharan mithlakum innakum idhan la-**khāsirūn***.

| Verse | Who says it | Who will come to loss |
|---|---|---|
| **34** | **The objectors** | **Those who follow the messenger** |
| **103** | **The text** | **Those whose scales are light** |

**The same root, in two different mouths, with two opposite verdicts.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**And the object must be recorded:** *khasirū anfusahum* — what is lost is not property but **themselves.**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** in the thirty-fourth verse the object of the loss was not stated; **here it is stated.**

---

## 23/104-105 — تَلْفَحُ وُجُوهَهُمُ ٱلنَّارُ وَهُمْ فِيهَا كَٰلِحُونَ · أَلَمْ تَكُنْ ءَايَٰتِى تُتْلَىٰ عَلَيْكُمْ فَكُنتُم بِهَا تُكَذِّبُونَ

*talfaḥu wujūhahumu'l-nāru wa hum fīhā kāliḥūn · a-lam takun āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum fa-kuntum bihā tukadhdhibūn*

"**The Fire scorches their faces, and there they are drawn back in a grimace. 'Were My signs not recited to you? And you used to deny them.'**"

### تَلْفَحُ — root: ل-ف-ح

**The concrete sense of the root: the striking of a flame or a hot wind against the face.**

**And the lexicographers record a difference of degree between *lafḥ* and *nafḥ*:**

| Word | Root | Degree |
|---|---|---|
| **نَفْح** | ن-ف-ح | **A light breath, a slight touch** |
| **لَفْح** | ل-ف-ح | **A violent striking, a scorching** |

**I pass this distinction on as it is transmitted.**

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.

**And the object of the verb must be recorded: *wujūhahum* — the faces.** **The face is the limb that comes regularly to the fore in the Qurʾān's tables of punishment and favour** — it was treated at `080-abese.md` 80/38-41 (*wujūhun yawmaʾidhin musfira* / *ʿalayhā ghabara*) and at `088-gasiye.md` 88/2 and 88/8.

### كَٰلِحُون — root: ك-ل-ح

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: the drawing back of the lips so that the teeth are left bare.** *Kalaḥa* — his lips drew back and his teeth showed.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.

**And the picture in the word is worth recording:** what is described is not an expression of pain — **it is a state of the face.** The lips have drawn back and the teeth have become visible.

**I give this as an entry about the language and go no further.** **And the finding recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/12 holds here too:** in these scenes the Qurʾān uses **a detail that can be seen** rather than an abstract adjective.

### أَلَمْ تَكُنْ ءَايَٰتِى تُتْلَىٰ عَلَيْكُمْ — the return to the sixty-sixth verse

**This sentence was treated at the sixty-sixth verse and tabulated there.** **In brief:**

| Verse | Pattern | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **66** | *qad kānat āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* — **a statement** | **In this world** |
| **105** | *a-lam takun āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* — **a question** | **In the hereafter** |

**And the verbs that follow also differ, and this must be recorded:**

| Verse | The reaction |
|---|---|
| **66** | *fa-kuntum ʿalā aʿqābikum **tankiṣūn*** — **drawing back on the heels** |
| **105** | *fa-kuntum bihā **tukadhdhibūn*** — **denying** |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the difference between the two verbs:** the scene in this world described a **behaviour** (drawing back); **the scene in the hereafter names the same thing as a *verdict*** (denial).

**The sūra, that is, gives the behaviour seen from outside its name at the end.** This is a reading, and it is not binding.

---

## 23/106-107 — قَالُوا۟ رَبَّنَا غَلَبَتْ عَلَيْنَا شِقْوَتُنَا وَكُنَّا قَوْمًا ضَآلِّينَ · رَبَّنَآ أَخْرِجْنَا مِنْهَا فَإِنْ عُدْنَا فَإِنَّا ظَٰلِمُونَ

*qālū rabbanā ghalabat ʿalaynā shiqwatunā wa kunnā qawman ḍāllīn · rabbanā akhrijnā minhā fa-in ʿudnā fa-innā ẓālimūn*

"They said: '**Our Lord, our wretchedness overcame us, and we were a people gone astray. Our Lord, bring us out of it. If we go back, then we are indeed wrongdoers.**'"

### غَلَبَتْ عَلَيْنَا شِقْوَتُنَا — the form of the excuse

**The sentence is an excuse, and its build must be recorded:** *shiqwatunā* is **the subject**, *ʿalaynā* — **they are the object.**

**In the sentence, that is, the party doing the acting is not they but their own wretchedness.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this voice of the sentence:** the excuse **takes the responsibility off the person and gives it to a condition.**

**And the verse does not leave this to stand alone:** immediately after comes *wa kunnā qawman **ḍāllīn*** — **and this time the subject is they themselves.**

| Sentence | Subject | What |
|---|---|---|
| *ghalabat ʿalaynā shiqwatunā* | **Wretchedness** | **An excuse** — something from outside |
| *wa kunnā qawman ḍāllīn* | **We** | **An admission** — their own condition |

**Two sentences side by side, with different subjects.** This is a fact that can be verified from the text, and I give it as an observation about the word order: **in the same speech there is both an excuse and an admission.**

The root **ش-ق-و**: **hardship, distress, unhappiness.** The word is the opposite of *saʿāda* (happiness). **The root was treated at `087-ala.md` 87/11 (*wa yatajannabuhā'l-ashqā*) and at `092-leyl.md` 92/15 (*lā yaṣlāhā illā'l-ashqā*).**

**A variant reading must be recorded:** the readings ***shiqwatunā*** and ***shaqāwatunā*** are transmitted. **Two verbal-noun patterns from the same root; it does not change the meaning.**

**And the finding recorded in `092-leyl.md` holds here too:** there too the word *ashqā* was set down not as a **condition** but as **the outcome of deeds**. I rely on that treatment. **The assumption the excuse rests on — that wretchedness is a fate independent of the person — is thus not supported by the Qurʾān's own usage.** I give this reading as my own reading.

### رَبَّنَآ أَخْرِجْنَا مِنْهَا — the second request in the sūra

**And this is the second form within the sūra of the request in the ninety-ninth verse.**

| Verse | The request | Where | The answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **99** | *rabbi'rjiʿūn* — **send me back** | **At the moment of death** | *kallā* (100) — **refusal** |
| **107** | *rabbanā akhrijnā minhā* — **bring us out** | **Inside the Fire** | *ikhsaʾū fīhā* (108) — **refusal** |

**The same sūra, the same request, two different times.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**And the request at `035-fatir.md` 35/37 uses exactly the same word as this second one:** *rabbanā **akhrijnā** naʿmal ṣāliḥan.* **A four-row table was built at the ninety-ninth verse;** this verse is added to that table as a fifth row.

**And its difference must be recorded:** the request in Fāṭir offered a **ground**, saying *naʿmal ṣāliḥan* (that we may do righteous deeds). **The request here offers no ground** — in its place it gives an **undertaking**: *fa-in ʿudnā fa-innā ẓālimūn.*

### فَإِنْ عُدْنَا فَإِنَّا ظَٰلِمُونَ

**The word order must be recorded:** the sentence is a **condition-and-answer** structure and takes the form of an undertaking — "if we go back, we are wrongdoers."

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the mood of the sentence:** the undertaking involves their **own mouths** naming their former condition as wrongdoing. **There is a tension, that is, between the excuse (106) and the undertaking (107):** one lifts the responsibility, the other accepts it.

---

## 23/108 — قَالَ ٱخْسَـُٔوا۟ فِيهَا وَلَا تُكَلِّمُونِ

*qāla'khsaʾū fīhā wa lā tukallimūn*

"**He said: 'Slink away into it, and do not speak to Me!'**"

### ٱخْسَـُٔوا۟ — root: خ-س-أ

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: *khasaʾa'l-kalba* — to drive off a dog, to send it away.** And from this comes the sense of **being driven off in humiliation.** *Khāsiʾ* — driven off, cowering.

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: al-Baqara 2/65 (*kūnū qiradatan **khāsiʾīn***) and this verse. **And at `067-mulk.md` 67/4 the same root occurs in another pattern** (*yanqalib ilayka'l-baṣaru **khāsiʾan** wa huwa ḥasīr*) — where it was used for **the eye returning weary and without hope.**

**The word's three occurrences can be compared:**

| Place | Pattern | Who / what |
|---|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/65 | *khāsiʾīn* — active participle | **A community** |
| `067-mulk.md` 67/4 | *khāsiʾan* — active participle | **The eye** — the return of the gaze |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/108** | *ikhsaʾū* — **an imperative** | **Those addressed** |

**The same root: in two of them it reports a state, here a command.**

**And the harshness of the word must be recorded, and I give this as an entry about the language:** the word is one used in Arabic for driving off an animal. **The lexicographers record this, and I pass it on as it is transmitted.**

### وَلَا تُكَلِّمُونِ — the cutting off of speech

The root **ك-ل-م**: **to wound; and to speak.** The lexicographers record both senses together: *kalm* — a wound; *kalām* — speech. **The link is drawn by way of speech too leaving a mark; I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.**

**And the *nūn* at the end of the verb arises, as recorded at the ninety-eighth verse, from the dropping of the first-person *yāʾ*:** its original is *tukallimūnī.*

**And this completes a weave in the sūra's last block, and it must be recorded:**

| Verse | Sentence | What |
|---|---|---|
| **99** | *qāla rabbi'rjiʿūn* | **Speech begins** — a request |
| **107** | *rabbanā akhrijnā minhā* | **Speech continues** — a second request |
| **108** | *wa lā tukallimūn* | **Speech is cut off** |

**All three verses can be verified from the text.** **And I record this as my own reading:** the block carries through a speech that begins at the moment of death and closes it with **a prohibition of speech.** **What is refused, that is, is not only the request but the voicing of the request.**

**And the finding recorded at the seventy-seventh verse goes one step further here:** there, at the core of the word *mublisūn*, was **finding nothing to say.** **Here there are words to be found, but no one left to address.**

---

## 23/109-111 — إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ فَرِيقٌ مِّنْ عِبَادِى يَقُولُونَ رَبَّنَآ ءَامَنَّا فَٱغْفِرْ لَنَا وَٱرْحَمْنَا وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلرَّٰحِمِينَ · فَٱتَّخَذْتُمُوهُمْ سِخْرِيًّا حَتَّىٰٓ أَنسَوْكُمْ ذِكْرِى وَكُنتُم مِّنْهُمْ تَضْحَكُونَ · إِنِّى جَزَيْتُهُمُ ٱلْيَوْمَ بِمَا صَبَرُوٓا۟ أَنَّهُمْ هُمُ ٱلْفَآئِزُونَ

*innahū kāna farīqun min ʿibādī yaqūlūna rabbanā āmannā fa'ghfir lanā wa'rḥamnā wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn · fa'ttakhadhtumūhum sikhriyyan ḥattā ansawkum dhikrī wa kuntum minhum taḍḥakūn · innī jazaytuhumu'l-yawma bimā ṣabarū annahum humu'l-fāʾizūn*

"**There was a party of My servants who used to say, 'Our Lord, we have believed; forgive us and have mercy on us, and You are the best of those who show mercy.' And you took them for mockery — until it made you forget My remembrance; and you used to laugh at them. Today I have recompensed them for their patience: it is they who are the ones who have won.**"

### فَرِيقٌ مِّنْ عِبَادِى

The root **ف-ر-ق**: **to separate.** *Farīq* — a party set apart. **The root was treated at `025-furkan.md` 25/1 (*al-Furqān*) and at `044-duhan.md` 44/4.**

**And the construction must be recorded: *min ʿibādī* — "of My servants".** The possessive is to the first person.

**I give this as an observation:** in this sentence the group that was mocked is presented as a party **of the speaker's own servants.** **The target of the mockery, that is, is named with its owner claiming it.**

### The prayer — and the weave with the sūra's last verse

**And here the most striking weave of the sūra is built.**

**The speech of the group mocked in the hundred and ninth verse is this:**

> *rabbanā āmannā fa'ghfir lanā wa'rḥamnā **wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn***

**And the last verse of the sūra (118) is this:**

> *wa qul rabbi'ghfir wa'rḥam **wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn***

**The last three words are word for word the same.** And the verbs too are from the same roots: *ighfir* and *irḥam.*

| | **109** | **118** |
|---|---|---|
| Who says it | ***farīqun min ʿibādī*** — the group that is mocked | **The Prophet addressed** — by command |
| Mood | *yaqūlūna* — **a report** (they used to say) | *wa qul* — **a command** (say) |
| Preceded by | *āmannā* — the confession of faith | **Nothing** |
| Shared | *fa'ghfir lanā wa'rḥamnā wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | *ighfir wa'rḥam wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* |

**This can be verified from the wording of the two verses.** **And I record this as my own reading, its ground being this exact overlap:**

**The sūra takes the speech that was mocked in the hundred and ninth verse and makes it its own last sentence in the hundred and eighteenth.** **The prayer that was mocked, that is, becomes the sūra's closing command.**

### سِخْرِيًّا — the variant reading and the meaning

The root **س-خ-ر**: **to laugh at someone in contempt; and to bend someone to one's service.** Both senses come out of the root: *sakhira minhu* — he mocked him; *sakhkharahū* — he made him subservient (*taskhīr*).

**A variant reading must be recorded, and the lexicographers connect it with the meaning:**

| Reading | Its vowel | The meaning according to some lexicographers |
|---|---|---|
| ***sikhriyyan*** | **kasra** on the *sīn* | **Mockery** |
| ***sukhriyyan*** | **ḍamma** on the *sīn* | **Putting to service, subjugating** |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **And some of the lexicographers record that there is no difference of meaning between the two readings, and that both report mockery.** **I pass both views on.**

**And the setting of the verse must be recorded:** the continuation of the sentence is *wa kuntum minhum **taḍḥakūn*** (and you used to laugh at them) — **the act of laughing is named openly.** This is a datum within the sūra supporting the first sense, and I record it as an observation.

### حَتَّىٰٓ أَنسَوْكُمْ ذِكْرِى — the outcome of the mockery

**And this sentence is the core of the verse, and it must be recorded.**

**The word order: *ansawkum* — the subject of the verb is *hum* (they), its object *kum* (you).** **The party that makes them forget, that is, is the ones being mocked.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this voice, and it carries an oddity:** those who are mocked do nothing; **but the occupation of mocking keeps the mocker from something.** **The sentence ascribes that keeping-away to them.**

**The lexicographers explain this as "ascription to the cause"** — that is, what makes one forget is not the ones mocked but **being occupied with them.** **I pass the explanation on as it is transmitted.**

**And what is forgotten must be recorded: *dhikrī* — "the remembrance of Me".**

**And the word is from the same root as *dhikrihim* in the seventy-first verse:**

| Verse | Construction | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| **71** | *ʿan **dhikrihim** muʿriḍūn* | **Their** reminder — what is turned away from |
| **110** | *ḥattā ansawkum **dhikrī*** | **My** remembrance — what is forgotten |

**The same root, with two different possessives.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### إِنِّى جَزَيْتُهُمُ ٱلْيَوْمَ بِمَا صَبَرُوٓا۟

**And the ground of the recompense must be recorded: *bimā ṣabarū* — "for their patience".**

The root **ص-ب-ر**: **to hold a thing, to confine, to endure.** **The root was treated in `103-asr.md` and at `090-beled.md` 90/17.**

**And what the *ṣabr* here stands against must be recorded:** the verse speaks of no hunger, illness or war — **it speaks of patience under mockery.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the setting of the verse:** what calls for the recompense is the situation described in the hundred and tenth verse — **being laughed at.**

**And the same ground was recorded at `025-furkan.md` 25/75:** *ulāʾika yujzawna'l-ghurfata **bimā ṣabarū***. **The same construction, in two sūras.** **I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | Sentence | The recompense |
|---|---|---|
| `025-furkan.md` 25/75 | *yujzawna'l-ghurfata **bimā ṣabarū*** | **al-Ghurfa** — a place |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/111** | *jazaytuhumu'l-yawma **bimā ṣabarū*** | ***al-Fāʾizūn*** — a name |

**The same ground, two different recompenses.** **And the two sūras' lists of attributes closed in the same way** — one with a place, the other with a name. This overlap agrees with the comparison table at the opening of the sūra.

### ٱلْفَآئِزُون — root: ف-و-ز

**The concrete sense of the root: to escape and reach what is sought; and to perish** (the lexicographers record the opposite sense as well). *Fawz* — gain, deliverance; *mafāza* — a place of deliverance, and a desert.

**The root was treated at `059-hasr.md` 59/20 (*aṣḥābu'l-jannati humu'l-fāʾizūn*)**, where its distinction from *falāḥ* was drawn:

> *Falāḥ*… an inner working in this world. *Fawz*, by contrast… in the verse that reports the outcome. **One names the process, the other the arrival.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **And in this sūra the distinction is in a testable state, and it was recorded at the hundred and second verse:** *falāḥ* stands at the two ends of the sūra and in the scene of the scales (1, 102, 117); *fawz* only here (111) — **in the verse where the recompense is given.**

**And the word order must be recorded:** *annahum **hum**u'l-fāʾizūn* — reinforced with the pronoun of separation. **Not "they too are among those who have won", that is, but "those who have won are precisely they."**

---

## 23/112-114 — قَٰلَ كَمْ لَبِثْتُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ عَدَدَ سِنِينَ · قَالُوا۟ لَبِثْنَا يَوْمًا أَوْ بَعْضَ يَوْمٍ فَسْـَٔلِ ٱلْعَآدِّينَ · قَٰلَ إِن لَّبِثْتُمْ إِلَّا قَلِيلًا لَّوْ أَنَّكُمْ كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ

*qāla kam labithtum fi'l-arḍi ʿadada sinīn · qālū labithnā yawman aw baʿḍa yawmin fa's'ali'l-ʿāddīn · qāla in labithtum illā qalīlan law annakum kuntum taʿlamūn*

"**He said: 'How many years did you stay on the earth?' They said: 'We stayed a day, or part of a day; ask those who count.' He said: 'You stayed only a little — if only you had known.'**"

### The variant reading

**Two readings are transmitted for the verb at the head of the hundred and twelfth and hundred and fourteenth verses:**

| Reading | Pattern | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| ***qāla*** | **Perfect** — "he said" | **The third person** |
| ***qul*** | **Imperative** — "say" | **A command to the one addressed** |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **On the second reading the question is put into the Prophet's mouth.**

### كَمْ لَبِثْتُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ عَدَدَ سِنِينَ

The root **ل-ب-ث**: **to stay in a place, to tarry.** *Labth* — staying, delay.

**And the word order must be recorded:** the question is asked with *kam* (how many) and the measure is given openly: *ʿadada sinīn* — **"by the number of years."**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the presence of this qualification:** the question itself fixes the unit of measure. **And the answer given does not use that unit** — it says *yawman aw baʿḍa yawm*. **The question is asked in years, that is, and the answer given in days.**

### لَبِثْنَا يَوْمًا أَوْ بَعْضَ يَوْمٍ — and the other measures across this series

**This expression is a repeated pattern in the Qurʾān and has been treated in several places across this series.** **A table of comparison:**

| Place | Expression | The measure |
|---|---|---|
| `046-ahkaf.md` 46/35 | *ka-annahum yawma yarawna mā yūʿadūna lam yalbathū illā **sāʿatan min nahār*** | **An hour of a day** |
| `079-naziat.md` 79/46 | *ka-annahum yawma yarawnahā lam yalbathū illā **ʿashiyyatan aw ḍuḥāhā*** | **An evening or a forenoon** |
| `030-rum.md` 30/55 | *yuqsimu'l-mujrimūna mā labithū **ghayra sāʿa*** | **Less than an hour** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/113** | *labithnā **yawman aw baʿḍa yawm*** | **A day or part of one** |

**All four expressions do the same work.** **And this measure was treated at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/35, where it was recorded:**

> **The measure is worth recording: *sāʿatan min nahār* — a portion of the daytime.** Not of the night but of the day: **that is, of the time a person spends awake and aware.**

**I rely on that treatment.** **And the difference of the expression in al-Muʾminūn must be recorded:**

**One. The measure is larger** — al-Aḥqāf and al-Rūm an *hour*, al-Nāziʿāt an *evening*, **al-Muʾminūn a *day*.**

**Two. And it carries an uncertainty:** *yawman **aw** baʿḍa yawm* — "a day, or part of a day". **No definite figure can be given.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the particle *aw*:** the answer says less that it is giving a measure than that **it cannot measure.**

### فَسْـَٔلِ ٱلْعَآدِّين

The root **ع-د-د**: **to count.** *ʿAdad* — a number; *ʿādd* — one who counts.

**And the exegetes differ over whom the sentence means:**

| Reading | Who *al-ʿāddūn* are |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The angels** — those who record deeds and days |
| 2 | **Those who deal with numbers** — a general expression |
| 3 | **No one in particular** — a deflection meaning "ask whoever is counting" |

**I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the form of the answer is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being that the sentence refers the matter to someone else:** the answering party **does not trust its own answer.** *Fa's'ali'l-ʿāddīn* — "ask those who count." **The answer, that is, is both a guess and a withdrawal.**

### إِن لَّبِثْتُمْ إِلَّا قَلِيلًا لَّوْ أَنَّكُمْ كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ

**And the response must be recorded: the answer given is not corrected — it is confirmed.**

*In labithtum illā qalīlā* — "you stayed only a little."

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the build of the sentence:** the answer does not turn out wrong. **What is wrong is that this answer was not given *in this world*.** Indeed the continuation of the sentence says so: *law annakum kuntum **taʿlamūn*** — "if only you had known."

**And the apodosis of the particle *law* has been elided** — the lexicographers record this and supply it as something like "…you would not have acted so." **The sentence is left unfinished.**

**This is an observation about the word order:** the elision leaves the completing of the sentence to the reader.

---

## 23/115 — أَفَحَسِبْتُمْ أَنَّمَا خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ عَبَثًا وَأَنَّكُمْ إِلَيْنَا لَا تُرْجَعُونَ

*a-fa-ḥasibtum annamā khalaqnākum ʿabathan wa annakum ilaynā lā turjaʿūn*

"**Did you suppose that We created you in vain, and that you would not be returned to Us?**"

### عَبَثًا — root: ع-ب-ث

**The concrete sense of the root is given by the lexicographers as follows: to meddle with a thing aimlessly, to keep fiddling with it by hand.** *ʿAbitha bi-shayʾ* — he played idly with a thing, he fiddled with it. **And from this *ʿabath* — a work attached to no purpose.**

**The root is worked out here for the first time in this series.** The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/128 (*a-tabnūna bi-kulli rīʿin āyatan taʿbathūn*) and this verse.

### عَبَث and لَعِب — the distinction

**And this verse must be compared with another word treated across this series.**

**It was treated in detail at `044-duhan.md` 44/38 (*wa mā khalaqnā'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa wa mā baynahumā lāʿibīn*).** What was recorded there:

> **The sentence does not say "We did not create."** *Mā khalaqnā … lāʿibīn* — what is negated is **not the act but the state of the act.**

**And in `044-duhan.md` it was given as an entry about the wording that the same sentence occurs at al-Anbiyāʾ 21/16 as well; that verse was treated at `021-enbiya.md` 21/16-18.** **I rely on both.**

**The two words can be compared, and the lexicographers record a difference between them:**

| Word | Root | The difference the lexicographers give |
|---|---|---|
| **لَعِب** | ل-ع-ب | **Play** — it has a purpose (pleasure, amusement), but it is not serious |
| **عَبَث** | ع-ب-ث | **Idle doing** — **it has no purpose at all** |

**I pass this distinction on as it is transmitted.**

**And three verses can be set side by side:**

| Place | The state negated | The object | About whom |
|---|---|---|---|
| `044-duhan.md` 44/38 | ***lāʿibīn*** — as those playing | **The heavens and the earth** | **The Creator** — *mā khalaqnā* |
| `021-enbiya.md` **21/16** | ***lāʿibīn*** | **Sky, earth and what is between** | **The Creator** |
| **al-Muʾminūn 23/115** | ***ʿabathan*** — in vain | **You** — the human being | **The addressee's supposition** — *a-fa-ḥasibtum* |

**I record the difference among the three verses as my own reading, its ground being the table above:**

**One. A difference of object.** al-Dukhān and al-Anbiyāʾ name **the universe**; **al-Muʾminūn names the addressee directly** — *khalaqnā**kum***.

**Two. A difference of mood.** The others are **statements** (We did not create); **al-Muʾminūn is a question** (did you suppose).

**Three. A difference of word.** In the others *laʿib* (play), here *ʿabath* (idle doing). **And by the distinction the lexicographers record, *ʿabath* is the heavier:** in play there is an aim, in *ʿabath* there is none at all.

**Four — and this is worth recording:** al-Dukhān and al-Anbiyāʾ negate a state of **the Creator**; **al-Muʾminūn asks about the condition of *what is created*.** *Khalaqnākum **ʿabathan*** — "you, in vain". **The question, that is, is about the meaning of the human being's own existence.**

### أَفَحَسِبْتُمْ — the return to the fifty-fifth verse

**The root ح-س-ب occurs three times in the sūra, and this can be verified from the text:**

| Verse | Sentence | What is supposed |
|---|---|---|
| **55** | *a-yaḥsabūna annamā numidduhum bihī min mālin wa banīn…* | **That what is given is a favour** |
| **115** | *a-fa-ḥasibtum annamā khalaqnākum ʿabathā* | **That the creation is in vain** |
| **117** | *fa-innamā **ḥisābuhū** ʿinda rabbih* | **The reckoning** — the root in its noun form |

**And the two questions are built on the same pattern: *annamā* + a verb.** **The sūra, that is, questions two great suppositions in the same language:** one about what is given **within life**, the other about **life itself.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this shared pattern.**

### وَأَنَّكُمْ إِلَيْنَا لَا تُرْجَعُونَ — the return to the ninety-ninth verse

**The root ر-ج-ع occurred in the ninety-ninth verse: *rabbi'rjiʿūn* — "send me back."**

| Verse | Sentence | Who asks / what is said |
|---|---|---|
| **99** | *rabbi'**rjiʿūn*** | They ask to return **to the world** |
| **115** | *ilaynā lā **turjaʿūn*** | They suppose they will not be returned **to Us** |
| **60** | *annahum ilā rabbihim **rājiʿūn*** | **What the believers know** — that they are returning to their Lord |

**All three occurrences can be verified from the text.** **And I record this as my own reading, its ground being this threefold distribution:**

**In the sixtieth verse the return is a piece of knowledge that makes hearts tremble.** **In the hundred and fifteenth the denial of that same return is put as a supposition.** **And in the ninety-ninth the return is what is at last asked for and not given.**

**The same root, that is, in three different conditions in the sūra:** known, denied, requested.

**And the word order must be recorded:** *ilaynā* has been brought forward (*taqdīm*) — "to Us — that you would not be returned". **The emphasis is on *to whom* the return is.**

---

## 23/116 — فَتَعَٰلَى ٱللَّهُ ٱلْمَلِكُ ٱلْحَقُّ لَآ إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا هُوَ رَبُّ ٱلْعَرْشِ ٱلْكَرِيمِ

*fa-taʿāla'llāhu'l-maliku'l-ḥaqq, lā ilāha illā hū, rabbu'l-ʿarshi'l-karīm*

"**Exalted is God, the true Sovereign. There is no god but He, the Lord of the noble throne.**"

### فَتَعَٰلَى — the root's last occurrence

**The root ع-ل-و was treated at the forty-sixth verse, and its three occurrences were tabulated there** (46, 91, 116). **This is the third and the last of them.**

| Verse | Word | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **46** | *qawman ʿālīn* | **Pharaoh's company** — human beings |
| **91** | *la-ʿalā baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍ* | **The supposed gods** |
| **116** | ***fa-taʿāla**'llāh* | **God** |

**And the word order must be recorded: the sentence begins with *fa*** — that is, as the consequence drawn from the preceding verse.

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this particle:** the hundred and fifteenth verse had asked whether the creation was in vain. **The hundred and sixteenth gives the answer with a name:** *al-Maliku'l-Ḥaqq* — **"the true Sovereign."**

### ٱلْمَلِكُ ٱلْحَقّ

**The root م-ل-ك was treated at the eighty-eighth verse (*malakūt*).**

The root **ح-ق-ق**: **a thing's being fixed and in its place.** *Ḥaqq* — what is fixed, what is real; and **a right, a due.**

**And the construction must be recorded: *al-Maliku'l-Ḥaqq* — an adjective, "the true Sovereign".**

**I give this as an observation:** the adjective reports not that the sovereignty **exists** but that it is **true.** **And several claims of false sovereignty were named across the sūra** — Pharaoh's company (46), the supposed gods (91), those who made others serve them (47).

**And the word *al-Ḥaqq* occurred several times in the sūra:** 41 (*bi'l-ḥaqq*), 62 (*bi'l-ḥaqq*), 70 (*bi'l-ḥaqq* and *li'l-ḥaqq*), 71 (*al-ḥaqq*), 90 (*bi'l-ḥaqq*), 116 (*al-Ḥaqq*). **And in the hundred and sixteenth verse the word comes for the first time as *an attribute of a name*.** This is a distribution that can be verified from the text.

### رَبُّ ٱلْعَرْشِ ٱلْكَرِيم — the return to the eighty-sixth verse

The root **ع-ر-ش**: **to raise, to roof a thing over.** *ʿArsh* — a throne, a raised canopy; *ʿarīsh* — a trellis for vines.

**And the construction had occurred in the eighty-sixth verse with a different adjective:**

| Verse | Construction | Adjective |
|---|---|---|
| **86** | *wa rabbu'l-ʿarshi'**l-ʿaẓīm*** | ***ʿAẓīm*** — mighty |
| **116** | *rabbu'l-ʿarshi'**l-karīm*** | ***Karīm*** — noble, precious |

**Two adjectives, in the same construction, thirty verses apart.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

The root **ك-ر-م**: **to be precious, to be generous.** **The root was treated at `096-alak.md` 96/3 (*wa rabbuka'l-akram*) and at `044-duhan.md` 44/49.**

**And the difference between the two adjectives is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** *ʿaẓīm* looks to **measure** — magnitude. *Karīm* looks to **worth** — nobility.

**And the settings of the two verses fit this difference:** the eighty-sixth verse was a **question** (who is the Lord of these) — there magnitude is named. **The hundred and sixteenth is a *conclusion*** — there worth is named.

**This is a reading, and it is not binding.**

---

## 23/117 — وَمَن يَدْعُ مَعَ ٱللَّهِ إِلَٰهًا ءَاخَرَ لَا بُرْهَٰنَ لَهُۥ بِهِۦ فَإِنَّمَا حِسَابُهُۥ عِندَ رَبِّهِۦٓ إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُفْلِحُ ٱلْكَٰفِرُونَ

*wa man yadʿu maʿa'llāhi ilāhan ākhara lā burhāna lahū bihī fa-innamā ḥisābuhū ʿinda rabbih, innahū lā yufliḥu'l-kāfirūn*

"**Whoever calls upon another god along with God, for which he has no proof whatever — his reckoning is with his Lord alone. The disbelievers do not attain deliverance.**"

### لَا بُرْهَٰنَ لَهُۥ بِهِۦ — the kind of adjective

**بُرْهَٰن** — **a decisive, irresistible proof.** The lexicographers differ over the origin of the word: it is said to derive from the root **ب-ر-ه** (whiteness, clarity); **the view that it is a word taken into Arabic is also transmitted.** **I pass the disagreement on; I do not impose a preference.**

**The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān:** al-Baqara 2/111, al-Nisāʾ 4/174, al-Qaṣaṣ 28/32 and 28/75, al-Naml 27/64.

**And the parsing of the sentence must be recorded: *lā burhāna lahū bihī* is an adjective to *ilāhan ākhar*.** The lexicographers count this adjective a ***ṣifa kāshifa*** — that is, an adjective brought **not to distinguish but to disclose the state of the case.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this parsing:** the sentence does not distinguish between "a god for which there is no proof" and "a god for which there is a proof". **The adjective is set down to show that there is no such distinction.** **And the matter had already been settled in the ninety-first verse of the sūra:** *wa mā kāna maʿahū min ilāh.*

### فَإِنَّمَا حِسَابُهُۥ عِندَ رَبِّهِ

**The root ح-س-ب was treated at the hundred and fifteenth verse, and its three occurrences in the sūra were tabulated there** (55, 115, 117). **This is the third, and it is the root's *noun* form.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sūra had twice asked "did you suppose?" (*a-yaḥsabūna*, *a-fa-ḥasibtum*); **here the same root turns into a word of outcome: *ḥisāb*.** **Supposing and reckoning, that is, come from the same root.**

**This is a datum of the language, and the lexicographers record under the root the core "to count, to reckon"** — *ḥasiba* (he counted) and *ḥasiba* (he supposed) are two branches of the same root. **I build no forced link between them;** I only record the distribution within the sūra.

**And *innamā* must be recorded: a particle of restriction** — "only, none but". **The place of the reckoning is being limited.**

**This is a link in the limit of responsibility tabulated at `025-furkan.md` 25/43 and `028-kasas.md` 28/56:** the party that judges is separated from the party that delivers the message. **And the same limit was drawn in this sūra at the fifty-fourth verse** (*fa-dharhum fī ghamratihim*).

### إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُفْلِحُ ٱلْكَٰفِرُونَ — the return to the sūra's first verse

**And here the clearest frame of the sūra closes.**

| Verse | Sentence | Mood | Subject |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *qad **aflaḥa**'l-**muʾminūn*** | **Perfect — affirmative** | **The believers** |
| **117** | *innahū **lā yufliḥu**'l-**kāfirūn*** | **Imperfect — negative** | **The disbelievers** |

**The sūra opens with the positive of a single root and closes with its negative.** This is a fact that can be verified from the text.

**And four differences must be recorded:**

**One. Mood.** The first is perfect (done and finished), the second imperfect (ongoing). **The deliverance is complete, that is, and the failure to attain it continues.**

**Two. Subject.** *Muʾminūn* and *kāfirūn* — both active participles, both plural, both definite (with the article).

**Three. Place.** The first is the sūra's **first sentence**, the second its **last verdict** (118 is a prayer).

**Four. And there is a third occurrence in between:** the hundred and second verse (*humu'l-mufliḥūn*) — **the place where the measure is set.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this threefold distribution:** the sūra gives the verdict at the start, sets the measure in the middle, and states the negative at the end. **And the root sense worked out in `002-bakara.md` fits this frame:** *falāḥ* is **to break through an obstacle and come out.** **The list at the sūra's opening describes how that breaking through is to happen.** This is a reading, and it is not binding.

---

## 23/118 — وَقُل رَّبِّ ٱغْفِرْ وَٱرْحَمْ وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلرَّٰحِمِينَ

*wa qul rabbi'ghfir wa'rḥam wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn*

"**Say: 'My Lord, forgive and have mercy; and You are the best of those who show mercy.'**"

### Two imperatives without an object

**The word order must be recorded, and this is the most striking place in the verse:** both verbs come **without an object** — *ighfir* and *irḥam.* **It does not say "forgive me" or "have mercy on us".**

**And in the hundred and ninth verse the same prayer had objects:** *fa'ghfir **lanā** wa'rḥam**nā***.

| Verse | The verbs | Object |
|---|---|---|
| **109** | *ighfir lanā wa'rḥamnā* | ***lanā / -nā*** — "us" |
| **118** | *ighfir wa'rḥam* | **None** |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the wording of the two verses.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being this elision:** in Arabic the dropping of the object reports **generality**; the lexicographers record this consistently. **The prayer, that is, is not closed off to one person or one group.**

**And the verse-ending must be recorded:** the dropping of the objects also fits **the sound scheme of the sūra** — throughout the sūra the verse endings close with *-ūn* / *-īn*, and this verse closes so as well.

**I pass both grounds on; they do not exclude one another.**

### وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلرَّٰحِمِين — the fifth superlative construction

**It is the last of the five superlative constructions in the sūra** (14, 29, 72, 109, 118). **And the table of the five:**

| Verse | Construction | Who says it | Setting |
|---|---|---|---|
| **14** | *aḥsanu'l-khāliqīn* | **The text** | **The forming** |
| **29** | *khayru'l-munzilīn* | **Noah** — inside a prayer | **The voyage** |
| **72** | *khayru'l-rāziqīn* | **The text** | **The question of payment** |
| **109** | *khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | **The group that is mocked** — inside a prayer | **The scene of mockery** |
| **118** | *khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | **The one addressed** — by command | **The closing** |

**All five of the five constructions are built on the agent of a verb:** creating, sending down, providing, showing mercy (twice).

**And three of them occur inside a prayer** (29, 109, 118). **This is a distribution that can be verified from the text.**

### The closing of the sūra

**And a structural entry:** the sūra had opened with a **verdict** (*qad aflaḥa'l-muʾminūn*) and closes with a **request.**

**The same structure was recorded in `002-bakara.md`:** *"the sūra, after 286 verses of legal ruling, history and dispute, ends with a request."* **And at `025-furkan.md` 25/77 too the sūra closed with the word *duʿāʾ*.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the shared closing of the three sūras:** all three, after ruling, history and dispute, are attached to **a request.** **And in al-Muʾminūn that request is the very speech that was made an object of mockery within the sūra itself.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

### The backbone of the sūra

**The sūra opens with a verdict and closes with the negative of that same verdict**, and in between it does four separate things:

| Work | Where | How |
|---|---|---|
| **Description** | 1-11 | **A list of attributes** — who it is that attains deliverance |
| **Proof** | 12-22, 78-92 | **The forming, water, plant, animal; the organs, three questions** |
| **Example** | 23-50 | **The sequence of messengers** — the repetition of the objection |
| **Outcome** | 93-118 | **Death, the barzakh, the trumpet, the scales, the reckoning** |

**And all four are attached to one another:** the attributes enumerated in the first list are attached to a set of scales in the hundred and second verse; the objections in the sequence of messengers to the addressee's own sentence in the eighty-second; the organs in the section of proofs to the drawing back in the sixty-sixth.

### The weave of words and roots

| Word / root | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **ف-ل-ح** | 1 — *aflaḥa* · 102 — *al-mufliḥūn* · 117 — *lā yufliḥu* | **The frame of the sūra** — verdict, measure, negative |
| **ن-ش-أ** | 14, 19, 31, 42, 78 | **Five occurrences** — the human being, the garden, a generation, generations, the organs |
| *basharun mithlukum* | 24, 33, 47 | **Three peoples, one objection** |
| *rabbi'nṣurnī bimā kadhdhabūn* | 26, 39 | **Word for word the same prayer** |
| *bihī jinna* | 25, 70 | **Noah and the Prophet addressed** |
| *ḥattā ḥīn* | 25, 54 | **The same span in two mouths** |
| *ābāʾ + al-awwalīn* | 24, 68 | **A ground / a question** |
| **غ-م-ر** | 54, 63 | **They / their hearts** |
| **ت-ر-ف** | 33, 64 | **The giving / the taking** |
| **س-ر-ع** | 56, 61 | **Delusion / attribute** — *fi'l-khayrāt* |
| **ح-س-ب** | 55, 115, 117 | **Two suppositions, one reckoning** |
| *āyātī tutlā ʿalaykum* | 66, 105 | **This world / the hereafter** |
| **أعرض** | 3, 71 | **Attribute / fault** — according to its object |
| **ع-ل-و** | 46, 91, 116 | **Human beings, supposed gods, God** |
| **خ-س-ر** | 34, 103 | **The word of the objection, the verdict of the sūra** |
| *rabbu'l-ʿarsh* | 86 (*al-ʿaẓīm*), 116 (*al-karīm*) | **Two attributes** |
| *khayru / aḥsanu …* | 14, 29, 72, 109, 118 | **Five superlative constructions** |
| *wa anta khayru'l-rāḥimīn* | 109, 118 | **The mocked prayer, the closing command** |
| **ر-ج-ع** | 60, 99, 115 | **The return: known, requested, denied** |
| **ق-ر-ر** | 13 (*qarārin makīn*), 50 (*dhāti qarārin*) | **Two places of shelter** |
| *ḥattā idhā* | 64, 77, 99 | **Three turning points** |

### The two lists of attributes — a summary

| | **23/1-11** | **23/57-61** |
|---|---|---|
| Items | **Six** | **Four** |
| Kind | **Acts** — prayer, speech, property, body, covenant | **States** — trembling, faith, tawḥīd, a trembling heart |
| Frame | **It opens with the prayer and closes with the prayer** | **The word *rabb* is in all four items** |
| Closing | *al-Firdaws* — **a place** | *yusāriʿūn / sābiqūn* — **a movement** |

**And these two lists were compared in detail at the beginning of this file with the *ʿibādu'l-raḥmān* list at `025-furkan.md` 25/63-77.** I do not repeat the table there; its summary is this: **four shared fields** (prayer, idle speech, property, chastity), **one item peculiar to al-Muʾminūn** (trust and covenant), **seven items peculiar to al-Furqān**, and **both lists ending with a dwelling.**

### Disagreements on which no preference is adopted in this commentary

**I record this per the method (`STYLE.md`): in the places below two or more readings were transmitted, and in none of them was a preference imposed.**

| Verse | The disagreement |
|---|---|
| **4** | The scope of the word *zakāt* — institutional *zakāt*, or the act of purification |
| **5-6** | The change of particle between *li-furūjihim* and *illā ʿalā azwājihim* |
| **8** | *li-amānātihim* / *li-amānatihim*; and *ʿahd* coming in the singular |
| **11** | The origin of the word *firdaws* |
| **14** | The parsing of *khalqan ākhar*; what "another creation" is; the construction *aḥsanu'l-khāliqīn* |
| **17** | What *ṭarāʾiq* is — the seven heavens, layers, paths |
| **20** | *tanbutu* / *tunbitu*; the work of the *bāʾ*; *saynāʾ* / *sīnāʾ*; the name of the tree |
| **27** | What the *tannūr* is; *min kullin* / *min kulli* |
| **29** | *munzalan* / *manzilan* |
| **31** | Who the people are — ʿĀd, Thamūd, indefinite |
| **44** | *tatrā* / *tatran* |
| **45** | The relation between *āyāt* and *sulṭān* |
| **49** | The referent of the pronoun in *laʿallahum yahtadūn* |
| **50** | *āyatan* coming in the singular; the root of *maʿīn*; the vowelling of *rabwa*; where the place is |
| **51** | Whom the address *yā ayyuha'l-rusul* is directed to |
| **52** | *wa inna* / *wa anna* / *wa in* |
| **53** | What *zubur* is — books, pieces, groups; *zuburan* / *zubara*; its parsing |
| **55-56** | Whether *annamā* is joined or separate |
| **60** | *yuʾtūna mā ātaw* / *yaʾtūna mā ataw*; the parsing of *annahum* |
| **61** | The work of *lahā* |
| **63** | The referent of the pronoun *hādhā*; the sense of *min dūni dhālika* |
| **67** | The referent of the pronoun *bihī*; *tahjurūn* / *tuhjirūn* |
| **71** | What *al-Ḥaqq* is — God, the revelation, the real, the order; the sense of *dhikruhum* |
| **72** | *kharjan fa-kharāju* / *kharājan fa-kharāju* / *kharjan fa-kharju* |
| **76** | The root of the verb *istakāna* — س-ك-ن or ك-و-ن |
| **77** | The origin of the name *Iblīs* |
| **85, 87, 89** | *sa-yaqūlūna li'llāh* / *sa-yaqūlūna Allāh* |
| **92** | The parsing of *ʿālimi* — genitive or nominative |
| **99** | *irjiʿūn* coming in the plural |
| **100** | The sense of *innahā kalimatun huwa qāʾiluhā*; the scope of *fīmā taraktu*; the direction of *min warāʾihim* |
| **101** | The relation between *lā yatasāʾalūn* and *yatasāʾalūn* at al-Ṣāffāt 37/27 |
| **106** | *shiqwatunā* / *shaqāwatunā* |
| **110** | *sikhriyyan* / *sukhriyyan* |
| **112, 114** | *qāla* / *qul*; who *al-ʿāddūn* are |
| **117** | The origin of the word *burhān* |

### The methodological limits drawn in this file

**I record them plainly:**

- **In verses 12-16 the staged forming was not equated with the terms of modern embryology.** The limit drawn at `040-gafir.md` 40/67, `039-zumer.md` 39/6, `032-secde.md` 32/7-9 and in `096-alak.md` was kept unchanged and its grounds were not repeated.
- **No matching with any modern classification was made on the basis of *sabʿa ṭarāʾiq* in verse 17.** The entry made in `086-tarik.md` for astronomical claims was counted valid here too.
- **No claim of "foretelling" about the hydrological cycle or underground water was built on *fa-askannāhu fi'l-arḍ* in verse 18.** The entry at `077-murselat.md` 77/20-23 was kept.
- **For the *barzakh* in verse 100, no description whatever beyond the wording of the verse was made.** On the state of the grave, the condition of souls and the like, no report was transmitted and no speculation was entered into.
- **The place of the *tannūr* in verse 27, the place of the *rabwa* in verse 50, the name of the people in verse 31** — in all three no detail the Qurʾān does not give was added; the place names and people's names that are transmitted were not passed on, since I am not certain of their sources.
- **For the construction *mā malakat aymānuhum* in verses 5-7 no legal ruling was issued;** only two entries from the text were made.
- **No ruling was issued in verse 4 about the threshold, the rate and the categories of *zakāt*.**
- **No theological dispute about destiny was opened on the basis of *ghalabat ʿalaynā shiqwatunā* in verse 106.**
- **The comparisons made with al-Anbiyāʾ in verses 52, 71 and 115 were rested on `021-enbiya.md`; the analyses made in that file were not repeated.**

### Points in this file of which I could not be certain

**I record them plainly:**

- **What "another creation" in verse 14 is.** The verse gives no name; four explanations were tabulated and none can be proved from the text.
- **The identity of the people in verse 31.** Three views were given; since the sūra gives no name, no preference was adopted.
- **The sense of the word *zubur* in verse 53.** The variant reading is directly bound up with the disagreement; the reading made by way of the core sense of the root is mine and is not binding.
- **The relation between *lā yatasāʾalūn* in verse 101 and *yatasāʾalūn* in al-Ṣāffāt.** Three explanations were given; the datum within the sūra (the scene of speech at 112-113) supports the first explanation but does not prove it.
- **The root of the verb *istakāna* in verse 76.** Since the disagreement is unresolved, no conclusion was drawn from the repetition of the root س-ك-ن within the sūra.

### My own inferences

**The following sections are my reading, not transmitted material, and they are not binding:**

- That the first list **opens with the prayer and closes with the prayer**, and that the four items between are to be read as **speech, property, body, relationship**.
- The interpretation of the four differences between 23/2 and 23/9 (singular-plural, *fī*-*ʿalā*, noun-verb, Form I-Form III) as a distinction between **a single prayer and a lifetime of prayer**.
- **The six observations drawn from the comparison table of the two lists of attributes.**
- The separation of the al-Muʾminūn list from the al-Furqān list as **one a description, the other an answer**.
- The reading of the **three occurrences of *thumma* in verses 12-14 as three thresholds**; the finding that the verb *kasawnā* reports **covering, not transformation**; and the reading concerning the fact that the threshold set by *anshaʾnā* is **left unnamed**.
- **The meaning of the change of pronoun (*iltifāt*) in verse 15**.
- The conclusion drawn from the **stopping / making-travel** contrast between *askannā* here and *salaka* in al-Zumar at verse 18.
- The reading of block II as a whole built on the **passive voice** of *tuḥmalūn* in verse 22.
- The reading of the command *kulū* in verse 51 as **the answer** to the objection in verse 33; and its comparison with the method of al-Furqān.
- The bringing forward of the **permanence** side of *zubur* in verse 53.
- The interpretation of the *yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt* weave between verses 56 and 61 as **delusion / attribute**.
- The reading of the limit of burden in verse 62 as **the measure of the race** in verse 61.
- Counting the **plural** form of *ahwāʾ* in verse 71 decisive for the working of the proof.
- The reading concerning verse 78 being placed **immediately after the cutting off of hope** in verse 77.
- The interpretation built on the **passive voice** of *tusḥarūn* in verse 89.
- The difference from Fuṣṣilat 41/34 in verse 96: **the absence of any promise of an outcome.**
- The separation of *barzakh* in verse 100 from its use in al-Furqān **by a difference of particle** (*bayna* / *ilā*).
- The separation of the request in verses 99-100 from those in Fāṭir and al-Sajda **in four respects**.
- The reading of the weave between verses 109 and 118 as **"the mocked prayer, the closing command"**.
- The interpretation of the difference between *ʿabath* and *laʿib* in verse 115 together with the differences of **object and mood**.
- The **measure / worth** distinction between *ʿaẓīm* and *karīm* in verse 116.
- The reading of the threefold distribution of *falāḥ* in verse 117 as **verdict, measure, negative**.
- The whole of the sections "The structure of the sūra", "Repetitions within the sūra" and "The sūra as a whole".
